# 08. Game flow: the front end and the play modes

Scope: everything the original shows and does outside combat. Part A (sections 1 to 6, this file's first half) is the front end: the text records, the console path and font, the palette and fades, the screen state machine from boot to flight and back, and mission start and end. Part B (sections 7 to 11) is the play modes: demo replay, training, qualification, the flight log and the hidden keys. The two parts are written by different authors; each edits only its own sections and its own sub-headings under Constants, Open questions and Verified by.

Sections 2 to 11 together are this note's "Format or Behaviour" part (`docs/re/README.md`); "Location" is section 1 (and section 7 starts with the play modes' own locations).

Tags used for rules: **V** = verified on the reference emulator (a capture, a per-blank log) or in the Musashi rig, with the count stated; **R** = read from the code and not run; **C** = a port choice or convention, labelled as such. Game text is never quoted; screens are identified by role.

## 1. Location

Addresses are flat image addresses (`00-binary-layout.md`); `V:xxxxx` is a variable in the zero-filled hunks 71 to 73 (address `0x4xxxx`).

**The correction that shapes this note.** The plan and note 02 (section 12, open question 7) assumed that the front end draws through the Amiga console device in the Kickstart ROM's Topaz font. That is wrong [V]. The two `_Write` call sites named there (`0x184A8`, `0x1861C`) write the 78-byte player record to the flight-log file, not text to a console; no graphics `Text`, Intuition or console call exists in the image (the stub region of note 00 lists none); and every text screen of the original (credits, log-disk prompt, main menu, mission briefings, game over, pause) is drawn by one text engine inside the game, in the game's own 5 by 7 pixel font stored in hunk 61, through the same bit planes as the 3D view. The menu, credits and log-disk captures of note 01a are reproduced **pixel for pixel** (64,000 pixels each, 0 mismatches, see Verified by) by that engine, that font and the records of hunk 64, with no ROM font involved. The consequence for the port: nothing about a Kickstart ROM, Topaz, a 40 by 25 character grid or console control sequences applies; section 3 states what replaces them.

## 2. Text records

Everything the original shows as text, outside the message window's flight messages (note 06, which uses the separate table of hunk 59), is a **text record** in hunk 64, played by the engine of section 3. This section settles the data; the extractor (Task 3b) and the core (Task 4) read it from here. Rule tags: **V** = verified, **R** = read from the code.

### 2.1 The id table and the id list

- **Ids.** A text id is a word from 1 to 110 (0x6E). The table at `0x43DFE` holds 110 words; the word of id n (at `0x43DFE + 2(n - 1)`) is the byte offset, from `0x43DFE`, of the id's record [V: 106 of 110 ids decode to a record and end inside the span, `text_check.py`]. Several ids share one record (95 offsets are distinct among the 110 ids; 91 distinct records remain once the four ids below are removed). **Four ids are not records** and no state uses them: id 0x15 (21; its offset is 0, the table itself), id 0x19 (25; its offset is the end byte of the record before it), and ids 0x12 and 0x13 (18 and 19) (their offsets fall inside the four-byte `0xFF` run before the record of id 20, so decoding them reads bytes of that record as a header). Ids 16 and 17 name two empty records (header bytes `0xFF 0xFF`, then the end byte); id 22 names an empty record with the header 6, 2 [V, decoded].
- **The id list.** A screen is a list of ids in the word array `V:4A802`, ended by a zero word. A state fills the list and the engine plays the ids in order (section 3); an id is played to its end (the last line and its delay) before the next id starts. The ids with bit 15 set (0x8000 or the id) play the same record but ignore its position: the record starts **two line steps below the last line of the previous record** (one line step is the next line's place; the blank line is the second step) [V: in the rig on the lists [98, 0x8000+99] and [0x40, 0x4D, 0x4E, 0x8000+110]]. Bit 14 (0x4000, always with bit 15 in the lists found) overrides the character delay of the record's first line with 2 passes (R: no state sets it; the lists seen carry bit 15 only).
- **Which list a state builds.** The states set the ids as plain constants (the writers of the groups of 2.4, found by scanning the state routines of `0x11700`-`0x14000` for immediate stores into the list [R]) or copy them from tables: the F2 to F6 selection lines come from the five words at `0x43DF4` (ids 0x4D to 0x51, copied one per list slot for the missions the player record has unlocked: the builder `0x122CE` loops over the completion bytes at `V:1D024` + 0x12 + m for m = 3 to 8 and copies the next word when byte m is non-zero, i.e. the line of the mission with mode M = m + 1 (F2 to F6, modes 4 to 8) appears when the byte at `V:1D024` + 0x11 + M is non-zero; the same rule as 5.4 and 10.2, written two ways); the briefing and outcome screens of the six missions come from the list table of section 1 through `0x29468(mode, slot, index)`, which stores word `index` of mission `mode`'s list into list slot `slot` [R].
- **The mission id lists** (index 0 to 4 of the list of mission modes 3 to 8, that is missions F1 to F6): index 0 and 1 the two briefing screens, index 2 always id 0x5F (the shared three-line notice), index 3 the orders screen, index 4 the success outcome. Per mission the five ids are a, a + 1, 0x5F, a + 3, a + 4 for a base id a that rises from mission to mission (the six bases are disk data and are not tabulated; single ids appear where a capture or a rule names them: `tables/briefing_chain.bin`, 6 * 5 bytes, FNV-1a-32 `f0c6d533`) [V: read from the image]. The failure and special outcomes are written directly by the debrief state `0x131F4` (section 5.13). Nine further ids are filler ids of the blocks (they name the shared notice or a blank record; the table of the ids that no path posts has them) and are never written [R].

### 2.2 The record format

A record is: **row** (one byte), **column** (one byte), then **lines**, ended by a flags byte with bit 7 set.

| field | size | rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| row | 1 | the scanline of the first glyph row of the first line [V: menu, credits and log-disk captures]; line k draws scanlines row + step x k to row + step x k + 6, and every line of the data ends at or before scanline 199 (the engine does not clip: a glyph row at scanline 200 or beyond would be written past the plane, 3.4 rule 6) |
| column | 1 | the byte column of the left edge of the first character: x = 8 x column pixels; every value in the data is even (the engine reads and writes a long word at the position, so an odd value would fault) |
| line flags | 1 | bit 7 set: end of record (the byte is then the whole line: no colour, no text); bit 0: 1 = typed-out line, 0 = instant line; bit 1: in a typed line, spaces are skipped without delay (section 3); the values in the data are 0, 1 and 3 |
| line colour | 1 | high nibble: the pen (0 to 15, an entry of the 32-colour palette, section 4); low nibble: the delay D in passes between two characters of a typed line (2 to 8 in the data; ignored by instant lines) |
| text | n | n characters from 0x20 to 0x5F (upper case, digits, punctuation; the data holds no byte outside that range), then one 0 byte; n may be 0 (a blank line: it still advances the position by one line step) |
| delay | 1 | after the zero: passes to wait before the next line, in units of 4 passes (0 gives one pass); values 0 to 120 in the data |

The line after the delay byte starts with its own flags byte, so flags, colour and delay differ line by line. Lines are placed one **line step** below each other: 440 bytes of plane (11 scanlines) in the engine's reset state, 480 (12 scanlines) while the main menu list plays (set by the menu state `0x11D30`, section 5.4). [V]

Quirks that a decoder must follow because the engine does:

1. A record ends at the first flags byte with bit 7 set, whatever its value. The record of id 0x6C (menu item 8) has no `0xFF` of its own: the next record's row byte (165) is the end flag, so the end address of that record is the first byte of the next one (an overlap of one byte) [V, decoded].
2. The three records `0x43F24`, `0x45FB3`, `0x45FB4` have no lines. Their header bytes are never drawn.
3. The line colour of a record's first line sets its pen; a later line changes the pen when its colour byte says so; the pen stays for the blank lines in between.
4. Text bytes 0x24, 0x5E and 0x26 are glyph halves: 0x24 followed by 0x5E draws the trademark sign and 0x26 the copyright sign (credits record) [V: the credits capture].

### 2.3 Counts and the extractor's re-encoding (for Task 3b)

The decoder of the note (`text_check.py`, rule of 2.1 and 2.2) gives [V]: 110 ids, 106 of them valid (all but four), 91 distinct records (three of them empty), **337 lines, of which 241 have text**, 7,137 text characters, longest line 45 characters, flags in {0, 1, 3}, pens in {0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 9, 10, 11, 13}, rows up to 182 and columns up to 34, every drawn line inside the 320-pixel width (the last cell of a line of n characters ends at pixel 8 x column + 7 (n - 1) + 4, so 8 x column + 7 (n - 1) + 5 <= 320 holds for every line; the seven-character answer field at column 34 ends at pixel 318). The engine's long-word read-modify-write of the last cell of a line can reach byte 41 of the scanline (the cell's four bytes start at byte column + 2 x floor(7k / 16)), that is the first bytes of the next scanline, but it writes those bytes back unchanged because the glyph's lit pixels all lie at or below pixel 319; a port that draws pixels, not words, has no such effect. The plan's figures (239 strings, 40 by 25 characters) came from a Ghidra string count and an assumed character grid; the counts of this paragraph replace them.

**Ranges, the empty records and the engine's limits** (for the extractor and the core; answers to the extractor review). (1) *Empty records*: a record is empty when the byte after its two header bytes already has bit 7 set. Three records are empty (`0x43F24` with header 6, 2; `0x45FB3` and `0x45FB4` with the header bytes 0xFF 0xFF, ids 0x10 and 0x11): the header bytes are not a marker, they are simply never used, so the extractor stores zero lines and may keep the header as read. The four ids 0x12, 0x13, 0x15 and 0x19 have **no record**: their offsets point into the middle of other data (the id table itself, a record's end byte, the middle of the run of 0xFF bytes before the record of id 20); the data gives them no meaning (no state writes them, 2.1) and the extractor stores line_count 0; if the original played one the engine would decode whatever bytes are there. (2) *Width*: the engine does not clip. A glyph row is a 5-bit pattern shifted into a long word at the cell's byte offset; a pixel beyond the right edge would land in the next scanline's first bytes. The exact bound that keeps a line on its scanline is 8 x column + 7 (n - 1) + 5 <= 320 (the last cell's glyph is 5 wide); every line of the shipped data satisfies it, and the row bound is row + 6 <= 199 for the first line and row + 11 k + 6 <= 199 for line k [V: all 337 lines, `text_check.py` part 1]. (3) *Ranges*: the flags byte is a line when bit 7 is clear; the engine reads only bits 0 and 1 (bits 2 to 6 are ignored), so 0 to 3 are the whole meaningful range and {0, 1, 3} is what ships; the delay byte is read as a signed byte and multiplied by 4, so 0 to 127 give 0 to 508 passes (120 is only the largest shipped value) and a value of 128 or more acts as no delay; the colour byte holds the pen 0 to 15 in its high nibble (all ranges allowed) and D in its low nibble (2 to 15 valid for typed lines, 0 or 1 never advance the original's cell, section 3.4 rule 5; the shipped values are 2 to 8); a text byte must be 0x20 to 0x5F (the glyph table has 64 entries; the shipped data stays inside) [R: the engine reads exactly these bits and bytes; V for the shipped values].

The extractor orders the records by their lowest id (ids 1 to 110, skipping the ids that are not records) and keeps the lines of a record in order. **How to verify that an id is not a record instead of hard-coding it**: decode from the id's offset with the ranges above; a record is valid only when every line has flags in 0 to 3 (bit 7 clear), every text byte lies in 0x20 to 0x5F and the end flag is reached before the end of the span. The four ids (0x12, 0x13, 0x15, 0x19) fail at once: id 0x15's offset is 0 (the address `0x43DFE`, the id table itself: its first line decodes with flags 4); id 0x19's offset is the address of the end byte of the record before it (`0x441EB`, the last byte of the record of id 99): the "header" is 0xFF 0xB6 and the first line's flags byte is 0x0A; ids 0x12 and 0x13 (offsets `0x45FB5` and `0x45FB6`) lie inside the four-byte 0xFF run that ends at the record of id 20: their first flags bytes are 0x2A and 0x0C. Ids 0x10 and 0x11 (the same run, offsets `0x45FB3` and `0x45FB4`) pass as empty records.

The extractor should write the records line by line. Proposed layout (a port choice, **C**), little-endian: `tables/text.bin`: `u16 line_count`, then per line `u16 first_id` (the lowest id naming the record), `u8 line` (index within the record), `u8 row`, `u8 col` (the record's header, repeated on every line), `u8 flags`, `u8 colour`, `u8 delay`, `u16 length`, `length` bytes of text; `tables/text_ids.bin`: `u16 id_count` (110), then per id `u16 id`, `u16 first_line` (index into text.bin), `u16 line_count` (0 for the empty records and for the four ids that are not records). Two ids that share a record carry the same first_line. The core derives the position of line k as scanline row + k x line step; the line step is 11 except in the main menu list (12).

### 2.4 The record table (no text)

The 91 distinct records of hunk 64 (`0x43F24` to `0x46159`, in address order; three of them empty) and the 110 ids are disk data. Their rows (address, ids, row, column, line count, flags, colour bytes and line lengths per record) are **not listed**; they are the contents of the two extracted files of 2.3: `tables/text_ids.bin`, whole file (662 bytes), FNV-1a-32 `725db064`; and the metadata of `tables/text.bin`, the 337 line headers of 10 bytes each (`u16 first_id`, `u8 line`, `u8 row`, `u8 col`, `u8 flags`, `u8 colour`, `u8 delay`, `u16 length`, the text bytes left out), in file order, 3,370 bytes, FNV-1a-32 `8f18c3cf`. A record's row is a scanline below 200 and its column an even byte column; the lines of one record share the record's header and differ in the colour byte and the delay (2.2).

**The roles of the records, by group** (the rule that tells which record a state plays; the writers are the state routines of section 5, whose addresses are entries of the state pointer at `0x1A6BC`; "mission id list" means the lists of 2.1) [R: found by scanning the state routines for immediate stores into the id list]:

- *Log-disk prompts* (`0x13622`): three one-line variants (no disk in the drive, disk not initialised, disk write-protected, the captured case) and an instruction record of five lines and a blank.
- *Countercode and security*: the failure notice of three lines, which is also the shared notice of every mission's id block and the target of seven alias ids (2.1; `0x127C8` and the mission id list); the screen heading and the prompt line (`0x127C8`, the challenge patched into the prompt, note 09); the answer field of seven blanks that erases the typed digits (`0x1287E`); the lock-out notice of four lines after repeated failures (`0x12A2A`).
- *Boot and end of game*: the integrity-check failure line, with the failure code patched in (`0x13534`, 5.2); the two game-over screens of three lines, one for the F-16 and one for the F/A-18 (`0x139F0`).
- *Pilot entry* (`0x131F4`, `0x1370A`, `0x1381E`): the callsign prompt (a record of five lines), the enlistment confirmation of three lines, the returning-pilot welcome of three lines with the callsign patched in; the free-flight location caption of two lines (`0x12AFC`).
- *Main menu* (`0x11D30`, redrawn by `0x11E04`): title line, header line, eight item records (demonstration, free flight, training demonstration, training practice, qualification, the mission list, the next advanced mission, the flight-log page) and one record of the ESC and SHIFT-ESC lines.
- *Mission selection list* (`0x122CE`): one record of five lines (header, the requirement note and the F1 line), then one one-line record per mission F2 to F6 (copied from the five words at `0x43DF4`, 2.1), then the continuation hint (ESC to the main menu, two lines, played with bit 15 so it follows the list).
- *Flight-log page* (`0x11E04`, `0x11F86`): one record of 18 lines (labels and key lines; the numbers are patched in).
- *Map-screen hints*: the SHIFT-ESC hint in a brighter and a dimmer variant (two lines each); only the dimmer one is written (`0x131F4`).
- *Training and qualification*: the manoeuvre screens of part 1 and part 2 (`0x12452`), the qualification briefing (`0x11CC0`), the qualification success screen (`0x131F4`), the lead aircraft destroyed by a collision (`0x131F4`).
- *Mission completion*: the mission-completed text, and the re-launch prompts of the land and carrier variants (`0x131F4`).
- *The six missions* (`0x12612`, `0x128DA`, the mission id list and `0x131F4`): the intro block (command header, location and date, 6 lines, `0x12612`), the clearance-confirmed line (`0x128DA`), and per mission the briefing A and B, the orders and the outcome records of the chain of 2.1. Missions 3 and 6 keep briefing A and B in one record under two ids. F1 has one success outcome (the confirmed signature); F2 has the success outcome (the airliner lands) and three debrief outcomes written by `0x131F4` (destroyed with a passenger aboard, destroyed without, destroyed by the player); F3 has the escape outcome of the stolen aircraft and an empty-line record shared by two ids; F4 has two outcomes (the pod near the pilot, too far); F5 has three (the missile exploded, nuclear destruction, no detonation). The one-line success banner is a single record shared by the success ids of three missions, and a further one-line empty record is written by `0x131F4`.
- *Flight notices*: the aircraft selection (caption and list, `0x12C02`), bailout survived (`0x1319C`), the pause text (`0x13B76`), the bogey vector line with the vector number patched in (`0x12DB8`), the take-off notice of the land start and the launch notice of the carrier start (`0x12DB8`), the credits screen of 17 lines (`0x13596`).
- *Records no state writes* (none found): the empty records and the three blank-line records, the brighter map hint, and the pause-key hint line (one line), which is the one record with words that nothing posts (section 11).

Records whose text the game patches at run time. **Text index** counts from the first character of the line (the byte after the colour byte), 0-based; "digits right to left" means the formatter `0x116BA` writes the n digits of a value into text indices k + 1 to k + n, least significant digit at k + n, leading zeros replaced by spaces except the last digit.

| record (id) | line | text index and width | format | patcher |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `0x44134` (0x61) | 0 | index 17, 1 character: the failed-check code (the characters 0x31 to 0x37, made from the bits 1, 2 and 4 of the three failed checks); then indices 19 to 22 (digits right to left, 4 hexadecimal digits) | one code character, then the first differing value | `0x119C4` [R] |
| `0x440A8` (0x47) | 0 | indices 29 to 32, 4 characters (the record's seven blanks at 29 to 34 hold the challenge and two blanks) | the four challenge nibbles as upper-case hexadecimal characters, left to right | the challenge builder `0x2963A` (the stores are at `0x296AA`) (note 09) [R] |
| `0x442D5` (0x02) | 0 | from index 8, up to 24 characters | the callsign bytes copied verbatim from `V:1D024` + 0x1E up to the first zero byte | `0x13774` [R] |
| `0x44624` (0x57) | lines 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11, 12 | twelve figures; the text index of each is the table of section 10.6 (it was checked against the original over 28,000 comparisons) | digits right to left, decimal | `0x2934E` [V, section 10.6] |
| `0x44E48` (0x5B) | 3 | month name ending at index 18 (nine characters copied backwards from the table at `0x292CC`), day number in indices 20 and 21 | month name, then 2 decimal digits right to left | `0x292F0` [R, section 10.9] |
| `0x45F62` (0x58) | 0 | indices 13 to 15, 3 characters | the bogey bearing in degrees (heading word `V:4AB7A` divided by 80), three decimal digits left to right, 000 to 359 | `0x295BE` [R] |

Ids no state writes (**R**, no immediate or table reference): 0x05 and 0x08 (copies of 0x01 and 0x07), 0x0B to 0x0D (blank records), 0x10 and 0x11 (empty records), 0x14 (a one-line hint about the P key in the record `0x45FB7`), 0x16 (empty), 0x52 (the brighter SHIFT-ESC hint: 0x53, the dimmer one, is written), and the filler ids of 2.1. They are candidates for section 11.

## 3. Console path and font

This section replaces the plan's "console path": there is none. It states the screen, the font, the text engine rule by rule, the typed-out timing, the sound and the input mode, so that the renderer (Task 5) and the screen module (Task 4) can be written from it. Tags as in section 2.

### 3.1 Which path draws the text

- **No console, no Topaz, no ROM font** [V]. The two `_Write` call sites named by note 02 write the 78-byte player record to the file `df0:config` (section 10). No call into the console device, the graphics `Text` function or Intuition exists. The text engine of 3.4 draws glyph bits straight into the bit planes with the processor. Reproduction check [V]: the main menu, credits and log-disk captures of note 01a equal the engine's output in all 64,000 pixels (0 mismatches each, `text_check.py`).
- **The display.** Text screens use the same 320 by 200 screen of five bit planes (8,000 bytes per plane, 40 bytes per scanline) as the flight view; the engine writes planes 0 to 3 (the pen is 0 to 15), clearing the fifth plane through `0x34802` when it is in use. The screen is double buffered and the page flips every pass; the engine therefore **draws every character twice, on two consecutive passes, one for each buffer** (3.4, rule 5) [R, with V on the effect: per pass the planes of the rig equal the model]. The frontend needs one text layer per frame, not two.
- **No grid.** The plan's 40 by 25 character grid does not exist. A record is addressed by scanline (row) and by byte column; a character cell is 7 pixels wide and the glyph is 5 pixels wide, so a line holds at most 45 characters (the 48-entry column table allows 48: no record uses more than 45). Line pitch is 11 scanlines (12 in the main menu list). For the port (**C**): keep the original's pixel addressing; do not convert to character rows and columns.
- **Control sequences**: none. Colour, position, clear and typed-out speed are the record fields of section 2 and the engine rules below.

### 3.2 The font (hunk 61, `0x4283C`)

- **Layout** [V: verified by rendering; the three captures above are reproduced pixel for pixel]. A table of 64 words at `0x4283C`: the word for character 0x20 + i (i = 0 to 63, that is space to underscore) is the byte offset from `0x4283C` of that character's glyph. A glyph is 7 bytes, rows top to bottom, the pixel columns in bits 7 to 3 (the low three bits are zero in all 54 distinct glyphs), bit 7 the leftmost pixel; a set bit is a lit pixel. The offsets run from 0x80 to 0x1F3 (54 distinct offsets, 53 distinct bit patterns: the equals sign and the hyphen/underscore point at two copies of one pattern), so glyph data lies at `0x428BC`-`0x42A35` (378 bytes, every byte referenced); several characters point at one glyph (for example the punctuation marks that the font lacks all map to one near-empty glyph) the 54 distinct offsets are 0x80 + 7i for i = 0 to 53, so the glyph data is contiguous; a reader must follow the offset table, not step through the data. Lower-case letters and characters above 0x5F are absent (an index above 63 would read glyph bytes as offsets); the data and the keys of 3.7 stay in 0x20-0x5F.
- **Extractor (Task 3b)** [C]: no Kickstart ROM is needed and the `--rom` option of Task 3 is not required for the front end. Write `fonts/menu5x7.bin`: 64 glyphs x 7 bytes in character order (448 bytes), built by following the offset table. Anchor: the file is at a fixed address, `0x4283C` in the relocated image (hunk 61 offset 0x16C); the sanity checks are that the first table word is 0x0080, all 64 words lie in 0x0080-0x01F3, the glyph of the space (offset 0x80) is seven zero bytes and the low three bits of every glyph byte are zero. The characters 0x24 with 0x5E and the character 0x26 form the trademark and copyright signs of the title lines.
- **Fallback font** is therefore not needed for correctness: the real font is in the game data the extractor already reads. Review Focus 4 of the plan (ROM absent) does not arise; a port-drawn font stays optional (**C**) only for runs without assets.
- **Pen and pixels.** A glyph pixel sets the screen pixel to the pen of the line; a clear glyph bit leaves the screen unchanged (no background is drawn: the engine overdraws, it never fills a cell). Erasing (a backspace in input mode, 3.7) draws the full-block glyph (index 0x3C) with pen 0.
- **Cell positions.** The character k of a line (k = 0, 1, ...) starts at pixel x = 8 x col + 7k, so the byte offset of its long-word write is col + 2 x floor(7k / 16) and its shift 7k mod 16 (the original's 48-entry table at `0x4615A` is exactly this) [V: all 48 entries].

### 3.3 The pass

The engine runs once per **pass** of the main loop (the frame routine `0x11124` ends with the call, so it runs in every mode). A pass is not a video blank: the loop is not locked to the blank in the front end. On the credits screen the pass counter `V:4AB8E` fell by 6 per blank in every one of 15 consecutive blanks (log `flow-s1-passrate.txt`): **6 passes per 20 ms blank, 300 passes per second, 37.5 passes per port step** at idle [V]. The rate depends on the work of the pass; section 3.8 gives the figures measured on the typed and instant screens. All delays below are in passes.

### 3.4 The engine, rule by rule

State: the id list, the current id, the running flag, the mode byte, the pen, the delay D, the flags of the current line, the current position (column index k, text pointer, plane byte offset) and the saved position of the start of the line (section 1 table). One call of the engine does exactly one of the following [V: the model of `text_check.py` equals the original's routine on 58,082 passes of every id alone and four lists, planes, running flag, index, mode byte, hold counter and sound-request count compared after every pass, 0 differences]:

1. **No text** (the current id is 0): the line step returns to 11 scanlines; when the hold counter is positive it falls by one and, on reaching zero, the mode byte gets bit 7 set (**text finished**); the running flag is cleared. The engine does nothing while the suppress flag `V:4A929` is set.
2. **A record begins** (the running flag is 0 and the id is not 0): the record is found through the id table (2.1); position = row x 40 + col bytes, or, with bit 15, the saved position plus one line step (the saved position is already one step below the last line, 2.1); the hold counter, the line delay and the character countdown are cleared; unless the mode byte is 2 or 3 (input mode, 3.7) the mode byte becomes 0 and the two draw-twice flags become 1; the running flag is set; flags, pen and delay D are taken from the record's first line (D = 2 when the id has bit 14 set); the line's start state is saved; the first character is handled **in this same pass** (rule 5).
3. **Waiting**: while the line delay counter (set by rule 7 as 4 x the delay byte, passes) is above zero after its decrement, nothing happens. A counter of 0 or below proceeds on the pass after the line ended.
4. **Instant line** (flags bit 0 clear): the whole line is drawn in one pass, every character, from the saved start of the line; at the end of the line the first draw-twice flag counts down: on the first end the engine restores the start of the line and a **key click** is requested (3.6), so the next pass draws the whole line again; on the second end the line is complete (rule 7). An instant line therefore occupies two passes, P and P + 1.
5. **Typed line** (flags bit 0 set): one character per D passes. The character at the current cell is drawn **twice**: when its countdown expires it is drawn (and the click is requested for a non-space character) and the countdown is reloaded with D; on the next pass it is drawn again and only then the cell advances; the passes in between do nothing. **Exception, the first character of a typed first line** when it is drawn by the record's own start pass (no skipped space before it): it is drawn on three passes, 0, 1 and 2 (the start pass, the reload pass that follows because the countdown starts at 0, and the advancing pass), with click requests on passes 0 and 1 (the draw-twice flag is still set on both); every other character is drawn on two passes with one click request [V: the model and the rig, e.g. id 0x61 requests clicks on passes 0, 1, 4, 7, ...]. With flags bit 1 set a space is skipped at once, without drawing, delay or click (it costs nothing); without bit 1 a space occupies a cell time like any character and draws nothing. D must be at least 2: with 0 or 1 the original's countdown would never let the cell advance (the data has 2 to 8) [R].
6. **A character is drawn** as the 7-row glyph at the cell's position, in the pen, as in 3.2; a text byte with bit 7 set ends the record (the engine tests the sign of the byte before drawing, so it acts as an end-of-record flags byte even in the middle of a line); 0 ends the line. The glyph pairs 0x24 with 0x5E (trademark) and 0x26 (copyright) are ordinary adjacent glyphs of the font and need no special drawing. The engine does not clip and does not wrap: the glyph's rows are written at plane byte offset (row x 40 + the cell's offset) + 40 x r for r = 0 to 6, so a line placed so that a row falls at scanline 200 or beyond writes past the 8,000-byte plane. This cannot happen with the shipped records (the last glyph row is at scanline 199, section 2.3) or with a bit-15 continuation id after them (the continuation after the mission selection list's last record starts at scanline 178 and draws two lines, the blank one and the hint, to scanline 195); the port clips at 200 (**C**).
7. **A line ends** (the text byte is 0): the delay byte is read, the next line's flags byte follows; the line delay counter is set to 4 x the delay byte (passes); the next line's flags and colour (pen and D) are loaded, its position is the previous line's plus the line step, and its cell index restarts at 0. If the next flags byte has bit 7 set the record is over (rule 8), after the same wait.
8. **A record ends**: when the list holds another id the engine passes to it (the running flag is cleared, the next record begins on the next pass); on the last id of the list the mode byte becomes 1, the hold counter 50, the list is emptied; 50 passes later (rule 1) the mode byte reads 0x81.

Closed forms, from the first pass of a record (pass 0 = the pass in which rule 2 runs) [V: equal to the rig on every id]:

- **Instant first line**: drawn on passes 0 and 1; the line ends on pass 1; the next line begins on pass 1 + max(4 x delay, 1).
- **Typed first line**, D the delay: the first non-space character appears on pass 0; character j (j >= 1, counting non-space characters when flags bit 1 is set, all characters otherwise) first appears on pass 1 + j x D (on pass j x D when the line starts with skipped spaces, because the skip reloads the countdown); every character is drawn again on the next pass. The line ends on pass 1 + m x D (m = the number of cells), then the wait of rule 7.
- **Later typed lines**: if the line begins on pass P (rule 3), character j first appears on pass P + j x D and the line ends on pass P + m x D; a line without text ends on P.
- **After the last line**: the record ends max(4 x delay, 1) passes after the last line end; the finished flag (bit 7 of the mode byte) is set 50 passes later.

Worked totals, passes from the start of the list to the finished flag [V, rig]: id 0x63 (game over, F/A-18) 375, id 0x62 (F-16) 416, the paused text (id 0x26) 140, the landing-successful screen (0x4A) 679, the first-credits record (0x0F) 1,159, the qualification briefing (0x49) 2,041, the first training screen (0x09) 2,277.

### 3.5 Pens

The pen of a line indexes the live 32-colour palette; the menu palette, the other palettes a text screen can sit on and the fades are section 4.

### 3.6 Sound

A key click is requested through the sound start routine (`0x1A042`, note 10) for every non-space character of a typed line at its first draw (the first character of a record also on its reload pass, 3.4 rule 5: two requests) and at the end of the first draw of an instant line, unless `V:4A88F` is 2 or more or `V:4A942` is positive [R; the model's sound-request count equals the rig's on every pass, 0 differences, V]. The request is sound 1 with parameter 2 (the original's call arguments are in the routine at `0x37C2E`). The sound content is note 10's (`docs/re/10-sound.md`, section 7: the click is a tone of voice 3).

### 3.7 Input mode and the cursor

- **Modes.** The mode byte is 2 for the name entry of the flight log (section 10) and 3 for the countercode (note 09, section 5.5). The state that wants input first waits for the prompt's finished hold (mode byte 1), then sets the id list to a one-record list, clears the running flag and writes mode 2 or 3 and the number of characters allowed (`V:4A8AD`; 20 for the name, 6 or fewer for the countercode, section 5.5) [R]. The engine begins that record in input mode: **the record's text is not drawn** (the engine returns at once while the ring is empty); its row and column only fix where the first typed character goes. For the countercode that record is id 4 (seven blanks at row 72, column 34, the cell after the prompt line), for the name entry id 3 (row 130, column 4) [V: the rig, below].
- **Keys.** Each pass the engine takes one raw key code from a 10-byte ring (`V:4A899`, count `V:4A8B1`, filled by the key handler; read index `V:4A8B0`) and translates it through the 96-byte table at `0x37C92`: raw 0x01-0x0A the digits 1 to 9 and 0, 0x10-0x19 Q to P, 0x20-0x28 A to L, 0x31-0x37 Z to M, 0x40 space, 0x41 backspace (character 8), every other code is ignored; the RETURN code (0x44) ends the entry [R, V for the effects below].
- **Effects** [V: the rig, `V:4A898` = 3, `V:4A8AD` = 6, list [4], raw codes pushed one at a time, four passes each]: a typed character is drawn in the line's pen at the next cell (7 pixels to the right of the previous one, starting at x = 8 x column) over **two passes** (draw, draw again and advance, as 3.4 rule 5); the count of characters allowed falls by one and the count stored (`V:4A8AE`) rises by one; **backspace** erases the previous cell (the full-block glyph in pen 0), moves the cell back by one and restores the counts, and does nothing before the first character; when the count of allowed characters is used up the next key (the seventh, for the countercode) ends the entry as RETURN would: bit 7 of the mode byte is set for one pass and the engine then ends the record (mode byte 1, hold 50). Quirk [V, rig]: a backspace moves the cell back but not the text pointer, so typed characters plus erased ones are bounded by the record's text length (7 for id 4): past it the engine reads the bytes of the next record as text and does not end; the port need not reproduce this.
- **No cursor is drawn** [V: the rig images show only the typed glyphs; the countercode prompt capture of note 01a shows the prompt, the challenge and a dash, nothing at the typing cell]. The "cursor" of the plan is only the engine's current cell, which a port may show as it likes (**C**) but which the original does not draw.
- **Storage and RETURN.** In mode 2 each typed character is also stored into the player record's callsign field (`V:1D024` + 0x1E); in mode 3 it is only drawn. RETURN in mode 3 runs the comparison `0x2970A` (note 09), which writes its result to `V:4A964`, and sets bit 7 of the mode byte; in mode 2 it clears bit 1 of the mode byte, calls the log writer `0x1858A` and sets `V:4A88D` to 0xFF [R]. The countercode attempts are section 5.5, the name rules section 10.

### 3.8 Passes per blank on the text screens

Measured on the reference emulator [V] (script `flow-t1-engine-5.txt`, log `flow-t1-engine-5.txt`: the key 5 pressed in the main menu, then 380 consecutive blanks, the pass counter `V:4A879` (a byte that counts passes) against the blank counter `V:4A82C`):

| state (flat address) and what it shows | blanks | passes per blank |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| `0x11E04`, the menu state on the key (first 8 blanks, includes the key's delivery) | 8 | 2.38 |
| `0x1201E`, the chosen item's line restated, text only | 39 | 5.41 |
| `0x1234C`, a state that draws the 3D scene (gate `V:4A84D` on) | 40 | 0.10 (4 passes) |
| `0x12378`, text only | 43 | 5.86 |
| `0x127C8`, the countercode screen set-up with the scene drawn | 36 | 0.14 (5 passes) |
| `0x1287E`, the countercode prompt typing over the picture | 73 | 3.52 |
| `0x128AA`, the prompt waiting for the answer | 134 | 5.95 |
| credits idle (`flow-s1-passrate.txt`) | 15 | 6.00 (every blank) |
| log-disk prompt while its lines are drawn (instant lines, `V:4AB8E` from -2 to -65, `flow-s1-boot.txt` blanks 27 to 53) | 26 | 2.4 |
| the same screen once the text is finished (blanks 54 to 59) | 5 | 5.2 |

So a pass is **not** a blank: text-only states run about 6 passes per blank when nothing is drawn (the loop is limited by the work of the pass, not by the display), about 2.4 while whole instant lines are being drawn (a pass then costs about 0.4 blank of processor time), 3.5 during the typed countercode line, and a state that draws the 3D scene takes about 10 blanks per pass. Every wait of the state machine that counts with `V:4AB8E` counts passes too (section 5.1). The engine's model reproduces the emulator: for the typed prompt record (id 0x47, delay 6) the text pointer read at 64 of 64 consecutive blank samples equals the model's pointer at the pass the pass counter gives, before the four patched challenge characters (after them the record differs from the unpatched image the model reads: the same pointer advances one cell per 6 passes through the digits) [V].

**Pass cost model and port rule (C, fitted).** A pass takes longer when it draws or requests a click, so a flat pass rate does not reproduce the measured typed-out durations (the credits text takes 245 blanks to type, 39 steps, against 1,109 passes: 4.5 passes per blank, not 6). A two-term cost fits: **a pass lasts 1/6 blank, plus 0.25 blank for each key-click request it makes, plus 0.0475 blank for each glyph it blits** (a glyph blit is every character of an instant line, spaces included, and every non-skipped character of a typed line, on each of its two draws). The terms were fitted on the credits screen (1,109 passes, 174 click requests, 348 glyph blits: 244.9 blanks predicted, 245 measured) and the log-disk window (63 passes, 26 blanks measured, `flow-s1-boot.txt` blanks 27 to 53); checks that were not fitted: the game-over text of the F/A-18 (id 0x63: 325 passes, 41 requests, 82 blits to the end-of-list pass: 68.3 blanks predicted against 78 measured by 5.12, 12 % low, the crash sound is playing there) and the main menu (256 passes, 30 requests, 724 blits: 84.6 blanks predicted against 96 measured to the end of its list in 5.4, 12 % low) [V as a fit; the counts are the model's, `text_check.py`'s `Model`]. **The timing contract for the port (C; this is the one rule, the tables of section 5 and 6 follow it).**

1. **Stage durations are the measured blanks, converted per stage.** Every screen sequence of sections 5 and 6 is a list of stages with a duration in blanks measured on the reference emulator (the tables name the log). Each stage becomes `(blanks * 8 + 25) / 50` steps (integer division) **individually**; a sequence's total is the **sum of its stages' steps** (not the conversion of the total blanks, which can differ by one or two steps because of rounding; the stage lists below sum to the totals they state). Stages are contiguous spans from one state's start to the next state's start.
2. **One-pass and wait stages are stages.** A state that takes one long pass (the 33-blank menu build, the 6-blank disk check) or waits on `V:4AB8E` or a fade is simply a stage with its measured blanks; the core waits that many steps and does nothing else.
3. **Typed text inside a stage runs the pass machine of 3.4 at the screen's measured rate**, so that characters, clicks, ESC and keys act at the right character: the engine runs against a budget of 37,500 units per step, a pass costing 1,000 + 1,500 x clicks + 285 x glyph blits (the fitted model above, 37.5 idle-pass units per step); when the stage list gives the typed stage a duration (credits 245 blanks, menu 96, game over 78) the core scales the budget so that the engine's end-of-list pass falls in the step the table gives. The figures of the model are the fallback for a typed stage that has no measured duration.
3a. **The mission texts use the same engine and the same cost model** [C, fitted on the measured records; rule read from the code]. The briefing and orders texts of the mission screens (F1 to F6) are not typed by a separate routine: they go through the text engine of 3.4 like the credits and the prompt, one typed character per D passes with D taken from the record. What differs from the credits is the cost of a click: on the mission screens a pass that requests a click costs about 0.85 of a blank, so the pass cost is 1,000 + 5,350 x clicks + 285 x glyph blits units against the budget of 37,500 units per step (the credits fit used 1,500 per click; the 5,350 of milestone 7, 3.8, replaced the first fit's 5,100). Every measured mission-text record is reproduced by this cost within 2.5 percent of its measured duration in blanks (the table below). The port therefore needs no per-screen typing rate: each text record is typed by the engine with its own D and click positions, and the fitted rate constant of the first port version (29,000 units per step) is dropped. **Measured typing durations to pin** [V, one run each; blanks from the start of the typing to the engine byte 1; 13.5]. The counts are the port engine's dry run of the real record lists (passes P, click requests C, glyph blits B up to the pass that ends the list; the briefing list is the intro record 0x5B plus the mission's briefing, the orders list the record pair of 13.5 segment B); the model is units = 1,000 P + 5,100 C + 285 B, blanks = units / 6,000 (37,500 units per 6.25-blank step):

| text | measured blanks | P | C | B | model blanks | error |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| F1 briefing (second) | 490 | 1,836 | 188 | 391 | 484.4 | -1.1 % |
| F2 briefing (second) | 430 | 1,568 | 174 | 363 | 426.5 | -0.8 % |
| F3 briefing (second) | 460 | 1,590 | 207 | 429 | 461.3 | +0.3 % |
| F4 briefing (first) | 470 | 1,801 | 187 | 389 | 477.6 | +1.6 % |
| F5 briefing (first) | 530 | 1,920 | 214 | 443 | 522.9 | -1.3 % |
| F6 briefing (first) | 480 | 1,760 | 184 | 383 | 467.9 | -2.5 % |
| F1 orders | 404 | 1,555 | 162 | 324 | 412.3 | +2.0 % |
| F2 orders | 312 | 1,234 | 112 | 224 | 311.5 | -0.2 % |

(F3 to F6 orders are not captured.) The port's own check types the records and compares steps, `blanks x 8 / 50 + 1` (the end hold adds one step): F1 78 against 79.4, F2 69 against 69.8, F3 74 against 74.6, F4 77 against 76.2, F5 84 against 85.8, F6 75 against 77.8 (-3.6 %, the largest), orders 66 against 65.6 and 50 against 50.9. **The F6 discrepancy is settled as noise, not a mis-fitted click cost** [C]: in blanks F6 is -2.5 %, and the step figure adds the quantisation of both figures (about one step) to it. A refit does not help: with the pass cost and the blit cost fixed, the click cost that minimises the largest error of the eight points is 5,125 (largest error 2.4 %, F6 still -2.4 %); freeing the blit cost as well (4,750 and 475) gives 2.2 %. The residuals change sign between records (F4 and the F1 orders high, F5 and F6 low) and do not follow the click or blit density per pass (F4 0.104 and 0.216, F5 0.111 and 0.231, F6 0.105 and 0.218 clicks and blits per pass), so the single-run measurements and the unmodelled part of the pass cost, not one term, account for them. Keep 5,100; the port test accepts 2 percent plus the quantisation, and F6's -3.6 % in steps lies at its edge. A second capture of F5 and F6 would tell whether the scatter is the measurement.

**Item 3a re-measured at one-blank resolution, F3 to F6 (milestone 7, Task 2; the question "a second capture of F5 and F6 would tell whether the scatter is the measurement" answered)** [V: `flow7-brief-m5.txt` to `-m8.txt` (the menu snapshot, the unlock bytes poked as in 13.5, the F key; the blank counter, the engine byte and the state every blank from the first frame of the state `0x126F6`), `flow7-orders-digits-m5.txt` to `-m8.txt` (the prompt snapshots, the answer typed digit by digit, RETURN at a known blank; the engine byte every blank from the orders state `0x12A50`, which starts one blank after the RETURN; these scripts carry the answers, so `gen-flow7.py` writes them to the ignored scratch directory and they are not kept) and `flow7-orders-m5.txt` to `-m8.txt` (the same with the `code` command, whose first sample falls 8 to 22 blanks after the text started: 506 against 516 for F3), 1 run each]. The duration is the number of blanks from the first frame of the state to the first frame in which the engine byte is 1:

| text | measured blanks | the stride-10 figure above | P | C | B | model, click 5,100 | error | model, click 5,350 | error |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| F3 briefing (second) | 464 | 460 | 1,590 | 207 | 429 | 461.3 | -0.6 % | 470.0 | +1.3 % |
| F4 briefing (first) | 484 | 470 | 1,801 | 187 | 389 | 477.6 | -1.3 % | 485.4 | +0.3 % |
| F5 briefing (first) | 530 | 530 | 1,920 | 214 | 443 | 522.9 | -1.3 % | 531.9 | +0.4 % |
| F6 briefing (first) | 472 | 480 | 1,760 | 184 | 383 | 467.9 | -0.9 % | 475.6 | +0.8 % |
| F3 orders | 516 | not captured | 1,892 | 199 | 397 | 503.3 | -2.5 % | 511.6 | -0.8 % |
| F4 orders | 504 | not captured | 1,843 | 193 | 385 | 489.5 | -2.9 % | 497.5 | -1.3 % |
| F5 orders | 340 | not captured | 1,304 | 121 | 241 | 331.6 | -2.5 % | 336.7 | -1.0 % |
| F6 orders | 381 | not captured | 1,399 | 145 | 289 | 370.1 | -2.8 % | 376.2 | -1.3 % |

(P, C and B from the port's own engine on the real lists, as in the table above; the orders lists are the records 0x5D with 0x2A, 0x31, 0x37 and 0x3E.) **Findings.** (1) The scatter of the first table was the measurement: F6 is 472, not 480, and its model error is -0.9 %, not -2.5 %; the port's -3.6 % in steps was the stride of 10 blanks and the quantisation, not a mis-fitted click cost. (2) The model is low in all eight texts, by 0.6 to 1.3 % for the briefings and by **2.5 to 2.9 % for the four orders lists** (the orders of F1 and F2, 404 and 312, were measured from a first sample taken after the text had started, as the `code` runs of F3 to F6 were, and are about 10 blanks short: 414 and 322 would give the same -2 to -3 %). (3) The three-term cost cannot separate the click and the blit (every record has about two blits to a click request), but a **click cost of 5,350 with the pass cost 1,000 and the blit cost 285 leaves every one of the eight within +1.3 % and -1.3 %** (the minimax over the eight; a free three-parameter fit reaches 0.35 % with a negative blit cost, which is not a cost, and a pass cost of 1,118 with a click cost of 4,276 reaches 1.1 %). The orders lists type slower than their model by 2 % more than the briefings, which no term of the cost explains; the figures stay the evidence. **Port rule (C): the click cost of the mission texts is 5,350 (`MS_CLICK_COST`), the measured durations to pin are 490, 430, 464, 484, 530 and 472 for the briefings and 404 (about 414), 312 (about 322), 516, 504, 340 and 381 for the orders, and the test accepts 2 percent plus the quantisation.** The briefings and orders texts of F1 and F2 should be re-measured with the answer typed digit by digit if an exact figure is wanted.

If a later capture of a text shows a larger deviation, the cost of a click (not the rate) is the term to refit.
4. **Exception: stages in which the flight simulation runs** (the three-tick wait 6.2, flight, the crash restart passes of note 05 17.17) count **one pass per step**, as the flight time-base ruling of note 05 section 14 says; their blank figures are not converted.
5. The fixed step is 125 ms (6.25 blanks); ESC, SHIFT-ESC and digit keys are acted on in the step after they arrive (one action per press, 5.8).
## 4. Palette and fades

This section settles the colours the text screens use and the fades at every change of mode. The fade mechanism itself (levels, the per-blank stepping) is measured in note 04 ("the fade table" row); what this note adds is which palette a screen shows, who loads it, and the fade targets at each mode change. Tags as in section 2.

### 4.1 The palettes (hunk 21)

All values are 12-bit Amiga colour words (4 bits per channel).

| table | address | content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| menu palette | `0x29F24` | 32 words (entries 0 to 15 and 16 to 31; disk data, not listed: `palette/menu.pal`, 96 bytes, FNV-1a-32 `c4733f3c`) |
| flash palette | `0x29F64` | 32 equal words (orange); the emulator shows (236, 159, 0), note 06 question 8 |
| fade steps | `0x29FA4` | 16 steps of 16 words, step s = 15 minus the fade level; step 0 is the flight palette (16 entries; not listed: `palette/flight.pal`, 48 bytes, FNV-1a-32 `87b6cb23`), steps 13 to 15 are all zero (note 04) |

The extractor's `palette/menu.pal` and `palette/flash.pal` (Task 3a) carry the first two tables; the frontend turns a 4-bit level into a display value with its measured curve (`FADE_CURVE` in `src/frontend/sdl/fade.c`: 0, 0, 0, 28, 43, 56, 72, 89, 106, 123, 141, 159, 177, 197, 216, 255). With that curve every pen of the main menu, credits and log-disk captures comes out exactly: pens 1 (141, 0, 0), 4 (0, 106, 0), 5 (56, 56, 159), 10 (56, 56, 56), 11 (123, 72, 0), 13 (106, 106, 106) and 2 (123, 123, 123) are the measured colours [V: `text_check.py` shows that the pen of every model pixel maps to one frame colour and different pens to different colours, 0 mismatches; that the colours equal the curve applied to the palette words was compared by hand for these seven pens when the note was written, not by the tool]; the measured points of the curve are 3 to 28 through B to 159, 13 to 197 and F to 236 on the flash, so the table's 255 for level 15 is the harness value (flash frames and a pen of level 15 differ by that, note 06 question 8).

### 4.2 Two palette regimes: a pen means two colours

A text pen indexes the **live** palette, and two palettes are live on text screens [V: the colour registers read in 12 reference snapshots and in the per-blank logs of section 5; `flow-t1-palettes.txt` and the logs named there]:

1. **The menu palette** (all 32 entries) on the screens that draw only text on a cleared screen: the credits, the log-disk prompt, the welcome and enlistment screens, the main menu and its restated item, the mission selection list, the flight-log statistics, the game-over text. The state that starts such a screen copies the 32 words at `0x29F24` into the live buffer `V:4A718` (`0x13C1C`, section 1) and the palette reaches the hardware within the reload passes (below).
2. **The flight palette** (fade step 0 in entries 0 to 15, entries 16 to 31 zero) on every screen where the 3D scene is drawn behind or beside the text: the free-flight location and aircraft selection (the "map" is the rendered terrain), the countercode prompt, the mission briefing, orders and outcome screens (ids 0x1E and 0x25 and 0x59 were logged on it), the pause text (fading to level 10, note 06 item 8) and every in-flight text.

The two palettes agree on pens 0, 3, 6, 7, 12, 14 and 15 and differ on the rest; the pens the records use, with their colours (the curve of 4.1; the last column counts the records whose lines use the pen):

| pen | menu colour | flight colour | records |
| ---: | --- | --- | ---: |
| 0 | (0, 0, 0) | (0, 0, 0) | 3 |
| 1 | (141, 0, 0) | (106, 0, 0) | 13 |
| 2 | (123, 123, 123) | (106, 106, 106) | 7 |
| 4 | (0, 106, 0) | (0, 56, 0) | 4 |
| 5 | (56, 56, 159) | (43, 43, 89) | 8 |
| 9 | (0, 28, 0) | (197, 123, 0) | 48 |
| 10 | (56, 56, 56) | (72, 72, 72) | 4 |
| 11 | (123, 72, 0) | (0, 28, 0) | 2 |
| 13 | (106, 106, 106) | (89, 89, 89) | 3 |

Pen 9, used by 48 of the 91 records (the briefings and prompts), is amber on the flight palette (the colour of the sun entry) and nearly black green on the menu palette: those records are meant for the flight palette. Pen 0 (three records, colour byte 0x07, the outcomes of the cruise-missile mission F5) draws black; what makes these lines visible is section 5.13's.

### 4.3 How a palette reaches the screen

- The live buffer `V:4A718` holds 32 colour words. A copy into it (`0x13C1C`) sets the reload counter `V:4A95C` to 2; the clear (`0x13C5E`) zeroes it and sets the counter too.
- Every pass the flip routine (`0x1827C`) loads **all 32** words of the buffer into the screen's colour registers while `V:4A95C` is positive and counts it down, unless bit 8 of `V:4A98A` is set; every blank the vertical-blank server loads **16** words (entries 0 to 15) when the fade level `V:4A958` equals the target `V:4A959` and `V:4A95C` is positive. So a menu palette copied at the start of a state is on screen within one pass and is kept for 2 passes; the pass rate (section 3.3) decides how many blanks that is.
- The fade levels (note 04): the level steps toward the target once per 2 blanks going down and 3 going up, loading the 16 words of fade step 15 minus the level; when the level reaches the target the step's 16 words are also copied into entries 0 to 15 of the buffer, so the reload keeps showing the flight palette's step there. **Initial state** [R, `0x1D17C`]: level and target 15, reload counter 0.
- **Consequence for the menu**: no fade is used between text screens; the transition is a cut (the planes are cleared by `0x34802` at the start of a screen, the new palette arrives within a pass). Between the credits and the log-disk prompt the hardware shows the first sixteen colours as the flight palette for 12 blanks (`0x135C8` copies the table at `0x29FA4` before the log-disk state starts) with a cleared screen, then the menu palette (log `flow-s1-boot.txt`, blanks 10 to 21) [V]: invisible, since the planes are empty.
- **Flash.** The flash palette is loaded by the flip routine only inside the crash hold (note 05 17.17 rule 8): once per two blanks during the 40 iterations, but only the last load is seen as a frame.

### 4.4 Palette and fade at every mode change

The table lists each change of screen or mode, the palette regime after it (4.2), what loads it, the fade level and target, and where the per-blank evidence is. "Cut" means the palette changes at once with no fade (the screen is cleared first). Level and target are `V:4A958` and `V:4A959`; the blanks are measured on the reference emulator [V] unless marked R (read from the writers listed in 4.3).

| change | palette after | loaded by | fade level and target | blanks and evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| start-up, then credits | menu palette | the start-up code stores level and target 15 and the in-flight palette (`0x1D17C`, `0x11962`); the credits state `0x135C8` copies the menu palette (`0x135A6`) | 15 and 15; no fade | menu palette in the colour registers 13 to 15 blanks after the state `0x13596` starts [V: 2 runs, 5.2] |
| credits, any key, to the log-disk prompt | menu palette after a cut | the key handler's state `0x135C8` copies fade steps 0 and 1 (`0x29FA4`, flight colours) into the buffer and clears the planes; the log-disk state `0x13622` copies the menu palette (`0x13676`) | 15 and 15; no fade | the registers show the flight colours for 12 blanks on a cleared screen (invisible), then the menu palette: log `flow-s1-boot.txt` blanks 10 to 21 [V] |
| log-disk prompt, RETURN, to the welcome screens and the main menu | menu palette | `0x1370A` (welcome) and `0x11D30` (menu) copy the menu palette (`0x13734`, `0x11D9C`) | 15 and 15; no fade | cut each time; the menu build then spends 33 blanks in its delay loop before its text starts (5.4) [V] |
| main menu, a mode key, to the echo line | menu palette | stays | 15 and 15 | no change at the key (5.4) |
| echo line to the first 3D screen of a mode (location or aircraft selection, countercode prompt, mission start, training, qualification) | black, then the flight palette | the mode dispatch `0x1201E` freezes the fade where it is (`V:4A959` = `V:4A958`, `0x120E8`) and clears the live buffer to black (`0x13C5E`; all 16 colour registers read zero from 43 to 45 blanks after the mode key, `flow-s1-menukeys-a.txt`, 5.4); the flight states then store target 15 with level 0 (`0x1235E`, `0x123D4`, `0x1254E`) | level 0 to target 15: one level per 3 blanks, 0 to 15 in 42 blanks | `flow-s2-esc-flight-dense.txt` (all-black palette k 29 to 169, fade in k 162 to 206 from the ESC key); 6.1 and 5.11 [V] |
| location and aircraft selection, countercode prompt | flight palette (entries 16 to 31 zero) | already live | 15 and 15 | the colour registers of the snapshots `location-select`, `aircraft-select`, `code-prompt`, `qual-prompt` read fade step 0 [V: `flow-t1-palettes.txt`] |
| selection (or any flight) ESC | fade out in the flight palette, then the menu palette for the echo, then black and fade in | the dispatcher `0x11748` stores target 0 (`0x1188C`) on the request (the other writers of target 0 are `0x11B1A`, the ESC state `0x11AE2` during a demo, and `0x13B64`, the end of the qualification result `0x13B24`); `0x11AE2` copies the menu palette (`0x11B40`); the dispatch `0x1201E` clears the buffer | 15 (10 from a pause) down to 0: **one duration, 32 blanks from the request to level 0 = 5 steps** (the levels move one per 2 blanks for 28 of them after a first step 3 blanks in; from a pause 21 blanks = 3 steps; other runs read 30 and 31 by pass jitter); then 0 up to 15 in 47 blanks = 8 steps (3 blanks per level) | 5.11 table [V] |
| SHIFT-ESC from flight to the menu | flight palette stays while the planes are cleared, then the menu palette | `0x11A70` resets (`0x1D1BE`), `0x11D30` copies the menu palette | 15 and 15; no fade | the menu palette is loaded 45 blanks after the key [V: `flow-s2-shiftesc-flight.txt`, 5.11] |
| game over | the flash block during the hold, then the menu palette | the hold's flip routine (note 05 17.17 rule 8); `0x139F0` copies the menu palette (`0x139FA`) | 15 and 15 | orange flash block `0x0FB2` on odd blanks k = 1, 3, 5 of the hold (only the last is a visible frame), menu palette from k = 12 [V: `flow-s2-gameover.txt`, 5.12] |
| pause | flight palette, dimmed | the flight state `0x12EFE` (`0x12FF8`) and the approach state `0x12D58` (`0x12DA4`) store target 10 when the pause byte is set; the pause state `0x13B76` waits for the level | 15 down to 10 (11 blanks); on release back up to 15 (3 blanks per level) | 5.14, `flow-s2-pause.txt` [V] |
| mission-end overlay (modes 3 to 9) | flight palette at level 15 (the frozen cockpit stays) | none | 15 and 15 | the colour registers constant in all five staged runs of 5.13 [V]; pen 0 text is black over the picture |
| demo start and mission start fade-in | black, then the flight palette | `0x11B7A`: target 15 with level 0 | 0 up to 15, 3 blanks per level | 6.1, 5.4 |
| blackout in flight | black | `0x13186`: target 2 (every entry zero) while the accumulator `V:4A941` exceeds 5, back to 15 afterwards | 15 down to 2 (28 blanks), up (3 blanks per level) | note 04 |
| crash hold, restart | flash frame, then the flight palette | the flip routine; `0x138D8` stores target 15 (`0x13922`) | 15 and 15 | one orange frame at the end of the hold (note 05 17.17) |

The table's rows with "cut" have no fade: **text-only screens are never faded in the original**; the frontend (Task 5) switches the palette in the step in which the screen starts and clears the text layer in the same step. The fade table of the frontend (`FADE_CURVE`, `fade.c`) applies to the rows with a level change, using the flight palette steps for the picture and for the text drawn over it (the pause text fades with the picture; the echo line does not fade because it is shown after the level reached 0 and before the new fade-in).


## 5. Screen state machine

Everything the original shows outside flight is one state machine (5.1). This section follows a session from boot to flight and back: 5.2 to 5.9 are the screens in the order a player meets them (credits, log-disk prompt, main menu, the security prompt the port skips, location and aircraft selection) with the key queue and the menu input rules; 5.10 to 5.14 are the mode byte and the screens that interrupt or end a flight (ESC and SHIFT-ESC, game over, mission end, pause). Section 6 is the start of a flight. The text on every screen is a text record played by the engine of section 3 in the palette regime of section 4.

**The journey at a glance** (original states in `0x`; "port" is what Task 4 builds; every screen has its per-blank table in the sub-section named):

| screen | states | text ids | palette | leaves when | goes to | port |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| credits (5.2) | `0x13596`, `0x135C8` | 0x0F | menu | any key except SHIFT, Amiga, Z, DEL | log-disk check | shown; ESC may quit here (C) |
| log-disk prompt (5.3) | `0x13622`, `0x136EE` | 0x44-0x46, 0x5E | menu | RETURN | log screen or menu | shown by ruling (check result 0 by default: no prompt; 1 to 3 as in 5.3) |
| first tour or log screen and name entry (section 10) | `0x1370A`-`0x13888` | 1, 2, 3 | menu | RETURN | menu | section 10 |
| main menu (5.4) | `0x11D30`, `0x11E04` | 6, 0x64-0x6D | menu | a mode key | echo line | shown |
| echo line (5.4) | `0x1201E` | 0x65-0x69 | menu | countdown 210 or 150 passes | the mode's start (6.1) | shown |
| security prompt (5.5) | `0x1234C`-`0x128DA` | 0x47, 4, 0x43 | flight | a right answer (three tries) | location screen | **skipped** |
| location selection (5.6) | `0x12AC0`-`0x12B74` | 7 | flight | a digit key | aircraft screen (not for the carrier) | shown |
| aircraft selection (5.7) | `0x12C02`, `0x12C36` | 0x4B | flight | key 1 or 2 | mission start | shown |
| mission start (6.1 to 6.4) | `0x12C6E`-`0x12EDA` | 0x59 or 0x5A | flight | arrival or KP-ENTER | the three-tick wait, then flight | shown |
| flight | `0x12EFE` | none | flight | ESC, SHIFT-ESC, crash allowance 0, mission end, P | 5.11 to 5.14 | flight module |
| ESC (5.11) | `0x11A96`-`0x12B74` | 0x65-0x69, 7 | flight, menu, flight | the restart reaches the selection | location or aircraft screen | shown |
| SHIFT-ESC (5.11) | `0x11A70`, `0x11D30` | 6, 0x64 | menu after a cut | the menu list is typed | main menu | shown |
| game over (5.12) | `0x138D8`, `0x139F0`, `0x13A36` | 0x62, 0x63 | menu | hold over | reset, main menu | shown |
| mission-end overlay (5.13) | `0x131C8`, `0x131F4`, `0x13A4C`, `0x13AA8` | 0x1E... (by mode) | flight | text finished | flight resumes, menu, or restart | shown |
| pause (5.14) | `0x13B76`, `0x13BA0` | 0x26 | flight, dimmed | the second P | flight | shown |

### 5.1 The mechanism: state pointer, countdown, gate

Everything outside flight is one state machine. A pointer at `0x1A6BC` (a long, the address of a "state routine" in hunk 0) is called once per pass of the frame routine by the accounting routine `0x11748`; a state routine does its work, optionally stores the address of the next state, and returns. This section gives the mechanism; the screens follow in 5.2 to 5.7 and the flight-time states in 5.10 to 5.14 and section 6. All addresses are hunk 0 unless stated. Tags: **V** verified on the reference emulator (log named in "Verified by"), **R** read from the code only, **C** port convention.

**A pass** [R, V]. The main loop (`0x17EE6`) calls the frame routine `0x11124` and then the display flip `0x1827C`, forever. The frame routine, in order: (1) the key poll `0x11514` (the key queue, 5.8; the mouse-button and gameport reads), (2) the accounting routine `0x11748`, which handles the ESC requests of 5.11, ticks two counters, **decrements the countdown word `V:4AB8E` once**, calls the state routine through `0x1A6BC`, and clears the any-key latch `V:4A85B`, (3) the message generator `0x13C94`, (4) either the flight path (when the gate byte `V:4A84D` is non-zero: view, world update, display lists, draw, the step counter) or a short path (gate 0: `0x14AA0` and one graphics call), (5) last of all the text engine `0x377B2` (section 2 and 3 of this note), which draws the next characters of the text list `V:4A802`. A pass is therefore not a blank, and its length is not constant:

| kind of pass | measured length | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| gate 0, nothing to draw (credits waiting for a key, menu idle) | exactly 6 passes per blank (V:4AB8E falls by 6 in each of 60 consecutive blanks) | V, `flow-s1-passrate`, 60 of 60 blanks |
| gate 0 while the text engine types (menu, log-disk prompt, security prompt) | 0 to 6 passes per blank, mean 2.94 (294 passes in 100 blanks, menu typed out) and 3.05 (122 in 40, log-disk prompt) and 3.0 (96 in 32, security prompt) | V, `flow-s1-boot` blanks 22-62 and 196-296, `flow-s1-menu2` blanks 247-279 |
| gate 1 (the map or world scene is drawn every pass) | 10 to 21 blanks per pass: 19, 21, 10, 11, 10 and 11 blanks in the six passes of one wait, 20, 15, 10, 11, 10, 11 in another | V, `flow-s1-menu2` blanks 54-125 and 169-246 |
| the menu build (state `0x11D30`) | one pass of 33 blanks (a delay loop of 0xC000 iterations, the sound start and the text clear) | V, `flow-s1-boot` blanks 161-194 |
| the log-disk check (`0x18434`, DOS Info on DF0:) | one pass of about 6 blanks | V, `flow-s1-boot` blanks 9-15 and 151-156 |

So every countdown below is stated twice: in passes (what the code stores) and in blanks (what the capture measured, which is what the port converts). The port's conversion is the rule of note 05 17.17: **steps = (blanks x 8 + 25) / 50** with integer division (C); the step column in the tables uses it.

**The countdown** [R, V]. `V:4AB8E` is a signed word. A state that wants to wait stores N in it; the accounting routine decrements it at the start of every later pass, before calling the state; the state acts in the first pass in which the word is negative after its decrement, i.e. in the (N+1)th pass after the store (the pass that stores it does not decrement again). In gate-0 states a countdown of 3 or 5 passes is a fraction of a blank to two blanks; in gate-1 states (the scene is drawn every pass) every pass is 10 to 21 blanks, so a countdown of 4 or 5 is 40 to 100 blanks (see the table above and the per-screen tables below).

**The gate byte `V:4A84D`** [R, V]. 0 for the text screens (credits, log-disk, menu, the confirmation text, the prompt text), 1 while a world scene is drawn (the map behind the security prompt and the location and aircraft screens, the flight). Fade sequences set it to 0 first (the frame is black while the palette moves, 5.10 and section 4).

**The text-finished byte `V:4A898`** [R, V]. Bit 7 set ("0x81") means that the text list has been typed completely and the hold after it has elapsed; 1 means the list is typed and the hold runs (50 passes); 0 while a record is being typed; 2 and 3 are the input modes (name entry, security answer). Most waiting states test "bit 7 of `V:4A898`" or "equals 1". `V:4A87B` is 1 while a record is active.

**The any-key latch `V:4A85B`** [R, V]. Set to 1 by the key handler when a key press (not a release) reaches its tail (5.8), cleared at the end of every pass. A state that waits for "any key" tests it during its own pass. KP-ENTER sets it to 2 on screens where `V:4A965` is 2 (the camera-mode screens, 5.6).

**The front-end states** [R unless marked V; the V rows were entered and left on the emulator]:

| state | role | leaves when | next |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `0x13596` | credits entry (the start-up code `0x11962` stores it with `V:4AB8E` = 3) | countdown negative (V: 13 blanks after the poke in `flow-s1-credits`) | `0x135C8` |
| `0x13534`, `0x1356E` | protection failure: a checksum of three words differs; text id 0x61 for 500 passes, then a loop | never (R) | itself |
| `0x135C8` | credits: waits for any key | `V:4A85B` set | `0x13622` |
| `0x13622`, `0x136EE` | log-disk check and prompt (5.3) | RETURN | menu build or log screen |
| `0x1370A` and followers `0x137EA`, `0x13800`, `0x1381E`, `0x13888` | log screen and name entry (section 10) | | `0x11D30` |
| `0x11A70` | return to the menu: `0x1D1BE` (reset, 5.4), `V:4A964` = `V:4A95E` = `V:4A903` = 0 | at once | `0x11D30` |
| `0x11D30` | menu build (5.4) | at once | `0x11E04` |
| `0x11E04` | menu: waits for a mode (5.4), also handles the screens of items 6 and 8 | `V:4A95E` non-zero | `0x1201E`, `0x122CE`, `0x11F86` |
| `0x1201E` | the confirmation text, then the mode dispatch | countdown (210 or 150 passes) | by mode (5.4, 5.10, section 6) |
| `0x1234C`, `0x12378`, `0x123C2`, `0x123EE` | fade out, fade in, to the prompt (5.5) | countdown, fade level | `0x127C8` |
| `0x127C8`, `0x1287E`, `0x128AA`, `0x128DA` | security prompt (5.5) | answer evaluated | `0x12A50`, `0x12AC0`, `0x12428`, `0x11CC0`, `0x12A2A` |
| `0x12A2A`, `0x12A4E` | failure after three wrong answers (5.5) | never | itself |
| `0x12AC0`, `0x12AFC`, `0x12B74` | camera mode 4 to 5: the location screen (5.6) | location key | `0x12C02` or `0x12C6E` |
| `0x12C02`, `0x12C36` | aircraft screen (5.7) | aircraft key | `0x12C6E` |
| `0x12C6E`, `0x12D58`, `0x12DB8`, `0x12E4E`, `0x12EDA`, `0x12EFE` | mission start and flight (section 6) | | |
| `0x11A96`, `0x11AC4`, `0x11AE2` | ESC: fade out and re-enter the mode (5.11) | | `0x11E04` |

### 5.2 Credits

**What it is.** The first screen after the game has loaded: the title and credit lines typed out (text id 15, in the menu palette `0x29F24`) and a "press any key" line at the end. The gate is 0 and no world scene is drawn (captures `credits.ppm`, note 01a).

**Entry** [R]. The start-up routine `0x11962` (called once from the main program before the world loop) loads the in-flight palette into the colour buffer, clears `V:4A95E`, sets `V:4A965` and `V:4A90F` to 0xFF, stores 3 in the countdown and the state `0x13596`. (The same routine stores the protection-failure state `0x13534` instead when three words of working storage differ from their constants; that path types a failure line, id 0x61, and loops: section 11.)

**Sequence** [V, `flow-s1-credits`, `flow-s1-credits-long`; two runs each]:

| event | blank | steps | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| state `0x13596` entered with the countdown 3 (here by a poke at blank 1) | 0 | 0 | |
| state `0x135C8` entered, menu palette loaded (the colour buffer is copied from `0x29F24`, `V:4A95C` = 2 reloads it for three blanks), text id 15 starts | 13 and 15 (two runs) | 2 | V, 2 runs |
| text list typed completely (`V:4A898` = 1) | 245 after the start of the text | 39 | V, 1 run |
| hold of 50 passes ends (`V:4A898` = 0x81): its own stage of 9 blanks | 254 after the start of the text | 1 (typing 39 + hold 1 = 40 in all) | V, 1 run |

The screen then waits indefinitely; there is no timeout and no attract mode on this screen (V: in `flow-s1-credits-long` the state stayed `0x135C8` for the 150 blanks after the text; R: the state routine has no timer). The typed-out rule (characters per pass) is section 3's; the blanks above are measured and include the mean 2.9 to 3.0 passes per blank of 5.1.

**Leaving** [V, `flow-s1-anykey`, 9 keys, one run each; `flow-s1-boot`]. State `0x135C8` waits for the any-key latch (5.1). Which keys set it: every key press that reaches the tail of the key handler (5.8): F1, TAB, CTRL, ESC and the raw code 0x68 did (V); **Z, DEL, left SHIFT and the left Amiga key did not** (V); by the code the right SHIFT key and the right Amiga key behave like their left twins and every other key reaches the tail (R). A key release never sets it (R). A mouse click does not (V, `flow-s1-mouse`: the state stayed `0x135C8` 1.5 s after a left click, then left on SPACE). ESC counts as any key and additionally leaves the request `V:4A8E2` = 1 pending, which the menu consumes without effect (5.4); the port's "ESC quits on the credits screen" is therefore a port convention (C), not the original's behaviour.

On the key, in the same pass [R, V]: the audio master-volume target `V:4A720` is set to 0x1F0000 (the volume ramp `0x294AC` follows it, 0x4000 per pass), the typing-sound byte `V:4A88F` to 1, the in-flight palette (step 0 of `0x29FA4`) is loaded (colours register values read back one blank later: sky, sea and land colours; the screen is empty), the text list and the plane text area are cleared, `V:4A88B` is set to 1, the countdown to 5, `V:4A95E` to 0, and the log-disk check `0x18434` runs (one pass of about 6 blanks: DOS Info on DF0:); the next state is `0x13622` (5.3). Blank offsets from the blank in which `V:4A85B` first reads 1 (call it L): palette change L+1, state `0x13622` L+6 (1 step), first text of the next screen L+12 to L+14 (2 steps).

**Port rule (C).** Credits show text id 15 typed out; any key except SHIFT, ALT, Amiga (command) keys, Z and DEL (the last two are ordinary letters in the port's key map: the original reinstalls its vertical-blank server on them, 5.8) goes on. The port needs no countdown for this screen.

### 5.3 Log-disk prompt

**What it is.** The original asks for a log disk: its flight log (the 78-byte player record, section 10) is kept on the game disk itself, so the game checks DF0: after the credits and, when the disk cannot take a log, shows an explanation and waits for RETURN. The check (`0x18434`, calling `0x11058`) asks DOS for the disk information of DF0: and classifies it [R]: no disk present (result 1), a disk without a DOS structure (2), a disk that is write protected (3), or a usable disk (0). Result 0 skips the prompt (state `0x13622` goes straight to the log screen `0x1370A`, section 10); 1, 2 and 3 show the prompt. The emulator's disk is write protected, so result 3 is the captured case; results 1 and 2 were produced by patching the call (V).

**Ruling (C, perfect replica): the port shows this screen.** The screen is the one the original shows for the check result of the player's drive; the port's log is a local file and always works, so the port's check result is the one for a write-enabled game disk, which is result 0: the original then shows **no prompt** and goes straight to the log screen. The core therefore keeps the screen module's log-disk states and texts (ids 0x44-0x46 and 0x5E, the table below) and selects them by a check result supplied by the frontend, 0 by default (usable log: no prompt); results 1 to 3 are reproduced if the frontend reports an unwritable log location (C). (Superseded text of the earlier version: "The port skips this screen".) Spec section 10's local file is unchanged. After the credits the port goes to the log screen or name entry of section 10 when a log needs one, else to the menu (5.4); nothing in the port reads a disk.

**Screen** [V, `flow-s1-boot`, `flow-s1-logdisk1`, `flow-s1-logdisk2`, one run each; captures `log-disk-prompt.ppm`, `extracted/captures/flow/logdisk-variant1.ppm`, `logdisk-variant2.ppm`]: the menu palette, grey text (pen 2 of the menu palette, colour byte 0x23 in all three one-line records), two text records: the first names the problem, the second (id 0x5E) is the five-line explanation of what to do, ending with the "press RETURN" lines:

| check result | first record id | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 0x44 | no disk in DF0: |
| 2 | 0x45 | disk not initialised |
| 3 | 0x46 | disk not write enabled |

The list in `V:4A802` is therefore (id, 0x5E, 0), typed as ordinary instant lines (flags bit 0 clear in these records, section 2).

**Per-blank table** (from the blank L in which the any-key latch of the credits is set; the steps column is (blanks x 8 + 25) / 50):

| event | result 3 | result 1 | result 2 | steps (result 3) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| state `0x13622` entered (the check, one pass) | L+6 | L+6 | L+6 | 1 |
| palette: in-flight step 0 from L+1, menu palette from | L+13 | L+13 | L+13 | 2 |
| state `0x136EE`, first record starts | L+12 | L+12 | L+12 | 2 |
| second record (id 0x5E) starts | L+20 | L+24 | L+20 | 3 / 4 |
| list typed (`V:4A898` = 1) | L+44 | L+49 | L+44 | 7 / 8 |
| hold over (`V:4A898` = 0x81) | L+53 | L+58 | L+53 | 8 / 9 |

(Result 1: 12 blanks for the first record because it is longer, 25 for the second; the measured passes per blank on this screen are 3.05.) The prompt is complete about 53 blanks (8 steps) after the key.

**Keys** [V, `flow-s1-logdisk-keys`, one run per row; R for the dispatcher paths]. While `V:4A88B` is 1 the key handler uses its special block (5.8):

| key | effect |
| --- | --- |
| RETURN (0x44) | clears `V:4A88B`; accepted at any moment, also while the text is still being typed (R: the byte is tested only by the state routine); state `0x136EE` sees it and returns to `0x13622`, which repeats the check (one pass of about 6 blanks) and, the disk being unchanged, goes to the menu build `0x11D30` |
| ESC | sets the request `V:4A8E2` = 1 and does nothing else (V: pending value read, state unchanged); the menu consumes it without effect (5.4) |
| SHIFT then ESC | sets `V:4A8E2` = 0xFF (V); the same |
| any other key | stored in the typed-key ring (5.8) and otherwise ignored (V for the letter A, which RETURN afterwards followed normally) |

Timeline of RETURN (L' = the blank in which `V:4A85B` first reads 1): state `0x13622` at L'+4 or L'+5, the menu build `0x11D30` at L'+9 or L'+10, the menu's first text at L'+42 or L'+43 (the build is one pass of 33 blanks, 5.1): 7 steps from the key to the first menu text. With a usable disk there is no prompt: the first check of the credits key returns 0 and `0x13622` goes straight to the log screen.

**A key pressed, or held, across the change** [V, `flow-s1-logdisk-keys`, `flow-s1-keyhold`]: a RETURN held for 1 s produced exactly one change (the menu build at L'+9) and nothing more when the release arrived 50 blanks later (120 blanks logged, state unchanged); a digit key pressed during the 33-blank menu build (2, 17 blanks after the RETURN) is not lost: the keyboard queue holds it (5.8) and the first pass of the menu state takes it (mode 1 stored one blank after `0x11E04` first ran, the confirmation text 6 blanks after that).

### 5.4 Main menu

**What it is.** The main menu: the title (text id 6), the header line (id 0x64) and eight numbered lines (ids 0x65 to 0x6C) in four colours, and two grey lines about ESC and SHIFT-ESC (id 0x6D), all typed out on a black screen in the menu palette `0x29F24`.
