SHIFT-ESC would go to the menu (`0x11A70`) in the same way as from flight.

**Who writes the engine and countdown bytes on this path** [R]: `V:4A898` is written only by the text engine here (0 at the record start, 1 at the end of the list, bit 7 set when the 50-pass hold has run) and by the reset (`0x13462`: 0; `0x1D1BE`: 0xFF); the states only read it. `V:4AB8E` is decremented once per pass by the dispatcher (`0x11942`) and stored by `0x13954` (5) and by `0x1D1BE` (5); nothing reads it after `0x139F0`. The gate `V:4A84D` is cleared by `0x13962` and by `0x1D1BE`; nothing sets it before the menu.

**Blanks per pass.** The 3 to 6 passes per blank of every text-only screen show in the countdown (it falls by 3 per blank at k = 11 to 14, then 5 to 6 per blank): the typed text is paced by passes, not blanks, so its duration is a property of the text records (section 3) at about 5.3 passes per blank here. The end hold of 50 passes lasted 8 blanks.

**Port rules (C), stages per the 3.8 contract.** **Reference point: the restart pass A of note 05 17.17, k = 6 of the table (the allowance goes 1 to 0), the step in which the port's crash flow reaches the game-over path; k = 0 is only the sample where `V:4A951` reads 3.** From k = 6: the clear screen and the wait for the gate and the text start (k 6 to 12) 6 blanks = 1 step; the text typed in 78 blanks (k 12 to 90; 23 + 38 characters with delays of 3 and 8 passes) = 12 steps; the end hold 8 blanks = 1; the reset 8 blanks = 1; the menu set-up 33 blanks = 5. The stages sum to **20 steps** (133 blanks; 21 if counted from k = 0, which adds the 6 blanks of the hold's last iterations = 1 step); the leave is at k = 98 of the table, k0 + 92 of note 05, after which the menu list starts. The text pens come from the menu palette (the screen is not faded: the palette is switched, level stays 15). The core (`screen_step`) counts these as steps from the crash hand-over; the typed rate is the engine's (section 3), not a separate constant.

### 5.13 Mission end and debrief

**Scope.** What the front end does when a mission ends: the request and code that the mission scripts store, the states that show the outcome text, and where the flow goes. The scripts themselves (the success tests, the kill counts, which event gives which code) are the mission slice (milestone 5); the codes, the states and the screens are settled here. **The captures of this section are staged**: the free-flight snapshot has the mode byte and the request pokes below written into it (the same stores as the scripts' code at `0x28AC6`-`0x28AE4` and `0x27C58`), because no mission can be flown to its end by the harness; the states, ids and timings of the front end are the original's, the choice of text is what the staged mode selects. Palette and background: **the flight palette (fade step 0, level 15) stays loaded and the frozen cockpit picture stays on screen; the outcome text is typed over it** [V: colour registers constant at the flight palette in all three staged runs, frames `end-m3-success-*`, `end-m4-failure-*`, `end-m9-success-*`]. A text pen therefore means the flight palette's entry (pen 9 amber, section 4); the menu palette is not loaded for these screens.

**The mission-end code `V:4A850` and the request `V:4A8E2`** [R unless marked]:

| code | stored by | meaning | `0x131F4` builds (ids) | what follows |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0xFF | the success branches of the scripts (`0x28AC6` and its siblings, section 12.2) | success | modes 3 to 8: word 4 of the mission's id list (the outcome id: the base a + 4, section 2.1), then 0x8055 (mode 3: return to base) or 0x8056 (modes 4 to 8: return to the carrier); mode 9: the player record's first word is set to 1, the log is written (`0x1858A`), ids 0x4A and 0x8053, `V:4A8E3` = 1, code becomes 0xEF; mode 0x7D: id 0x48, code becomes 0xF0; modes 1 and 2: id 0x41 (a blank line) | after the text: 1 (flight resumes) except as stated |
| 0xFE | the failure branches of the scripts of modes 4 to 7 and 0x7D (12.2) | failure | mode 4: id 0x24 when bit 0 of the step counter `V:4A992` is set, else 0x25; mode 5: 0x2C and code 0xF0; mode 6: 0x33; mode 7: 0x39, then 0x803A (counter bit 0 set) and code 0xF0, or 0x805C; other modes: 0x42; then, unless the code is 0xF0, the words 0x8056 and 0x8053 are appended **one word too far** (see below) | after the text: 2 |
| 0xFD | the script of mode 4 when `B20` bit 0x80 of the destroyed Air Force One is set (12.6) | special failure of mode 4 (the player destroyed the aircraft himself) | id 0x4C, code becomes 0xF0 | `0x13A4C` |
| 0xFC | the tail of the dispatcher (`0x28B04`) when the code is 0xFF or 1 and the landing test `0x28B12` passes (12.2) | mission complete | ids 0x54 and 0x8053, the log update `0x134A0` and the log write `0x1858A` | after the text: 4 |
| 0xF0 | `0x131F4`, `0x118BC` (bail-out) | final text, then the menu | - | `0x13A4C` (below) |
| 0xEF | `0x131F4` (mode 9) | qualification result text, then a retry | - | `0x13AA8` (below) |
| 1, 2, 4 | `0x1301E` (from 0xFF, 0xFE, 0xFC), `0x16FC2`, `0x1D938`, `0x2A8D2` | the outcome has been shown; the scripts continue | - | - |
| 0 | start, `0x1F144` (refuel) | none | - | - |

The request values: 1 and 0xFF are the player's ESC and SHIFT-ESC (5.11); **2** is stored by the entity code (`0x27C58`) when a record of class 0x30 (`+0x62`; the code says no more, note 05 17.10 has the ejection seat's classes) has run out its counter `+0x4C`, with `V:4A8E4` = 4; **3** is stored by the mission scripts (`0x28AD4`, the shared end `0x28ACE`) together with a code in `V:4A850`, the delay `V:4A978` (the value of D0 at the end; 12.2 lists the values, 0, 4, 7 and the quirk of the mode 8 script), `V:4A8E4` = 4 and `V:4A965` = 1. The scripts, the triggers and the rule that the code arrives once are section 12; the log updates of the 0xFC debrief are 14.2. The dispatcher counts `V:4A8E4` down once per pass (`0x11922`); a request 2 or 3 is acted on when it is below 0: the store pass reads 4 and the next five passes 3, 2, 1, 0, -1, so **6 passes after the store** the state changes (V: F2, F1 and F3 captures, 12.12; note 07 6.5).

### The outcome screen (request 3)

[V: staged runs of mode 3 (code 0xFF), mode 4 (code 0xFE), mode 9 (code 0xFF); `flow-s2-end-m3-success.txt`, `-m4-failure.txt`, `-m9-success.txt`; k counts blanks from the first step after the pokes; 1 run each]

| k (modes 3 / 4 / 9) | state | gate | pause byte | text | event |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | `0x12EFE` | 1 | 0 | none | the request 3 and the code are in place; the cockpit is flying |
| 0 to 28 / 27 / 28 | `0x131C8` | 1 | 0 to 1 | none | the dispatcher takes the request 6 passes later (the store pass reads `V:4A8E4` = 4, then 3, 2, 1, 0, -1) (`V:4A8E2` = 0, `V:4AB8E` = `V:4A978`, here 0): `0x131C8` runs a pass that sets `V:4A866` = 1 (**freeze**), the countdown 2 and `V:4A965` = 1 (**flight keys locked**: only the restricted key set of 6.4 acts; ESC is still accepted) |
| 29 / 28 / 29 | `0x131F4` | 1 | 1 | none | the state waits its countdown (2 passes), then builds the list for the code (the table above), clears the gate and stores `0x12EFE`; the engine is reset (`0x13462`) |
| 42 / 41 / 43 | `0x12EFE` (mode 9: `0x13AA8` from k = 44) | **0** | 1 | the list | the text is typed over the frozen cockpit: mode 3: ids 0x1E and 0x8055 until k = 184 (**143 blanks**); mode 4: id 0x25 (the counter's bit 0 was clear) until k = 220 (180 blanks); mode 9: ids 0x4A and 0x8053 until k = 281 (**238 blanks**) |
| 185 / 221 | `0x12EFE` | 1 | 0 | none | modes 3 and 4: the text has finished (the engine byte is nonzero and the list active: `0x1301E`): the code becomes 1 (FF) or 2 (FE), the gate opens, the engine is reset, `V:4A965` = 0, `V:4A866` = 0, `V:4A968` = 0xFE, a display-list request, and, if the head was turned, the view is reset: **the flight resumes at once, no end hold**; the cockpit picture is redrawn over the text |
| 282 / 290 | `0x13AA8` | 0 | 1 | none | mode 9: text done (engine 1 at k = 282, 0x81 at k = 290, the 50-pass hold of 8 blanks): the simulation bits `V:4A984` 0x40 and 0x6000 are cleared, countdown 100, state `0x13B24` |
| 290 to 307 | `0x13B24` | 0 | 1 | none | mode 9: the countdown of 100 passes (**17 blanks**), then the restart: `V:4A8E2` = 0, code 0, `V:4A8E3` = 1, `V:4A866` = 1, countdown 3, gate 0, target 0, state `0x11A96`: **the ESC restart of 5.11 starts by itself** (k = 307 to 329 in the log: the fade out) |

**The pause byte through the outcome of a mission, one more run (milestone 7, Task 2; the follow-up item "the pause byte is not 1 there as in the original" closed)** [V: `flow7-pause-outcome.txt`, F6 from its first flight pass, `V:4A963` poked to 8 (the kill count) so that the script succeeds, every blank for 420 blanks, 1 run]. The byte was 0, then **1 for the one frozen pass of the script (blank 2 after the poke)**, 0 from the pass that stored the code 0xFF, the request 3 and the countdown 4 (blank 7; the byte stayed 0 through the countdown's passes 4, 3, 2, 1, 0, -1 at blanks 7, 13, 19, 25, 31 and 38), then **1 again from the freeze state `0x131C8` (the state pointer read `0x131F4` with the byte 1 and the countdown 2 at blank 43) through the whole of the typed outcome text** (the flight state `0x12EFE` with the gate 0 and the engine byte 0 from blank 103 to the end of the text), and **0 at the resume (blank 179): the code 1, the gate 1, the engine byte reset, `V:4A965` 0 and `V:4A968` = 0xFE** (the flight pass that follows consumes the 0xFE and writes 0 at blank 185). This is the table above (the byte 1 from the freeze to the resume) in a second mode and on a second route (F6, the default script, with no lock held). **The port's outcome screens do not set the byte** (a probe of the F6 success: the byte is 0 in every step from the request to the resume, and `V:4A968` is 0 at the resume): the port deviates in the byte, not in the timing. **Port rule (C): the byte is set to 1 when the freeze state is entered (the step the screen enters `SCR_END_WAIT`'s freeze), stays 1 through `SCR_END_TEXT` and the hold, and is cleared with `V:4A968` = 0xFE at the resume; the P and view-key tests of 5.14 and 6.4 then see the byte as the original does.** **Port rule (C), the resume is not a flight pass (milestone 7, final round; the Task 5b report's open question 2 closed):** the resume pass (blank 179) writes the 0xFE and the next flight pass, the first of the resumed flight, consumes it at blank 185; 6 blanks are 1 step by (6 x 8 + 25) / 50, and the flight-state passes count one per step (3.8 exception 4), so **the step of the resume runs no flight pass and the first one is the step after** (`game.c` `resume_skip`: the world, the lists and the sound routine of that pass, and with them the pickup of the 0xFE, move one step later after every 0xFF, 0xFE and 0xFC outcome; the training part 2's lost lead of 8.9 freezes for 14 blanks = 2 steps and the resume step: 3 steps without a flight pass). Pinned by `tests/test_missions.c` (the F6 success: the byte is still 0xFE after the resume step and taken by the next) and `tests/test_training.c`; the six mission goldens were regenerated for it.

**A quirk of the failure list** [R from `0x133E2`-`0x133F8`; V: the first two list words of mode 4 read `0x0025`, `0x0000`]: the code that appends the "return" ids writes them **two words after** the last stored id, leaving one word in between that the reset block cleared to 0; the engine stops at the first 0 word, so for failures of modes 4 and 6 (code 0xFE, no 0xF0) the words 0x8056 and 0x8053 are never played. The port plays the list up to the first zero word.

### The final screens: 0xF0 and 0xEF

- **0xF0 (`0x13A4C`)**, entered from the flight state at the start of a pass in which the code is 0xF0 (`0x1313E`): waits for the engine byte negative (text typed and held), clears the simulation bits (`V:4A984` 0x40 and 0x6000), countdown 100, state `0x13A84`: when the countdown is out: `V:4A8E2` = 0, `V:4A850` = 0, state `0x11A70`: **the menu** (5.4) with the countercode flag cleared. About 8 blanks of hold plus 100 passes (17 blanks) after the text.
- **0xEF (`0x13AA8`)**, the qualification result: the same wait and then `0x13B24` (the restart of the qualification, above). During the wait SHIFT-ESC (request 0xFF) sends the state to `0x11A70` (the menu) and ESC (request 1) skips the countdown (it stores -1 and goes to `0x13B24` at once). The text's last line is the SHIFT-ESC hint (id 0x8053).
- **Bail-out landing (request 2, `0x1319C`)**: the dispatcher sets the code 0xF0, `V:4A965` = 1, `V:4A866` = 1, countdown 6 and state `0x1319C`; that state (when the countdown is out) sets the gate 0, stores id 0x0E (the bail-out-survived text) and `V:4A898` = 0 and stores `0x13A4C`; so the flight ends with that text and the menu [V: staged, `flow-s2-end-bailout.txt`, 1 run, k from the pokes: the dispatcher takes the request 2 and the state is `0x1319C` at k = 29 (code 0xF0, `V:4A965` = 1, `V:4A866` = 1), `0x13A4C` at k = 56 with id 0x0E typed over the frozen cockpit until k = 131 (75 blanks), the end hold ends at k = 140 (`0x13A84`), the countdown of 100 passes at k = 157 (17 blanks), `0x11A70` at k = 157, `0x11D30` at k = 165, the menu list at k = 199; the flight palette stays until the menu palette loads at k = 200].

**While the outcome screen runs.** The flight keys are locked by `V:4A965` = 1 until the flight resumes (modes 3 to 8) or the restart begins (mode 9) (the restricted key set of 6.4 still acts, ESC and SHIFT-ESC included); the world is frozen by `V:4A866` = 1, the fade level stays 15 and the flight palette stays [V]. **SHIFT-ESC** in the 0xEF state [V: `flow-s2-end-m9-shiftesc.txt`, SHIFT-ESC 120 blanks into the staged qualification result, k from the key]: `0x13AA8` sees the request at k = 0, `0x11A70` at k = 1 (the reset: text cut off, code 0), `0x11D30` at k = 11, the menu list from k = 45 to 142, hold over at k = 150: the menu in 45 blanks (7 steps). The dispatcher takes ESC and SHIFT-ESC only for a non-negative code (5.11); with a code set the flight state or the final-screen state takes them (`0x13118`: engine byte 0xFF and state `0x13A4C`: the menu after the 100 passes; `0x13AA8` as above) [R, except where named]. ESC (request 1) in `0x13AA8` skips the countdown and restarts the qualification at once [R].

**Pen 0 outcome texts** (the three records of mission 5, ids 0x39, 0x3A and 0x5C, colour byte 0x07 = pen 0) [V: staged mode 7, code 0xFE, step counter poked to an even value, `flow-s2-end-m7-a.txt`, frames `end-m7a-60`, `-120`, `-160`; 1 run; the list was 0x39 and 0x805C, the odd value gives 0x39 and 0x803A, not captured]: the flight palette is live (colour registers constant, entry 0 = black), the frozen cockpit picture is the background, and the glyphs are drawn **in pen 0, black, over the blue sky of the cockpit picture**: the engine writes the pen's bits into all four planes at the glyph pixels (section 3), so pen 0 clears them to colour 0. The text is readable because it lies on the sky at the top of the picture (the records start at scanline 6, 11 scanlines per line) and would be invisible on a black background; no flash palette and no other palette is involved. The three lines were typed one under the other from k = 41 (the whole text 0x39, 0x805C is 3 lines).

**Port rules (C), steps by (blanks x 8 + 25) / 50.** The outcome screen is an overlay on the frozen last picture, in the flight palette; Task 4 sets the freeze 6 flight passes after the request (the countdown 4, 3, 2, 1, 0, -1 of the store pass and the five after it): by the 3.8 contract's exception 4 those passes count one per step, so the freeze comes **6 steps** after the request (the table's k = 28 blanks would convert to 5; the pass rule is the one to use), shows the list typed at the engine's rate (143 blanks = 23 steps for the F1 success, 180 blanks = 29 steps for the mode 4 failure, 238 blanks = 38 steps for the mode 9 result), and then, for the codes that resume the flight (0xFF, 0xFE), unfreezes in the step in which the typing ends, without an end hold; for 0xF0 holds 8 blanks (1 step) plus 100 passes (17 blanks = 3 steps) and goes to the menu through the reset (5.12 table: 8 + 33 blanks); for 0xEF the same wait and then the ESC restart of 5.11. The mission scripts (milestone 5) set the code and the request.

### 5.14 Pause text

**What it is.** The pause screen is not a state of its own screen: it is the frozen flight picture, dimmed by a fade to level 10, with one typed text (id 0x26, the pause label in pen 9) at the top centre. The pause key is P (raw `0x19`, handler `0x1ED9A`, reached when the camera-mode byte `V:4A966` is not negative). Pause is entered from the flight state `0x12EFE` and from the camera-approach state `0x12D58` (both test `V:4A866` negative); the mission-flow states of the menu, selections and briefings have no pause (the key is ignored while `V:4A966` is negative, and `V:4A966` is `0xFF` between a reset and the first camera mode).

**The byte.** `V:4A866` is the pause byte: 0 running; `0xFF` paused (set by the key); 1 "frozen by a flow state" (set by the ESC request, the mission-end screens and the game-over path, section 5.11 to 5.13: the same freeze, no pause screen). A nonzero value stops the step counter `V:4A992`, the player step and the sky and ground passes (note 06 item 8 lists what still runs); only the negative value starts the pause screen. The key toggles: if `V:4A866` is nonzero it writes `V:4A968` = `0xFE` and clears the byte (resume), else it stops the four sound channels (`0x115F6`), when the recorder is idle writes four `0xFF` bytes at the recorder pointer (Part B, the demo stream's pause marker) and sets `V:4A866` = `0xFF` [R].

**Per-blank table** (script `flow-s2-pause.txt`, log `flow-s2-pause.txt`, free flight, airborne snapshot; k counts blanks after the key command returns; the key reaches the handler about 3 blanks later) [V, 1 run each for entry and exit; ESC from the pause: `flow-s2-esc-pause.txt`]:

| k | event | state | gate `V:4A84D` | fade level / target | text | engine `V:4A898` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 to 3 | running | `0x12EFE` | 1 | 15 / 15 | none | 0x81 |
| 4 | the key was polled: `V:4A866` = 0xFF; the flight state sees it in its next pass: the countdown `V:4AB8E` is set to 2, the target `V:4A959` to 10, the clock is read, the pause state `0x13B76` stored | `0x13B76` | 1 | 15 / 10 | none | 0x81 |
| 8 | the gate falls (the pass in which the state runs with the gate clear) | `0x13B76` | 0 | 14 / 10 | none | 0x81 |
| 7 to 15 | fade down one level per 2 blanks (first step 3 blanks after the target changed), 15 to 10 | `0x13B76` | 0 | 14, 13, 12, 11, 10 at k = 7, 9, 11, 13, 15 | none | 0x81 |
| 16 | level equals target: the text id 0x26 is stored, state `0x13BA0` | `0x13BA0` | 0 | 10 / 10 | 0x26 | 0 |
| 16 to 40 | the text is typed (10 characters, delay 6 passes per character, then the line delay) | `0x13BA0` | 0 | 10 / 10 | 0x26 until the list is consumed at k = 40 | 0 |
| 40 | list consumed: engine byte 1, the 50-pass end hold begins | `0x13BA0` | 0 | 10 / 10 | none (the pixels stay) | 1 |
| 49 | end hold over: 0x81 | `0x13BA0` | 0 | 10 / 10 | none | 0x81 |
| 49 on | waits for the second P (V:4A866 negative = stay) | `0x13BA0` | 0 | 10 / 10 | none | 0x81 |
| 101 | second P (pressed at k = 100 of the log): `V:4A866` = 0, `V:4A968` = 0xFE; the state sets gate 1, resets the text engine (`0x13462`), target 15 and returns to `0x12EFE` (or to `0x12D58` when `V:4A966` is 6) | `0x12EFE` | 1 | 10 / 15 | none | 0 |
| 103 to 116 | fade up one level per 3 blanks, 10 to 15 | `0x12EFE` | 1 | 11 at 103, 12 at 106, 13 at 109, 14 at 112, 15 at 116 | none | 0 |
| 106 | the step counter turns again (first flight pass after the resume; the pass in flight is 5 to 8 blanks at this scene) | `0x12EFE` | 1 | 12 / 15 | none | 0 |

The time spent paused is removed from the mission clock: entering stores the clock reading in `V:4A9BC` (`0x13006`, `0x12FF8`...), leaving adds the difference to the paused-time accumulators `V:4A9C0` and `V:4A9C4` (`0x13BD6` to `0x13C0A`) [R; the clock is section 10].

**What the screen shows** [V, frames `pause-10`, `pause-30`, `pause-60` under `extracted/captures/flow`]: the last flight picture at fade level 10 (every flight colour darkened: sky entry 5 shows `0x002`, the instrument faces nearly black), the text in orange-red at about (x 128, y 43), nothing else redrawn (the gate is 0, so no world pass runs and the cockpit is not redrawn; the text engine writes into the same planes). The palette at level 10 is step 5 of the fade table (section 4).

**Pass rate.** In the pause the main loop runs 4 to 6 passes per blank (the countdown falls by 4 to 6 per blank, same log), because no world pass runs; the text engine and the fade counter are therefore paced per pass, and the typed text takes 24 blanks.

**The pause line's clicks, placed (milestone 7, Task 2; the question of the port's 8 against the capture's 9)** [V: the per-write trace `sound7-pause.txt` (`audtrace`, P from the air, 1 run; the same offsets in the second capture of note 10 7.3), the frame numbers counted from the first logged frame, the stop of the four voices at frame 15; the line is the record 0x26, one typed line, flags 3 (spaces skipped), pen 9, D = 6, ten characters of which **eight are not blank**, the delay byte 10]. The tone starts of voice 3 (the write of period 300 and then of volume 2) fall at frames **28, 31, 33, 35, 37, 39, 41, 43 and 45**, that is **+13, +16, +18, +20, +22, +24, +26, +28 and +30 blanks after the stop** (note 10 7.3 reads "+13, +15, ... +29": the second start is 3 blanks after the first, not 2, and the rest follow at 2), and a tenth start follows at frame 49 (+34). **The nine are the model of 3.6 exactly**: the first character of a record is drawn on three passes and requests a click on the first two of them (3.4 rule 5), the other seven non-blank characters one each at the pass 1 + j x D (j = 1 to 7, six passes apart); the pause screen runs about three passes a blank in its first blanks (3.8), so the six passes between two characters are two blanks, **and the first two passes are 3 blanks apart, not in one blank** (the first pass of the pause screen is longer than the later ones; what makes it long is not traced): the first character's two requests are two tone starts, +13 and +16, and the seven others follow at +18 to +30. The port's eight starts at +13 to +27 put both of the first character's requests in blank 13 (one audible start) and are two blanks early from the third on. **The tenth start (+34, frame 49) is not a character**: it falls one pass-pair after the line's last character (pass 43) at the line's end (pass 49 = 1 + 8 x 6) and is the only start that 3.6's rule does not list; its cause is not settled (R: the line-end handling of 3.4 rule 7 requests no click for a typed line; a second record or an extra instant line does not exist, the record has one line), and it is the click that the port lacks last. (Milestone 7 code round: the port now makes the nine starts at +13, +16, +18 to +30 in every phase of the step, the second pass of the pause screen being 3 blanks long, `tests/test_screen.c`, `sound7_check.py pause` 9 of 9; the tenth start stays a NOTE-GAP in `screen.c`, no test pins it.) **Port rule (C): the pause line makes nine click requests in blanks +13, +16, +18, +20, +22, +24, +26, +28 and +30 after the stop of the voices (the first two from the first character, 3 blanks apart); the capture's tenth start at +34 is a NOTE-GAP (its cause is not settled), the port makes none and no test pins its absence; the stop is in the key's step.**

**Port rules (C), steps by (blanks x 8 + 25) / 50:** the pause is entered at the step after the key; the fade from 15 to 10 takes 11 blanks = 2 steps (a fade step is 2 blanks down, 3 up, state in section 4); the text appears when the fade ends and is typed in 24 blanks = 4 steps, after which the engine's end hold (50 passes, 8 to 9 blanks = 1 step) has no visible effect; the resume sets the target to 15 and the picture returns over 15 blanks = 2 steps while the flight steps again at once (the step counter turns 5 blanks after the key). Keys other than P, ESC and the view and head keys do what they do in flight (note 06 item 8); ESC from the pause: section 5.11. The pause text is a text record like any other (id 0x26, section 2): the core's `screen_fill` sets it with `shown` advancing at the typed rate, and the frontend dims through the fade target 10.

**P on the other screens and the passes at 0xFF** [V: `followup-pause-screens.txt`, three runs, one each; the pass counter `V:4A879` and the pause byte per blank]. (1) **Flight, byte at 0**: the key press is polled at the start of a pass (4 blanks after it was sent, here), the key handler sets `V:4A866` to `0xFF`, and the flight state, which runs later in the **same pass**, takes the byte at once (countdown 2, target 10, state `0x13B76`): **exactly one pass runs with the byte at 0xFF before the pause screen** (the pass counter 98 to 99, a 4-blank pass in which the gate is still 1 and the step counter does not turn; the pass counter then jumps by 4 in the first blank of gate 0 and runs 4 to 6 passes per blank). The "one or two" of 12.11 is one, the pass of the key poll: the poll comes first in the frame routine's order, so the state routine of that pass always sees the byte. **Port rule (C): the pause is entered in the step in which P arrives; that step's world update is a paused one (no step counter, no player step).** (2) **The outcome screens** (staged: mode 3, code 0xFF, request 3, `V:4A965` = 1 as the real scripts store it; the pause byte is 1 while the outcome text is typed): P pressed 70 and 110 blanks after the staging changed **nothing**: `V:4A866` stayed 1, `V:4A968` was not written, no pause screen; the restricted key set for `V:4A965` non-zero (6.4) does not contain P. **Port rule (C): P is ignored in the outcome screens (the byte stays 1); only ESC and SHIFT-ESC are taken.** (3) **The take-off notice** (free flight, runway start, state `0x12E4E`, gate 0): P is accepted at once (two blanks: the passes of a gate-0 state are fast) and sets the byte to `0xFF`; **no pause screen opens in the notice** (the state is neither the flight state nor the approach state); the notice is typed and held at the unpaused rate (text consumed at blank 26, the end hold over at 34) and the wait state `0x12EDA` follows with the byte still `0xFF` and the gate 1 for its 33 blanks, as unpaused. A second P, sent 58 blanks after the first, cleared the byte (`V:4A968` = `0xFE`) before the flight state ran, so the flight started normally. With no second P (`followup-pause-notice.txt`, 1 run) the byte stays `0xFF` through the notice and the wait state, the flight state `0x12EFE` runs its first pass at blank 66 and **the pause screen opens at its next pass** (state `0x13B76` at blank 75, the fade 15 to 10 and the pause text typed as in 5.14): **P pressed in the notice screen opens the pause screen as soon as the flight state runs (9 blanks, one pass, after the flight starts)**. **Port rule (C): `GAME_BTN_PAUSE` in the notice screen sets the pause byte; the pause screen opens at the first flight pass; a second P before that clears the byte.**

## 6. Mission start and end

The start of a flight and its end by the mission code: the ordered initialisation list (6.1), the wait before the first simulated step (6.2), the crash allowance by mode (6.3) and the camera modes that frame the start (6.4). The end of a flight (ESC, game over, mission end) is in 5.11 to 5.13. Items for the placement tables (`tables/scenario.bin`) are settled in 6.1 where the extractor asked for them.

### 6.1 Mission start initialisation list

Mission start is not one routine: it is a chain of three blocks, and a core must run them in this order. The first two are the same for every mode; the third is the player's start (note 05 section 15). Everything below is [R] unless a capture is named; the order of the states is [V] from `flow-s2-esc-flight-dense.txt`, `flow-s2-mission-start.txt`, `flow-s2-mission-start-long.txt` and `flow-s2-mission-start-skip.txt`.

### The reset block `0x1D1BE` (called at start-up `0x11968`, by the reset-to-menu state `0x11A70` and by the ESC state `0x11AE2`)

In this order:

1. **Clear the flag block**: the bytes `V:4A848` to `V:4A87C` are zeroed (among them the gate `V:4A84D`, the mission-end code `V:4A850`, the HUD switch `V:4A859` (note 06: the H key toggles it; the reset turns the HUD off; the state `0x12EDA` of the table below turns it on again, the only store of the constant 1: `0x12EE6`; note 06 section on the HUD switch; **port rule: the switch is cleared by the reset block and set to 1 only in the state `0x12EDA` at `0x12EE6`, once the countdown has gone below 0, not at the flight start**, which the milestone-5 port does with `out_hud`; the debug `--flight` start sets it at once), the key latch `V:4A85B`, the pause byte `V:4A866`, the flags `V:4A86C` to `V:4A86E`, the text-running flag `V:4A87B`) and the words `V:4A978` to `V:4A9DF` (the mission-end delay `V:4A978`, the flag words `V:4A980` to `V:4A98E`, **the step counter `V:4A992`**, the clock accumulators `V:4A9BC` to `V:4A9CC`).
2. **Set the switch block to 1**: the bytes `V:4A83C` to `V:4A847` become 1: the view selector `V:4A83D` = 1 (an external view: free flight starts in the chase view), the menu-keys latch `V:4A83F` = 1, the collision switch `V:4A844` = 1 (nothing in the image ever stores 0 to it, Part B section 8.10: collisions are on in every mode), and the other training switches `V:4A83C`, `V:4A83E`, `V:4A840` to `V:4A843`, `V:4A845` to `V:4A847` (Part B section 8 names them).
3. `V:4A95F` is reloaded from `V:4A960`; the recorder counters `V:54E42` and `V:54E40` are cleared.
4. The text system is reset: the id list `V:4A802` (two words), the line step `V:4A7F6` = 440, the engine byte `V:4A898` = 0xFF, the display-list request `V:4A910` = 0xFF, the camera mode `V:4A966` = 0xFF, the countdown `V:4AB8E` = 5.
5. `0x34802`: the planes of both buffers are cleared (8,000 bytes per plane).
6. The 16 object records at `0x4B23C` are cleared over their first 164 bytes (record n at `0x4B23C` + 0x200 n) and the 16 effect records at `0x4D23C` over 32 bytes each; then the player record routine `0x1D8B8` (note 05 section 15: word 0 = 0x11C8 and the refuel).
7. Variables: the words `V:4AA5E` and `V:4AA60` = 0x140; `V:4A900` = 0 (location 0); the direct-draw flag `V:4A90F` = 1; `V:54E72` = 0x800; the map-camera constants (`0x1D3A4` with `0x1D3F2`: the camera position `V:4ACF6`/`V:4ACFA`/`V:4ACFE` = 0x10C00000, 0x05000000, 0x11400000 and the negated, cell-masked copies at `V:4ACEA`...); the map view's altitude long `V:4A71C` = 0x03000000 (note 06); the camera trail `V:4AD02`...`V:4AD0A` = 0; `V:4AB4C` = 0x1C20 and `V:4AB4E` = 0; the view words `V:4AA3C` = 0xA7, `V:4AA3E` = 0x32 and `V:4AA40` = 0x320; `V:4ABB2` and `V:4ABBA` = -1; `V:4A90D` = 0xFF; the frame-rate setting `V:4A976` = 15; `V:4ABA0` = 0x7FFF; `V:4A7B2` = 0x800000; `V:4AAF6`, `V:4AAF8`, `V:4AAFA` = 0xA8, 0xFC, 0x80; `V:4A895` = 0x80; the 22-word index table at `0x53870` (0 to 21); the 48 bytes `V:4A8B2`...`V:4A8E1` = 0x30 and the 28 bytes `V:4A8C2` = 0x20 (the HUD string buffers: the second fill overwrites the first's tail).
8. The shared tail of the player start (`0x1D4FE`; it **places the player** at the start block `V:4A900`, which item 7 has just set to 0, and then stores the recorder pointers: `V:54E4C`, `V:54E54`, `V:54E5C`, `V:54E58`, then `V:4A9E4` and `V:4A9E8` cleared) and `0x1F31C` (clear and rebuild the terrain visibility bit tables), `0x1F54E` and `0x1F770` (view-dependent list refresh and display-list rebuild, note 04a).

The block takes about 8 blanks in the `0x11A70` pass (the game-over log: `0x11A70` from k = 98 to 106) and 6 in the `0x11AE2` pass [V].

### The dispatch state `0x1201E` (every mode; entered from the menu echo `0x11E04`)

Runs when its countdown has expired: 210 passes from the main menu (`V:4A8E3` = 0 and bit 7 of the flag byte `V:4AC13` set), 150 after an ESC restart (`V:4A8E3` = 1); the echo text of the mode (5.10) is on screen meanwhile. [V: from the menu the state lasts 40 blanks for modes 2 and 9, `flow-s1-menukeys-a.txt`; after an ESC restart 32 blanks, `flow-s2-esc-flight-dense.txt` k = 55 to 87; about 5.3 passes per blank in both.]

1. `V:4A88F` = 0 (menu sound state off); the sound channels 0 and 1 are stopped (`0x19C58`); the sound volume target `V:54FBE` = 0x3F0000; the music call by mode: modes 3 to 8 `0x19D7A(0x28)`, otherwise `0x19F9A(8)` when bit 4 of `V:4AC13` is set, else `0x19E46(8, 0x300)` (note 10).
2. `V:4AA3C` = 0xB3; the text reset `0x13462` (5.4); the countdown `V:4AB8E` = 4.
3. **The allowance `V:4A94F` = 3 and the countercode tries `V:4A950` = 2** (6.3).
4. **The placement `0x2CE36`** (below): the scenario's own records.
5. `V:4A866` = 0 (unpaused), the gate `V:4A84D` = 1, the panel redraw counter `V:4A8EE` = 3 (tested at `0x1129A` for the external view; not decoded further), the fade target `V:4A959` = the current level `V:4A958` (freeze the fade where it is), the live palette cleared to black (`0x13C5E`: all 32 entries zero, `V:4A95C` = 2).
6. State `0x12534` (the default for modes 3 to 8), `V:4A900` = 3 and `V:4A901` = 0x11 (the carrier location and the F/A-18 as defaults).
7. The **mode branch** (6.3 lists the allowance of each): mode 1: map-camera constants (`0x1D3B8`), allowance 5, state `0x1234C`; mode 2: view words `V:4AA3C` = 0xA7, `V:4A86C` = 0xFF, the training scene `0x2DBA4`, `V:4A83D` = 1, `V:4A966` = 0, `V:4A86D` = 1, the viewed record `V:4A994` = 4 and `V:4A996` = 0x800, the ring direction `V:4A96A` = 3, a flag bit 0x100 in record 4's second word (the instructor's `W2`, at `0x4BA3E`; not the player's: see 8.3), state `0x123C2`; mode 3: `V:4A900` = 0 (the airfield start); mode 9: `V:4A900` = 3, `V:4A966` = 0, `V:4A865` = 0, the placement again, **the player start `0x1D4E2`**, the text reset, `V:4A965` = 1, the gate 1, `V:4AA3E` = `V:4AA40` = 0, `0x1EFEC`, `V:4A910` = 0xFF, `V:4A83D` = 0, state `0x1234C`; mode 0x7D: allowance 1, `V:4AA3C` = 0xA7, `V:4A86C` = 0xFF, `V:4A86E` = 3, `V:4A900` = 2, **the player start `0x12CE0`** (the shared tail `0x1D4FE` of the player start plus one player step `0x26AF8` with `V:4A984` bit 0x800 set), the training camera `0x2DBAE` and `0x1D3E0`, the placement `0x2CFDA`, `V:4A865` = 0, state `0x123C2`; mode 0x7F: `V:4A866` = 1, state `0x11B54` (the demo, Part B); modes 4 to 8: no branch.
8. Last, when `V:4A900` is 3 the aircraft `V:4A901` is forced to 0x11, and the display lists are rebuilt (`0x1F770`).

**The camera before the location screen: no flight (milestone 7, Task 2; review M4 and the NOTE-GAP of `game.c:1998` closed)** [V: `flow7-loc-mover.txt`, free flight from the menu key 2 to the countercode prompt, the camera longs `V:4ACF6`, the target `V:4AD0E`, the trail `V:4AD02`, the mover stage, the camera mode, the ring flag `V:4A86D` and the fade every 4 blanks, 1 run; `flow7-latch-loc.txt` for the location screen itself]. The camera holds the planted position of the reset block (x 0x10C00000, height 0x05000000, z 0x11400000) at the key and at the dispatch state `0x1201E` (blank 8 after the key), and holds **x 0x10800000, height 0x03000000, z 0x10C00000, the standing position of the selection screens, from the first sample after the dispatch's mode-1 branch (blank 48, the state `0x1234C`)**, unchanged to the unit through the fade-out and the fade-in (the fade level rises from blank 120), the prompt (camera mode 4 from blank 164) and the whole of the location screen. **The mover does not run**: the ring flag `V:4A86D` stays 0, the target and the trail stay zero and the stage 0, the step counter stands at 5 and then 11 (the map passes of the prompt). The standing position is therefore a plant of the dispatch (the map-camera constants of the mode-1 branch, item 7), 70 blanks before the first picture of the map, not the end of a flight; **the port's camera at the standing position from the first picture is right, and nothing is missing** (the qualification and the training dispatches plant their own constants, items 6 and 7; their cameras were not sampled).

**Training part 1 and the start block** [R: `0x12102`, `0x1D256`, `0x1D4FE`; settled in the port's fourth round]. The dispatch state stores `V:4A900` = 3 for **every** mode (item 6, at `0x12102`), but the reset block had already run (`0x1D256` stored 0, and its item 8 `0x1D4FE` placed the player at block 0), and nothing places the player again in mode 2: **in training part 1 the player stands on the runway of airfield 1 (start block 0, 8.3) while `V:4A900` reads 3 (the carrier default)**. The flight readers of `V:4A900` that see the mismatch are `0x1E404` (the fixed camera of Shift+KP-Enter indexes the table `0x47ADE` with it, so in part 1 its point is the carrier's, not the airfield's; the keypad group, KP-ENTER included, is active in part 1, 8.8, and the shift test is made in the key body, so the key is available: R) and `0x28B28` (inside the landing test of the mission script, which takes the carrier-deck condition when `V:4A900` is 3; it belongs to the mission modes' script, and whether the training sequencer ever calls it is not established).

### The player start in free flight (`0x12CE0` at the end of the aircraft selection)

Free flight reaches the player start late: `0x12C6E` (after the aircraft key, 30 blanks of passes with the gate open) calls `0x12CE0`, which enters the player start at its shared tail `0x1D4FE` (not at `0x1D4E2`: the view selector and the head request are not reset there, which is why free flight starts in the external view that the reset block set) and runs one player step; then `0x1D42A` (the camera target offset from the player's matrix), the mission music for modes 3 to 8, camera mode 6 and state `0x12D58`. The start block used is the one the location key stored (`V:4A900`, section 5.6; note 05 17.14) and the aircraft `V:4A901`. [V: the log `flow-s2-mission-start.txt`: state `0x12C6E` from k = 1 to 36, `0x12D58` from k = 36]

### The placement `0x2CE36` (the scenario's own aircraft, ships and landmarks; called at the dispatch and, for the training re-position, from the player step)

Inputs: the mode byte, the selector `V:4ABB0` (the low word of the microsecond reading of the last timer-device read `V:4ABAE`, taken by the countercode challenge builder `0x2963A` and by `0x12E4E`/`0x18E54` at a mission start: it changes only when the clock is read, so within one session it takes a new value at each countercode challenge and mission start; its low two bits are effectively random, so variant 0 occurs half the time and variants 1 and 2 a quarter each, [R] on the source, not measured as a distribution; the port may draw it from its own seeded generator at the dispatch (C), and a fixed variant 0 is the minimum) (a free-running count read at the call; 0 and 1 give variant 0, 2 and 3 give 1 and 2; with `V:4A8E3` set the variant stored last time, `V:4ABD4`, is reused: an ESC restart keeps the same variant), the progress counter `V:4A95F`, the unlock byte of the mode `V:1D024`+0x12+mode. Steps: clear the hostile count `V:4A961`, the counter `V:4A963` and a word `V:4A83A`; mode 0x7D: the routine below; otherwise take the row of the table `0x2DEDA`; set the hostile cap `V:4A962` from the row; walk the chosen sub-list and place each entry (the first entry of a sub-list is placed without the threshold test, the others when their progress threshold is not above `V:4A95F`; every placed entity is then turned to face its destination by the aim routine `0x1454A`).

**The table `0x2DEDA`** [R; all 10 rows decoded]: 10 rows of 12 bytes. Row index (0-based) = mode - 1 for modes 1 to 9 and 9 for 0x7E and 0x7F (note 05 17.17 rule 5 counts the same rows from 1: "row 10"). A row is three pairs, one per variant: the byte offset of a **list** from `0x2DEDA` and a small count; the three offsets of a row are the same for rows 0, 1, 3 to 9 and differ by 8 bytes for row 2 (mode 3, the three variants). The count is **the hostile cap** `V:4A962` (2 for most rows, 4 for mode 8, 6 for the demo row): **every entry** (not only the aircraft: the carrier and the pod too, `0x2D2D6`) is skipped once `V:4A961` (the count of placed entries of the aircraft class nibble whose "not counted" bit is clear, the cruise missile included) has reached `V:4A962`, so the order of the entries decides which are placed; the success test of the kill-count script (`0x28A96`) compares the counter `V:4A963` (cleared by the placement) with the cap (section 12.3 and 12.9).

**A list** begins with a header of four words, the offsets (from the start of the list) of four **sub-lists** (selector 0 to 3: the byte `V:1D024`+0x12+mode, 0 for the demo rows, which the code doubles into a word offset; in free flight, mode 1 and 9, the four offsets are equal or the four sub-lists differ in one word, note 05 17.17 rule 5); every list of the 11 distinct lists has this header [R, all 11 read]. A **sub-list** is a run of 10-byte entries ended by a first word with the sign bit set (the walk tests the sign, so any negative word ends it; 0xFFFF is the value the extractor reads) [R]. The **entry** is five words:

| word | meaning |
| --- | --- |
| 0 | byte offset into the object-record table `0x25ECC`: 20 times the record id (note 02 section 1): 160 is the carrier (id 8), 60 the F-16 (id 3), 120 the MiG-29 (id 6). The core needs only the id (offset / 20); the template's five longs are copied to the run-time record copy at `0x2600C` + 20 slot |
| 1 | class word: the low byte is the class stored at record +0x62 (0x20 carrier, 0x10 F-16, 0x12 MiG-29, 0x15 cruise missile, ...: note 02 section 9) |
| 2 | bits 0 to 6: the slot, the record index in `0x4B23C` (14 is the carrier, 4, 6, 8, 10 aircraft); bits 8 to 14 and bit 15 give the entity's **destination** [R, not run]: bit 15 set: bits 8 to 14 are an index of a position block (byte offset (word & 0x7F00) >> 7 into the table of word 3; 0 = no destination), stored at record +0x2C to +0x34; bit 15 clear: the destination is the entity's own start block and bits 8 to 14 name the record index of a leader to follow (record +0x38 = that index with bit 7 set); record +0x3A gets bits 8 to 14 in both cases |
| 3 | position-block reference: a **byte offset into the word table `0x2DCE8`** (confirmed: for the carrier entry the value 66 gives the word 2198 at `0x2DCE8` + 66, and the block at `0x2DCE8` + 2198 reads cell (70, 65), fine offsets (6144, 6144), height 0: the carrier of note 05 17.14) |
| 4 | flags: bit 15 set = the entity is **not counted** as a hostile (record flag +1 bit 3 set; clear and aircraft class = counted in `V:4A961`); bit 14 = record +5 set to 8; bit 13 = record +1 bit 0 set; bit 12 = random offset of the placement (the offset from `V:4ABB0`: +-1 to 3 cells, half of it when bit 7 is set); bits 8 to 11 = mode filter (placed only when the mode equals this value; 0 = every mode); bit 7 = half offset; bits 0 to 6 = progress threshold (placed only when `V:4A95F` is at least this value) |

The **position block** (note 02 section 9) is five words at the offset the table gives: cell x, cell z, fine x, fine z, height; the position is cell << 22 plus the fine values times 256 (cell = position >> 22, the unit of the record's cell words `+0x06`/`+0x08`).

**The spawn regions `0x2DE28`** [R]: four 32-bit pointers (to `0x2DE3A`, `0x2DE6E`, `0x2DE8E`, `0x2DEA4`) and the word `0xFFFF` at `0x2DE38` as the end mark (the walk `0x2D0AA` reads the high word of each pointer and stops at the sign bit; at most 8 regions). A region is: a **box** of four words xmin, xmax, zmin, zmax in the cell unit above (disk data, not listed: `tables/scenario.bin`, spawn-region sub-table, FNV-1a-32 `5001d22d`), a **count word**, entries of five words as above, and a terminating 0xFFFF. The count word is the number of entries the trigger processes when the player's cell enters the box (`0x2D22A`, once per entry into the box: the bit of the region in `V:4A855` records "inside"; modes 1, 2 and 0x7D never trigger, and the demo's flag `V:4A848` switches the walk off): 0 for regions 0 and 1, whose two entries (F-16s with bit 15 set) are therefore inert, 1 for regions 2 and 3 (a MiG-29 and a cruise missile for mode 8 only). A region trigger runs only for the modes above 2 other than 0x7D (the missions and the qualification); the walk of the regions runs **every 16th pass** (low nibble of the step counter `V:4A992` equal to 3, `0x26B4C`; section 12.4); when the player leaves the box the entries it placed are re-matched by slot (`0x2D116`, `0x2D5D4`) and sent to their destination block. [R]

**The training placement `0x2CFDA`** (mode 0x7D; asked by the extractor round) [V: Musashi rig, 300 random player states, 0 differences for the position, 100 random states for the attitude copy]: it copies the first 256 bytes of the player record into record 4 (slot `V:4AA6C` = 4, byte offset 0x800), installs the F-16 template (record id 3, copied to the run-time copy at `0x2600C` + 80) and moves the copy **ahead of the player along the player's heading**: position = the player's position plus the vector (0, 0, 72) rotated by the heading-only matrix of the player (rows (cos h, 0, -sin h), (0, 1, 0), (sin h, 0, cos h) in 14-bit fixed point, 16384 = 1, h the player's heading word, 28,800 units per circle, the sine table of note 03), shifted right by 4: that is **73,728 position units** (72 x 1024) along (-sin h, 0, cos h), no vertical offset; the attitude (pitch, heading, roll) is the player's; class 0x10, `+0x5D` = 1, `+0x5E` = 4, `+0x2C` = 0xFFFF, the cell words recomputed from the position and the sub-index `+0x0A` from the low two bits of the cells, no carrier. The rig note's value "(273,237,745, 280,545,007), heading 2256" is this formula evaluated at the player's position and heading at that moment (it is not a constant of the scenario). It is also what the RETURN re-position (Part B section 8) re-uses.

**Port rules (C).** The extractor's `scenario.bin` carries the table, the lists and the position blocks (the progress threshold and mode filter in the flags word, the cap in the row); the core implements the entry rules above from the words; the training placement is a formula, not a table.

### 6.2 The three-tick wait before flight

**What it is.** The last states before the flight state `0x12EFE` are `0x12DB8` (the take-off or launch notice), `0x12E4E` (the instant the mission starts) and `0x12EDA` (the wait). They run for every mode that goes through the briefing flow, and for free flight after the camera approach (6.4). The step counter `V:4A992` is cleared in `0x12E4E` (the instruction at `0x12EC0`), the countdown `V:4AB8E` is set to 3 there, and the flight state is entered after the countdown has run out [R: `0x12E4E`, `0x12EDA`; note 06 line 488 names the same sequence].

**The states** [R, order and times V]:

| state | what it does |
| --- | --- |
| `0x12DB8` | after the countdown of `0x12D58` (5): the gate `V:4A84D` = 0; for the modes 3 to 8 the outcome lookup `0x29534` (id 0x58, the vector notice, when it returns a non-negative value); for modes other than 2 the notice id: 0x5A (launch from the carrier) when bit 3 of the player's `B4` (`V:4B240`) is set, else 0x59 (take-off); for mode 2 the engine byte `V:4A898` is set to 0xFF so that the next state runs at once; stores `0x12E4E` |
| `0x12E4E` | waits for the engine byte `V:4A898` to be negative (the notice typed and its end hold run out); then: gate = 1; `V:4A984` bit 0x40 set (**the flight simulation switch**: from here the player step runs); `V:4A986` bit 0x100 set; `V:4A98A` bit 2 set (a flag bit of the mission-status word; note 05 names its bits 1 and 14 only); for mode 2 `V:4A84B` = 1; the clock is read (`0x18E54`) and stored as the mission start `V:4A9CC` and `V:4A9C4`; **`V:4A992` = 0**; the countdown `V:4AB8E` = 3; stores `0x12EDA` |
| `0x12EDA` | waits for the countdown to go below 0; then `V:4A859` = 1 (the HUD is switched on), the start sound (`0x37C44`), stores `0x12EFE` |

The countdown falls once per pass, so the wait is the passes of `0x12EDA` with the countdown at 2, 1, 0 and the pass in which it is -1 (that pass acts), then the first pass of `0x12EFE`. During all of them the gate is open and the pause byte is 0, so the world runs and the step counter turns at the end of every pass: the counter reads 0, 1, 2, 3 at the starts of the four `0x12EDA` passes and 4 and 5 at the starts of the next two passes (V:4A992 first counts in `0x12EDA`, not in the flight state).

**Per-blank table** (`flow-s2-mission-start-skip.txt`, log `flow-s2-mission-start-skip.txt`; free flight, KP-ENTER pressed during the camera approach; k counts blanks from the KP-ENTER) [V, 1 run; the same tail in `flow-s2-mission-start-long.txt`, which reaches `0x12EDA` at k = 1338 of its own count after the approach has ended by itself, and the flight state at k = 1374: 36 blanks]:

| k | event | state | gate | step counter `V:4A992` | text | engine `V:4A898` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | the approach (camera mode 6) runs | `0x12D58` | 1 | 0x32 | none | 0 |
| 2 | KP-ENTER seen: the view selector becomes the cockpit (`V:4A83D` = 0) | `0x12D58` | 1 | 0x33 | none | 0 |
| 14 | `0x12D58` sees the cockpit: camera mode 0, countdown 5 | `0x12DB8` | 1 | 0x34 | none | 0 |
| 14 to 62 | six passes of 8 to 9 blanks with the countdown running | `0x12DB8` | 1 | 0x34 to 0x39 | none | 0 |
| 70 | the notice id 0x59 is stored (free flight, runway start), the gate falls | `0x12E4E` | 0 | 0x3A | 0x59 | 0 |
| 70 to 107 | the notice is typed (the picture of the cockpit stays under it) | `0x12E4E` | 0 | 0x3A | 0x59 until k = 107 | 0 |
| 107 | list consumed, end hold begins | `0x12E4E` | 0 | 0x3A | none | 1 |
| 115 | hold over (0x81): the state acts: gate 1, simulation on, **step counter cleared**, countdown 3 | `0x12EDA` | 1 | 0 | none | 0x81 |
| 123, 131, 139 | the three waiting passes (8 blanks each) | `0x12EDA` | 1 | 1, 2, 3 | none | 0x81 |
| 148 | the acting pass: HUD on, start sound; the counter shows 4 | `0x12EFE` | 1 | 4 | none | 0x81 |
| 157 | the first flight pass (counter 5 and counting every 8 to 9 blanks) | `0x12EFE` | 1 | 5 | none | 0x81 |

**Rules.** The wait is **three waiting passes plus the acting pass** after the pass in which the counter is cleared: 4 passes of about 8 blanks, from the clear (k = 115) to the first pass of the flight state (k = 157) 42 blanks. With the conversion (blanks x 8 + 25) / 50 that is 7 steps; the port's fixed step replaces the passes (one step per pass; this is the 3.8 contract's exception 4: the flight simulation runs in these passes, so they are not converted from blanks), so **the port waits 4 steps after the step that clears the counter, then enters the flight state in the fifth**, and the counter reads 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 at the starts of those steps [C: the port step is one pass; the 42 blanks equal 6.7 steps at 8 per second]. The simulation is switched on at the first of them: the aircraft is already simulated during the wait with idle controls (note 06 line 488: the original's first flight pass has `n` = 5 here). The notice before it is typed in 37 blanks and held 8 blanks (45 blanks = 7 steps).

### 6.3 The crash allowance by mode

**One byte, four stores** [R; the complete set of writers in the code region, checked by scanning every operand of `V:4A94F`]: `V:4A94F` is the number of crashes the player may still survive. It is stored at mission start by the dispatch state `0x1201E`: 3 for every mode first (`0x120BC`), then overwritten by the mode's own branch (5 at `0x1217A` for free flight, 1 at `0x12202` for mode 0x7D); and decremented in the crash wait state `0x138D8` (`0x1394A`, after the restart `0x1D538`). Nothing else reads or writes it outside the data hunks: the HUD does not display it.

| mode | allowance at mission start | game over after | evidence |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| 1 free flight | 5 | the 5th crash | [V] `allow` = 5 in all captures of free flight (airborne snapshot and the ESC restarts; the restart does not reset it: it stays 5 through a restart, `flow-s2-esc-flight.txt`, 420 blanks); the staged game over (poked to 1) |
| 2 training demonstration | 3 | 3rd | [R] |
| 3 to 8 missions | 3 | 3rd | [R] (note 05 17.17 read 3 in the demo, whose mode byte is 3) |
| 9 qualification | 3 | 3rd | [R] |
| 0x7D training practice | 1 | 1st | [R]; collisions are on (`V:4A844` stays 1, Part B 8.10), so the first crash of the player is counted and, the allowance being 1, ends the flight with the game-over screen (5.12) |
| 0x7F demo | 3 | not reachable (the demo is not flown) | [V] `demo.vasnap` reads 3 |

**Rules.** (1) The value is set once per selection, in the pass in which `0x1201E` runs, so every ESC restart (5.11) resets it to the mode's value: a player who crashed four times in free flight and presses ESC and re-selects free flight has five again [R: the dispatch runs again after the ESC restart; the captured restart kept 5 because the mode's value is 5]. (2) The crash path decrements it after the restart record has been rewritten, so game over shows on the crash that finds it 1: with 5 the fifth crash. (3) A game over leaves the mode (5.12): the next selection sets it again. (4) `V:4A950`, a second small counter of the same dispatch, is **not** a crash count: it is the number of wrong countercode answers still allowed (stored 2 at `0x120C4`, decremented at `0x12A06`; the port skips the countercode, so it does not exist there).

**Port rules (C).** Task 4: `allowance` is an integer in `game_t`; `screen_start_flight` sets 5, 3 or 1 by mode as the table; the core's crash flow (milestone 3, `game.c`) already decrements it per crash and ends the flight at 0 with the screens of 5.12.

### 6.4 Camera modes 5 and 6 and their key set

**What the camera modes are.** `V:4A966` is the camera-mode byte of the front end: how the scene behind the text is made while a selection screen is up. It is `0xFF` after a reset (`0x1D1F0`) and 0 in normal flight; the values 2 to 6 belong to the map flights that bring the player from the menu to the cockpit. Their writers are the states of 5.5 to 5.7 and the camera mover of hunk 27 (`0x2D756` and the stage routines `0x2D93A`-`0x2DCE7`: it moves the camera position `V:4ACF6`... toward the target `V:4AD0E` by damped steps; the stage `V:4A86E` (0 to 8, a jump table at `0x2D640`) advances as the distance falls through thresholds from 0x1C00000 down to 0x240 position units, the damping shift shrinking from 13 to 5, and the mode byte is set at some of the stages) [R, writers read; the effect V in the logs named below]:

| mode | meaning | set by | left by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0xFF | none: menu and text screens | `0x1D1F0` (reset) | the first camera state |
| 0 | normal camera: cockpit or the flight camera of note 06 | `0x11C18`, `0x121B6`, `0x1225C` (starts), `0x12D10`, `0x12D6C` (the cockpit start, in `0x12CFE` and `0x12D58`), `0x2DC54` (the approach ends) | any selection |
| 2 | approach to the mission's first hostile (briefing screens of the missions) | `0x12780` | the camera mover sets 3 on arrival |
| 3 | arrived at the target: set by the mover when its distance to the target falls below the threshold of its stage | `0x2DA3A` | `0x1279C` waits for it, then the prompt |
| 4 | the map overview of the bay, with the countercode prompt or its skip | `0x123A0` (state `0x12378`, after the fade-in), `0x2DA72` | `0x12AC0` waits for it (always already 4), then 5 |
| 5 | the location selection: the map, the four numbers and the prompt text id 7 | `0x12B4A` (state `0x12AFC`, free flight only) | a location key; then 6 |
| 6 | the approach from the map down to the start location ("fly-in") | `0x12BD8` (at the location key for a runway or airfield start; `0x12C90` for the carrier start, which skips the aircraft choice), `0x124F8` (mode 0x7D) | `0x2DC54`: when the camera is within 0x240 of the target for 5 passes (`V:4A95D` counts 5 down) it sets the view toggle request (`V:4A97E` bit 1: cockpit view) and mode 0 |

**The chain for free flight** (the other modes join at `0x12AC0` or skip it): `0x12378` (fade-in, mode 4 stored when the levels are equal) to `0x127C8` (the countercode prompt, skipped when `V:4A8E3` is set: the port skips it always, 5.5) to `0x128DA` (the verdict; mode 1 stores `0x12AC0`) to `0x12AC0` (waits for mode 4; sets `V:4A86D` = 0, `V:4A90F` = 0, countdown 2) to `0x12AFC` (when the countdown has run out: gate 0, mode 1: text id 7, camera mode 5, `V:4A90F` = 1, `V:4A965` = 0, the engine flag reset) to `0x12B74` (the location selection, 5.6; the gate opens when the prompt text has been typed). After a location key `0x12B74` closes the gate, sets `V:4A8EE` = 3 and the countdown 2 and: runway or airfield start: `V:4A849` = 1 (the aircraft menu is live), camera mode 6, state `0x12C02` (countdown, then the aircraft prompt id 0x4B, state `0x12C36`: waits for `V:4A849` = 0, which the aircraft key clears after storing `V:4A901`); carrier start: straight to `0x12C6E`. `0x12C6E` (after its countdown) runs the player start (6.1) and `0x1D42A` and stores `0x12D58`, which keeps the approach going until the camera mover ends it or the player skips it; then `0x12DB8` (6.2).

**The camera mover, stage by stage** [R: read from `0x2D756`-`0x2DCE7`; the pass count of the whole approach is V, below]. The mover runs once per pass of the map states (it returns at once while the pause byte `V:4A866` is nonzero or the view is not an external one). Each pass it takes D = the largest of the three absolute coordinate differences between the camera `V:4ACF6` and its target `V:4AD0E` (position units, 256 per height unit), looks up the stage `V:4A86E` (0 to 8, jump table `0x2D640`), possibly advances the stage and sets the target, and then moves the camera one damped step toward the target (`0x2DC1A`-`0x2DCCA`). The stage thresholds on D and the step shift s:

| stage | step shift s while D is above the threshold | advances to the next stage when D is at or below | target in this stage |
| ---: | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | 13 above 0x1C00000; then 11 above 0xD00000 (at 0x1C00000 the view flags `V:4A86C` = 1 and `V:4A865` = 0 are set) | 0xD00000 | the player's position blended with the camera (`0x2DB1E`: four successive halvings of the sum of camera and player position) |
| 1 | 11 above 0x200000, then 10 above 0x60000 | 0x60000 | as stage 0 |
| 2 | 9 above 0x60000, then 7 above 0x30000 | 0x30000 | as stage 0, then the player's position (`0x2DBAE`) |
| 3 | 7 above 0x60000, 6 (never reached: same threshold), 5 above 0xD000, 3 above 0x8000 | 0x8000 | the player's position, then an offset target (`0x2DBC0`): 6 and 3 position-shifted units for airfield starts 1 and 2, -6 and -9 for the others, shift 4 |
| 4 | 2 above 0x2800 | 0x2800 | the offset target (`0x2DBE6`: shift 1, offsets 6 or -6, 6) |
| 5 | 1 | arrival | the offset target |
| 6 | (arrival, camera mode 6 only) the stage is set when D is at most 0x240 for the first time: `V:4A95D` = 5, `V:4A86C` = 0 | 5 further passes | none: after 5 passes the view toggle request (cockpit) is set and the camera mode becomes 0 |
| 7, 8 | the mission approach (camera modes 2 and 3): shift 14 above 0x800000, 12 above 0x100000 | 0x100000 (mode 3 set, then mode 4 for the map) | the first hostile's position / the map target (`0x1D3A4`) |

The damped step (`0x2DC5C`): the difference vector is scaled down by 4 until D is below 0x4800 (a long way off), normalised (`0x29C0E`, length 0x200), shifted left by s, averaged with the previous step `V:4AD02` (new = old + (new - old) / 2) and added to the camera. So the approach is a geometric slow-down in six stages (the shifts fall from 13 to 1, a smaller shift is a shorter step) and ends when D <= 0x240 for 5 passes. The measured whole approach of free flight from the aircraft key is 112 passes (the step counter turns 0x21 to 0x91) of about 10 blanks, 1,176 blanks [V, below]; the port may reproduce the mover with this table (the position arithmetic is exact integer arithmetic) or stop it at the pass count 112 (C).

**The stage list from the end of the approach to the first flight pass** (3.8 contract; these states run the world every pass with the gate open, so by exception 4 they count **one pass per step in every mode**, not the slow blanks of the logs; the notice is typed text and keeps its measured blanks). Stages: the arrival and the cockpit view in `0x12D58` take 2 passes = 2 steps; the `0x12DB8` countdown of 5 passes = 6 steps (the pass that stores it and the five that follow); the notice typed (37 blanks) = 6 steps; its end hold (8 blanks) = 1; the wait of 6.2 (four passes) = 4. **Total 2 + 6 + 6 + 1 + 4 = 19 steps** from the approach's end to the first flight pass. Evidence: the step counter `V:4A992` in the part 2 log runs 0x2E to 0x30 over `0x12D58` and 0x30 to 0x36 over `0x12DB8` (one count per pass), and the per-blank table of 6.2 (passes of 8 to 9 blanks). The earlier list of 30 + 56 blanks (5 + 9 steps) measured slow emulator passes, not the rule, and is withdrawn.

**Per-blank evidence** (V; one run each): from the ESC restart (`flow-s2-esc-flight-dense.txt`, k from the ESC): `0x12378` k = 162 to 205 (fade-in 0 to 15, one level per 3 blanks), `0x127C8` from k = 207 (camera mode 4, gate 1, `V:4A965` = 1; the passes are map passes of 12 to 16 blanks: **77 blanks** until the countdown runs out), `0x12AC0` k = 285 (`V:4A965` = 2), `0x12B74` k = 286 (mode 5, text id 7, gate 0), gate 1 and engine byte 1 at k = 410 (**124 blanks** for the typed prompt: on the map screen a pass is about 1 blank, not 0.2, so 43 characters at 3 passes take 124 blanks) [`flow-s2-esc-flight-dense.txt`]. From the aircraft key (`flow-s2-mission-start.txt` and `-long.txt`): `0x12C36` to `0x12C6E` at k = 1 to 6 (the key was consumed), `0x12C6E` k = 6 to 36 (30 blanks, the player start at its end), `0x12D58` from k = 36 with camera mode 6, **the approach ends by itself at k = 1212 of the long log** (camera mode 0; 1,176 blanks, 23.5 s, 188 steps), the view is the cockpit at k = 1224 and `0x12DB8` at k = 1242 [the long log samples every 6 blanks].

**KP-ENTER skips the approach** [V: `flow-s2-mission-start-skip.txt`: KP-ENTER at k = 0 gave the cockpit view at k = 2 and `0x12DB8` at k = 14, 250 blanks into the approach; frames `skip-4`... under `extracted/captures/flow`]. In mode 6 the key handler accepts KP-ENTER directly (`0x1DCA0`, no other condition) and runs the view-toggle code (`0x1E390`: key latch `V:4A85B` = 2, `V:4A97E` bit 1, `V:4A8EB` = 0xFF, `V:4A86D` = `V:4A86C` = 1); `0x12D58` sees the cockpit and continues as in 6.2. The states `0x12A50`, `0x12A92`, `0x12AC0`, `0x12AFC` and `0x12B74` also test the latch for the value 2 and then run `0x12CFE`, which starts a flight **at once with the defaults** (`V:4A900` = 0 and `V:4A901` = 0x11 from the reset: airport 1 and the F/A-18, no selection; in a mission the dispatch has already stored the mission's own start, `V:4A900` = 3 in F2) [R]; the key handler passes KP-ENTER in camera modes 3 to 5 only while `V:4A965` = 2, which holds from the countercode answer through the orders text (to `0x12AFC`) **[V, C11: `followup-brief-skip.txt`, KP-ENTER 100 blanks into the orders of F2: the state was `0x12DB8` within 4 blanks, the approach skipped entirely]**. **The camera mode after the skip is 0** [V: `followup-brief-skip.txt`, the byte `V:4A966` per blank: 3 (the orders) at the key, **0 from the next blank on** and for the whole run, with the state `0x12DB8` within 4 blanks; the mode 6 of the question is the approach's own camera, which the skip never enters]. The skip is therefore reachable during the orders; the port's briefing keeps KP-ENTER as the skip of the orders and the approach together (to the 6-pass countdown of 6.2). SHIFT-KP-ENTER is a different handler path (`0x1E3EE`: the ring direction change of note 06).

**The key set by camera mode** [R, from the dispatcher `0x1DAA0`, `0x1DC0C` to `0x1DD64`; note 06 item 11 has the mode-5 part]:

| camera mode (and flags) | keys the dispatcher acts on |
| --- | --- |
| 3, and every mode while `V:4A965` or `V:4A88B` is nonzero (`V:4A88B` is the log-disk phase flag of 5.3) | only the restricted set at `0x1DD64`: KP-ENTER when `V:4A965` = 2; SHIFT make and break (the shift latch `V:4A930`); the break codes of the held control keys; the Amiga keys' latches; DEL and the typed-input key `0x31` (log-disk and name entry, 5.3, Part B section 10); **ESC (`0x1DE38`)**; RETURN, which only clears `V:4A88B` (the log-disk prompt flag, 5.3) |
| 2 or 4 (`V:4A965` = 0) | ESC (`0x1DCA8`), then the restricted set |
| 5 (and `V:4A965` = 0) | ESC (`0x1EF50`); the digits 1 to 0 and the function keys F1 to F10 (`0x1E99E`, `0x1E97C`); the keypad keys KP1 to KP8 as selection digits (`0x1E9A4`); the bracket keys with their releases (cockpit zoom); P (the pause key: allowed because `V:4A966` is not negative); then the restricted set above (KP-ENTER only at `V:4A965` = 2) |
| 6 | the same as mode 5, and KP-ENTER at any time |
| 0 | the full flight key set (note 06) |

What a digit does depends on the flags: with `V:4A849` set (the aircraft menu) 1 stores `V:4A901` = 0x11 and 2 stores 0x10 and clears the flag (5.7); otherwise, with the location prompt up (the camera mode 5 states, the mode byte nonzero, the simulation bit `V:4A984` 0x0800 not yet set), the digit d selects the start block d - 1 through the table walk of `0x1EB96`; the walk accepts the digit when it reaches an entry index at least d - 1 or an entry of the carrier type (first word negative, not -1) first, so digits above 4 are accepted as soon as the walk meets the carrier entry 3 [V: key 5 stored `V:4A900` = 4 and key 7 stored 6 on the location screen, `flow-s2-loc-key5.txt`, `flow-s2-loc-key7.txt`, one run each; the screen advanced to the aircraft prompt (state `0x12C02`, camera mode 6) in 3 and 2 blanks; what the flight does with the stored value is section 11]; the selection takes effect only when `V:4A966` is not negative and `V:4A965` is 0 (the latch `V:4A86D` toggles; `0x12B74` consumes it).

**Port rules (C).** Task 4 models the free-flight chain as screens: prompt skipped (5.5), the map (camera mode 5) with the location prompt, the aircraft prompt, then the approach as a camera-driven stage (the camera path itself is the frontend's: the scene carries `camera_mode`), ended by the arrival (a flag from the core after a step count) or by KP-ENTER; the three-tick wait of 6.2 follows. **Correction (Part C, 12.14 N1): the approach state `0x12D58` runs the world every pass (gate 1), so by the contract's exception 4 it counts one pass per step: 112 steps for the free-flight carrier start, not 188; the blank figure is the slow map passes (about 10.5 blanks each).** The approach lasts 1,176 blanks = 188 steps in the original (the camera mover moves once per pass; the step counter turns 0x21 to 0x91, 112 passes of about 10 blanks, in the long log); the port runs the mover of the table above (or, simpler, ends the approach after 188 steps) and keeps KP-ENTER as the skip. **P on the selection screens** [R, not captured]: the key is accepted in camera modes 5 and 6 (`V:4A966` is not negative); it sets `V:4A866` = 0xFF and stops the sound channels, which freezes the map camera mover (it returns while the pause byte is nonzero) but starts no pause screen and no fade, because only the flight state `0x12EFE` and the approach state `0x12D58` test the pause byte for the pause screen (5.14); the typed text, the digit keys and ESC go on working, and a second P clears the byte. On the countercode and confirmation screens (camera mode 0xFF or 4 with `V:4A965` nonzero) P is not in the key set and does nothing. ESC is acted on in every one of these states (the restricted key set contains it), so ESC at the countercode stage, the location prompt, the aircraft prompt or during the approach restarts the selection (5.11); the other keys follow the table above.

## 7. Demo replay

Tags as in the header of this note. Scripts are under `tools/vamiga-ref/scripts/` (`flow-demo-*.txt`, written by `gen-flow-demo.py` where generated), logs under `extracted/captures/logs/` (git-ignored); the rig check is `tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/replay_check.py`. Game text is not quoted.

The original's "demo" (menu item 1) is not a separate program or a canned film: it is a normal flight of the F/A-18 from the carrier deck in which the player's controls are read from a recorded stream instead of the keyboard. The flight model, the world, the cameras and the HUD run as in play; only the input of the player's step (`0x1DFAA`) and the events of the key handlers (`0x1D954`) come from the stream. A port that has the flight model of note 05 therefore only needs the stream, the executor and the start and end screens of this section.

### 7.1 Location

| item | where | what |
| --- | --- | --- |
| key stream | disk file `df0:text/textply` (3676 bytes) | one key byte per recorded player step |
| event stream | disk file `df0:text/textctl` (14,704 bytes = 3676 x 4) | two big-endian words per recorded step |
| buffers | `V:54E44` (key buffer pointer), `V:54E48` (capacity in steps, a long), `V:54E50` (event buffer pointer) | allocated once at start-up by `0x10FAC` (called at `0x17DB6`): the available public memory halved and capped at 125,000, shifted right by 4 gives the capacity (7812 = `0x1E84` on the reference machine, read in `credits.vasnap`, `flow-menu.vasnap` and `demo.vasnap` [V]); the key buffer is capacity + 2 bytes, the event buffer 4 x capacity + 8 bytes, both cleared; if either allocation fails both pointers are zero and no stream is loaded |
| pointers | `V:54E5C` key read pointer, `V:54E58` event read pointer, `V:54E4C` record end, `V:54E54` event record pointer | set to the buffer starts (the event read pointer one record further) by every flight start `0x1D4E2` [V: after the demo start, `flow-demo-start-dense.txt` (key pointer = start, then start + 1 after the first step) and `flow-demo-esc.txt`]; `V:54E5C` already holds the buffer start in `credits.vasnap` and `flow-menu.vasnap` (the event read pointer start + 4 bytes of events, the record end the start) [V: `flow-demo-boot.txt`], so a flight start has run once during start-up and menu item 1 works from the first menu |
| loader | `0x18668`, called once at start-up (`0x17DC0`) when the buffers exist | reads `textply` into the key buffer (up to the capacity) and `textctl` into the event buffer (up to 4 x capacity bytes) and sets the record end to the key buffer start; if a file cannot be opened for reading it opens it for writing and closes it, which **creates an empty file of that name** on a writable disk (R) |
| reader | `0x1DFAA` | called by the player step at `0x2A3AC` in every simulated step; section 7.4 |
| executor | `0x1D954` | called by the key poll `0x11524` (at `0x11560` for the replay states `V:4A903` = 1 and 2, `0x11582` for `V:4A903` = 3); section 7.3 |
| recorder | `0x2A1A6` | writes the key byte and the two event words of a step at the record pointers; **dead in play** (7.8) |
| store routines | `0x18732` (writes both files), `0x187BE` (reads both files again) | no caller anywhere in the image (R, section 11) |
| demo states | `0x1215E` (mission-start branch of mode `0x7F`), `0x11B54`, `0x11B9C`, `0x11BD0` and the setup `0x11BF4` | section 7.5 |
| menu item 1 | `0x1EB76` | stores `V:4A84A` = 1 and, only if `V:54E5C` is non-zero, the mode `0x7F` through `0x1EB18` |
| stream end | `0x1EEE0` | section 7.4 rule 5 |
| ESC | `0x1EF50` | section 7.7 |

For **Task 3b**: `tables/demo.bin` is the two files re-packed (the extractor reads `text/textply` and `text/textctl` from the unpacked disk; both are plain files on the OFS volume): `u32 frame_count` (3676), then `frame_count` key bytes, then `frame_count` records of `u16 word1, u16 word2` (little-endian words, the values of the file's big-endian words). The whole stream is exported; the port plays it only up to the first end marker (7.4 rule 5: 2046 frames), the rest is unplayed content (7.9).

### 7.2 The record: one step of the stream

A frame is the triple (key byte, event word 1, event word 2). Frame i of the files is bytes `textply[i]` and `textctl[4 i .. 4 i + 3]`.

**The key byte** is exactly the player record's control byte `+0x65` (`V:4B2A1`, note 05 section 2): bits 0-1 throttle (1 up, 2 down), bits 2-3 roll (4 right, 8 left), bits 4-5 stick (0x10 forward, 0x20 back), bits 6-7 rudder (0x40 right, 0x80 left). The reader stores it into the record and the ordinary control code of the step turns it into the commands `+0x28..+0x2A` as for a pressed key. In the 2046 played frames [V: counted from the shipped file] the byte takes 23 values (0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10, 16, 17, 18, 20, 21, 24, 25, 26, 32, 33, 34, 36, 37, 40, 41); bit counts: throttle up in 408 frames, throttle down 38, roll right 287, roll left 347, stick forward 236, stick back 172, rudder 0; 896 frames carry 0; no frame has both throttle bits. The throttle presets (F-keys, `V:4A928`) and the pointer lever are not part of a frame: in replay the throttle follows the two key-byte bits only (the preset block of the step is skipped for `V:4A903` = 3, `0x1E092`).

**The two event words.** The first word goes to `V:4AA4E`, the second to `V:4AA50`. Each bit stands for one key handler body (the part of the key handler `0x1DAA0` that runs when that key is pressed), recorded when the live handler ran it, and the executor `0x1D954` runs the same body again in the replay. 7.3 lists the bits.

### 7.3 The event bits and the executor

The executor takes the **first** set bit in the order below, clears it, runs its body and returns; the poll calls it again until both words are zero (replay state `V:4A903` = 3). Order: word 1 high byte bit 8 to bit 15, word 1 low byte bits 0 to 3, then word 2 high byte bits 8 to 15, then word 2 low byte bits 0 to 7. A set bit in the low byte of word 1 above bit 3 (mask `0x00F0`) is not an event: the executor posts the notice `0x33` and returns without clearing it, so the poll would loop for ever (R: no frame of the shipped stream has such a bit). In the replay states `V:4A903` = 1 and 2 only word 1 is executed; in `V:4A903` = 3 both. (Here and in the rig check "mode" means the replay state `V:4A903`, not the game mode byte `V:4A95E`.) The bodies are executed with the **live** state (shift flag `V:4A930` clear in the demo, the current view, the current weapon byte), so the same bit does different things in different views, as the key does.

| word | bit | value | body | the key that records it | what the body does in the demo | uses in the 2046 played frames |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- | --- | --- | ---: |
| 1 | 8 | 0x0100 | `0x1E93E` | G | gear toggle (note 05 section 8) | 2 |
| 1 | 9 | 0x0200 | `0x1E342` | A | hook toggle (F/A-18 only) | 0 |
| 1 | 10 | 0x0400 | `0x1DF48` | SPACE press | fire (gun and selected weapon; ignored with the shift flag) | 1 |
| 1 | 11 | 0x0800 | `0x1DF58` | SPACE release | stop firing | 1 |
| 1 | 12 | 0x1000 | `0x1E8A6` | RETURN | in modes other than 2 and 0x7D: the weapon-select cycle | 1 |
| 1 | 13 | 0x2000 | `0x1DE52` | E with the shift flag | eject (without the shift flag: nothing) | 0 |
| 1 | 14 | 0x4000 | `0x1DEF4` | R | radar range step (the low nibble of `+0x63`: 9 to 0xD to 0xB to 9) | 1 |
| 1 | 15 | 0x8000 | `0x1DED0` | T | target request (`V:4A871` = 1), click | 1 |
| 1 | 0 | 0x0001 | `0x1E308` | `\` or BACKSPACE | brake toggle (`W0` bit 0x0800), clears the throttle preset and the lever | 1 |
| 1 | 1 | 0x0002 | `0x1EE0C` | F | flare | 0 |
| 1 | 2 | 0x0004 | `0x1EE9E` | C | chaff | 0 |
| 1 | 3 | 0x0008 | `0x1EF1E` | J | ECM toggle | 0 |
| 2 | 8 | 0x0100 | `0x1ECB8` | M | map view toggle | 0 |
| 2 | 9 | 0x0200 | `0x1E398` | KP-ENTER | inside/outside view toggle (the body continues the key's body after its first instruction) | 20 |
| 2 | 10 | 0x0400 | `0x1E3EE` | SHIFT and KP-ENTER | fixed-camera view | 0 |
| 2 | 11 | 0x0800 | `0x1E51C` | KP5 in the cockpit view; `]` in an outside view | cockpit: head forward request; outside: the camera one distance level closer (`V:4A83D` minus 1, at least -1) | 15 |
| 2 | 12 | 0x1000 | `0x1E624` | KP8 | as the key (head turn in the cockpit, camera direction outside; note 04) | 4 |
| 2 | 13 | 0x2000 | `0x1E4AC` | KP9 | as the key | 2 |
| 2 | 14 | 0x4000 | `0x1E68C` | KP6 | as the key | 17 |
| 2 | 15 | 0x8000 | `0x1E4C6` | KP3 | as the key | 3 |
| 2 | 0 | 0x0001 | `0x1E640` | KP2 | as the key | 6 |
| 2 | 1 | 0x0002 | `0x1E6D8` | KP1 | as the key | 6 |
| 2 | 2 | 0x0004 | `0x1E65C` | KP4 | as the key | 17 |
| 2 | 3 | 0x0008 | `0x1E6B6` | KP7 | as the key | 5 |
| 2 | 4 | 0x0010 | `0x1E4E2` | KP5 in an outside view | outside direction 8 (from above) | 1 |
| 2 | 5 | 0x0020 | `0x1E4EE` | KP-dot | outside direction 9 (from below) | 0 |
| 2 | 6 | 0x0040 | `0x1E892` | release of a head or view key (codes 0x9D, 0x9E, 0x9A, 0x9B, 0xAD, 0xAF, 0xBD) | the head returns: `V:4A949` = 5 | 59 |
| 2 | 7 | 0x0080 | `0x1E5BC` | `[` in an outside view | the camera one distance level further | 4 |

**[V, rig]** `replay_check.py` part 4: the executor `0x1D954` was run in the rig with each of the 28 bits alone in the replay states `V:4A903` = 3 and 1 (each body replaced by a return, the program counter watched): the body of the table is the one reached, word 2 is ignored in mode 1, and 300 random sets of bits ran their bodies in the order stated above (356 cases, 0 mismatches). That the body does what the third-to-last column says is read from the bodies (R; the keys' effects are in notes 05 and 06 and section 11 of this note).

The event stream of the shipped demo uses 13 of the 28 bits; 140 of the 2046 played frames carry an event, 27 of them carry two or more; the first is frame 14 (the first view toggle) and the last frame 1981. The 59 releases and the 20 view toggles with the head and direction keys are the camera work of the demo (7.6).

### 7.4 The replay state `V:4A903` and the reader

`V:4A903` (a byte) selects what the player step does with the stream:

| value | meaning | written at | [tag] |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | live play; the recorder is armed only for `V:4A903` 0 or 1 together with `V:4A848` non-zero. `V:4A848` is set to 1 by the demo setup (`0x11C24`) and stays non-zero for the whole demo flight (it is cleared by the next mission initialisation, `0x1D35A`; the flight-time booking of 10.7 and the leave test of `0x11748` rely on it), but the demo runs with `V:4A903` = 3; every flight with `V:4A903` = 0 has `V:4A848` = 0. **So no state has both, and nothing is ever recorded** | start-up `0x1169A`, the menu `0x11A84`, the non-demo ESC branch `0x11B34` | V (the value in every free-flight capture), R |
| 3 | replay: the key byte and both event words of each step come from the stream | the demo setup `0x11B62` and the stream end `0x1EF0A` | V (`demo.vasnap`; `flow-demo-*.txt`) |
| 4 | the record buffer is full (only the recorder can write it) | `0x2A200` | R, unreachable |
| 1, 2 | developer modes: 1 reads the stream but re-records the second event word over it; 2 reads it and executes word 1 only; both skip the control block of the key handler | nowhere in the image | R: no store of 1 or 2 exists; `0x1DFB4` handles 1 to 3, `0x11544` lists 1 and 2 |

The reader (`0x1DFAA`, once per simulated step, before the player's control code) does the following for `V:4A903` = 3.
