The five rules were compared with the original's code in the rig [V: `replay_check.py`, parts 1 and 2: the shipped stream for 2051 consecutive steps (the 2046 played frames, the end and the first five steps after it) and 300 + 2000 random streams (modes 3 and 1, random markers, wraps and record-end pointers; 19,129 + 126,826 frames), comparing the key byte `V:4B2A1`, the words `V:4AA4E` and `V:4AA50`, both pointers, `V:4A903` and `V:4A8E2` after every call: 0 mismatches].

1. The key byte at the key pointer is stored into the record (`V:4B2A1`) and the pointer advances by one. The two words at the event pointer are stored into `V:4AA4E` and `V:4AA50` (`V:4A903` = 1: only the first) and the event pointer advances by four bytes. **At the first step of a flight the key pointer is at index 0 and the event pointer at index 1**: step s (s = 0, 1, 2...) uses key byte s and the event words of index s + 1. The words are executed by the poll at the start of the **next** pass, before the next step's reader runs, so the events of index s are executed before step s and the words of index 0 are never executed. (This is how the recorder wrote them: a step's key byte was written together with the event bits that the live key handler set in the same pass before the step.) [V: `flow-demo-events.txt`, `demo.vasnap` at stream step 47, one group of peeks per blank: the step that read key index 59 loaded the second event word `0x2000` (stream index 60) at blank 164; at blank 178, the next pass, the executor had run its body (the outside-view direction `V:4A96A` changed from 7 to 1) and cleared the word, and the step of that pass read key index 60 (pointer 61); the same pairing held for every pointer value from 48 to 67.]
2. If the event pointer has reached the end of the event array (4 x capacity bytes), it returns to the start of the array.
3. End test, after the key pointer has advanced: if the three bytes at the key pointer are all 0xFF, or if the key pointer equals the record end (`V:54E4C`, which a flight start sets to the buffer start, so it is only met again after a wrap), the stream has ended (rule 5). Otherwise, if the key pointer has reached the capacity, it returns to the start with the event pointer at index 1 and the end test is made again at once. For the shipped stream the capacity (7812) is never reached: the end marker comes first.
4. The first end marker of the shipped key stream is the four bytes 0xFF at offsets 2046 to 2049. The reader at step 2045 advances the pointer to 2046, finds three 0xFF and ends: **2046 frames (indices 0 to 2045) are played**. [V: rig part 1; emulator `flow-demo-end-staged.txt`: with the pointers moved to step 2040 the leave request appears in the step that reads index 2045.]
5. Stream end: if the key pointer is non-zero (a stream exists) the reader sets the pointers to key index 1 and event index 2 (`0x1EEE0`; the flight start will reset them again), sets `V:4A903` = 3 and the leave-flight request `V:4A8E2` = 1, which is exactly the request that ESC makes (7.7). Nothing else happens in the step: the step that read the last frame completes normally.

The unplayed rest of the files: the key bytes 2050 to 3675 and the events after index 2049 (three more markers, at 2393, 2433 and 2749) are never reached (7.9).

### 7.5 The demonstration start (menu item 1)

Per blank, from the emulator [V: `flow-demo-start-dense.txt`, 380 consecutive blanks from `flow-menu.vasnap`; `flow-demo-start.txt`, the same at lower resolution to blank 700; the menu key 1 pressed at blank 3 of the script was handled at blank 10]. The time unit of the countdown `V:4AB8E` is the **pass** (section 5.1, 3.8); the "passes" column is the number of passes with the countdown not negative, the wait ends with the pass that finds it negative.

| blank (0 = first sample with the mode stored) | state (flat) | what it shows and does | blanks in the state | passes |
| --- | --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| 0 | `0x11E04` (the menu) | the key handler has stored the mode byte `V:4A95E` = `0x7F` and `V:4A84A` = 1 (menu key 1; the key is ignored if `V:54E5C` is zero); the menu pass echoes the chosen item: text id 0x65, and sets the countdown to 210 (`0xD2`; 150 if bit 7 of `V:4AC13` is clear, R) | 4 | 1 |
| 4 | `0x1201E` | the echo line is typed out (text only); the countdown runs 210 to 0 | 37 | 211 |
| 41 | `0x1201E` body, then `0x11B54` | the mission-start dispatch (section 6.1) runs for the mode `0x7F`: sounds stopped, the allowance `V:4A94F` = 3 and the tries byte `V:4A950` = 2 are stored (the demo uses neither), the carrier is placed by `0x2CE36` for the row of the modes `0x7E`/`0x7F` (position 286,785,536 / 0 / 286,785,536, heading 28600, note 05 17.17 rule 5), `V:4A900` = 3, `V:4A901` = 0x11, the gate `V:4A84D` = 1, then `V:4A866` = 1 (pause) and the state `0x11B54` (`0x1215E`) | - | 1 |
| 41 | `0x11B54` | waits for the countdown (4 at entry) to go negative; each pass runs the world-drawing body `0x34802`; the countdown falls by one per pass | 100 | 5 |
| 141 | `0x11B54` body | the demo setup `0x11BF4` runs once: it copies `V:4A95F` to `V:4A960` and clears it, stores `V:4A900` = 3 (the deck start; the store of 4 two instructions before is overwritten), clears `V:4A966` and `V:4A865`, sets `V:4A848` = 1, places the carrier again (`0x2CE36`, mode `0x7F`), **stores the mode byte 3** (`0x11C32`), starts the flight (`0x1D4E2`: the player at the deck start of note 05 17.14, the replay pointers reset, the view selector 0), opens the gate; then `V:4A903` = 3, `V:4A83D` = 0, the countdown 2, the fade target 15 and level 0 | - | 1 |
| 142 | `0x11B9C` | the screen fades in: the level `V:4A958` rises 0 to 15, one level per 3 blanks (142 to 186); the countdown runs out in 3 passes (the gate `V:4A84D` goes to 0 at blank 172), then the state waits for the level to equal the target | 44 | 3 |
| 186 | `0x11BD0` | the countdown 2 runs out; `V:4A866` = 0 and the gate 1 | 2 | 3 |
| 188 | `0x12D58` | the flight-start tail shared with every mission (section 6.2): the view is the cockpit (`V:4A83D` = 0), the camera flags are cleared, the countdown is set to 5 | 7 | 1 |
| 195 | `0x12DB8` | the countdown 5 stored by `0x12D58` runs out (gate 1, the world drawn every pass); then the gate falls and the notice ids 0x58 (the outcome lookup, mode 3 takes the branch of modes 3 to 8, `0x12DE6`) and 0x5A (the launch from the carrier: the carrier start sets bit 3 of `B4`; 0x59 otherwise) are posted | 36 | 6 |
| 231 | `0x12E4E` | the two notice records are typed out (text only) until the engine byte `V:4A898` goes negative: **301 typing passes in 84 blanks, then the end hold of 47 passes in 8 blanks** (the engine byte is 1 from blank 84 and 0x81 at blank 92 when the state acts): 348 passes, 92 blanks in all. The list at the entry of the state is [0x58, 0x5A]: the outcome lookup `0x29534` returned a non-negative value in the demo (the mode byte is 3 when this state runs), so id 0x58 is posted, then the carrier-launch notice 0x5A [V: `flow-demo-notice.txt`, per blank, the text list read at the state's entry; the pass counter `V:4A879` and the countdown agree: 301 = 45 + 256] | 92 | 348 |
| 325 | `0x12EDA` | four passes with the countdown 3 to 0, **each a simulated step**: the first reads stream index 0 (the key pointer leaves the buffer start at blank 326) | 27 | 4 |
| 352 | `0x12EFE` | the flight state: one step per pass; the step counter n counts from 4 | - | |

The times of the later rows are the ones of the mission-start tail (section 6.2); the demo differs from the other modes only in the states `0x11B54`, `0x11B9C`, `0x11BD0` and in the stream. From the stored mode to the first step the original takes 326 blanks (6.5 s). The pass rate of a text-only state is not constant: the two records of `0x12E4E` need the same number of passes (301 of typing) but took 94 blanks in the first run, 90 in the ESC run, 30 in the staged run of 7.7 and 84 in the pass-counted run (3.2, 3.2, 9.6 and 3.6 passes per blank), so the durations in blanks are one machine run each and the pass counts are the rule (section 3.8). The port converts these waits by section 5's rules (passes, 37.5 per step in text-only states, one per step where the world is drawn); with the world-drawing states `0x11B54` (5 passes + the transition pass) and `0x11BD0` (3 passes) taking one pass per step, the demo start is: echo line (211 text passes = 5.6 steps), 6 steps, fade (15 levels at 3 blanks = 45 blanks = 7 steps), 3 steps, the tail of section 6.2, then the stream. **C** for the pass-to-step conversion; the blank counts are V.

**Mode bytes.** The demo goes through three values of `V:4A95E`: `0x7F` from the menu key to the setup `0x11BF4`, then **3** for the whole flight (the demo snapshot reads 3, note 05 17.17 rule 5; the crash allowance `V:4A94F` reads 3 in it, so the demo's flight would be ended by the third crash). The value `0x7E` is never stored by any instruction (the stores of the byte, scanned by operand: `0x1199E`, `0x11A7E`, `0x11AF8`, `0x11B20`, `0x11C32`, `0x11F74`, `0x12016`, `0x1360A`, `0x1EB18`): it exists only as the twin of `0x7F` in the carrier placement (`0x2CE5E`, `0x2CEF6`). **A port needs only the values 0x7F (demo set-up) and 3 (demo flight).**

The carrier of the demo is the one of note 05 17.17 rule 5: record 14 at (286,785,536, 0, 286,785,536), heading **28600**, placed by `0x2CE36` in both calls (the second, from `0x11BF4`, repeats the first because `V:4A8E3` is zero); the player is at the carrier start of note 05 17.14 (`V:4A900` = 3): the carrier position plus ((11 M0 + 104 M2) >> 4, (11 M6 + 104 M8) >> 4) = (+15,900, +105,900) for this heading, y = 30,472; the aircraft is the F/A-18 (`V:4A901` = 0x11, stored by the start-up `0x1D1B4` and by the dispatcher `0x1210A`, read in every capture of the demo: `ac` = 0x11 in 3 runs [V]).

### 7.6 In the demo: views, keys and what runs

- **Views.** The flight starts in the cockpit view (`V:4A83D` = 0, 7.5). The stream switches views by events only: 20 KP-ENTER toggles (the first at frame 14), the head and direction keys of the keypad with 59 releases, `[` and `]`. The mode byte does not change (it stays 3). There is no other mode or view switch: no frame of the stream selects `0x7E` or `0x7F` (the stream cannot write the mode byte; only the setup and the leave flow do).
- **Live keys.** In replay only the system keys act (section 11.1, phase R): P (pause, not paused by the stream), ESC (7.7), the shift and Amiga flags, Z and DEL (the mouse server re-install, no effect), RETURN in the wait states, and the release codes of the arrows and `,` `.` `-` `=` (they can clear the control bits of the record for the rest of that step; the next reader call overwrites the key byte). The digit keys, F-keys, the flight keys and the view keys are ignored (section 11.2 phase F is skipped when `V:4A903` = 3). The mouse does not move the throttle (the pointer block of the step is skipped).
- **What does not run.** The mission-seconds counter does not add the demo's flight time to the log (`0x11748` tests `V:4A848`), the mission scripts' dispatcher is skipped (`0x28550` tests `V:4A848` too), the recorder does not run and the countercode is not touched. **The log record is touched, however**: the weapon, crash and hit counters are incremented by the same code as in play with no demo test. The shipped stream has one fire event (frames 599 to 602) with the AIM-120 selected: the counter `+0x3E` (AIM-120 fired) goes from 0 to 1 and the dirty flag `V:4A87D` from 0 to 1 [V: `flow-demo-log.txt`, the 78-byte record read before the event, after stream frame 599, after frame 612 and at frame 2000: at the last reading only `+0x3E` (1) differs from zero, the flight time `+0x08` is 0 and the allowance 3]; the counters stay in the buffer and reach the file at the next of the four save points of 10.8. The crash counters (`+0x46` at `0x16FB0` and `0x2A8C6`, `+0x10` at `0x12F44`) are likewise not gated by the demo, but the shipped run has no hard touchdown or crash [V: section 7.8], so they stay unchanged in it. The weapons, targets and the radar run as in flight but the milestone-5 modules decide what the T, R and SPACE events do (7.3).
- **Pause.** P pauses the demo like a flight; while paused no step runs, so the stream pointers stand still and the events are not executed.
- **A crash** (the replayed controls diverge from the recording if the port's flight model differs): the crash flow of note 05 17.17 runs, the allowance (3) falls, the restart keeps the stream pointers (the restart does not reset them: 17.17 rule 4) and the stream goes on; the third crash ends in the game-over screen. The shipped demo does not crash in the original [V: `flow-demo-full.txt`, section 7.8].

### 7.7 Leaving and ending: ESC, SHIFT-ESC and the end of the stream

ESC (raw `0x45`, handler `0x1EF50`) sets the leave request `V:4A8E2` to 1, or to -1 with the shift flag. The stream end (7.4 rule 5) sets it to 1. Both are consumed by the same pass routine (`0x11748`) and run the ESC flow of section 5.11. At its third screen `0x11AE2` the flow looks at the replay state: with `V:4A903` = 3 it does **not** return to the menu and does not restart the "selection" as for other modes, it restarts the demo (`0x11B00`-`0x11B2C`): `V:4A866` = 1, the countdown 2, the fade target 0 (`0x11B1A`), the mode byte `0x7F`, the state `0x11B54`; the setup `0x11BF4` then runs as in 7.5 and the stream starts again from index 0. SHIFT-ESC (request -1) takes the other branch of `0x11748` (`0x11838`-`0x1184A`): `V:4A8E2` and `V:4A8E3` are cleared and the state is `0x11A70`, which clears the mode, `V:4A964` and `V:4A903` and returns to the menu (R; section 5.4).

Per blank [V: `flow-demo-esc.txt`, ESC pressed at blank 3 of the script, handled by the pass at blank 12; `flow-demo-end-staged.txt`, the stream end, blanks counted from the step that reads the last frame]:

| blank | ESC in the demo | stream end (staged at step 2040) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | the step in progress | the step that reads index 2045 starts (key pointer 2045) |
| 12 / 14 | ESC consumed: `0x11A96`, gate 0, pause 1, countdown 3, fade target 0 (blank 12) | the step has ended: key pointer 1, `V:4A8E2` = 1 (blank 14) |
| - / 27 | | `0x11A96` (gate 0, pause 1, fade target 0) |
| 12 to 43 / 30 to 58 | fade out: 16 levels, one per 2 blanks | fade out 15 to 0, one per 2 blanks |
| 44 / 59 | `0x11AC4` (countdown 2) | `0x11AC4` |
| 46 / 61 | `0x11AE2`, gate 1 | `0x11AE2`, gate 1 |
| 61 / 75 | the pass body of `0x11AE2` has run: mission initialisation `0x1D1BE` (the step counter n is cleared), gate 0, pause 0 | the same |
| 68 / 82 | mode `0x7F`, state `0x11B54`, `V:4A900` 0, pause 1; the pointers read the buffer starts (key index 0, event index 1) | the same |
| 77 / 91 | `0x11B9C`: setup done, mode 3, `V:4A900` 3, `V:4A903` 3, view 0, fade target 15 | the same |
| 79 to 121 / 93 to 135 | fade in: one level per 3 blanks | the same |
| 122 / 136 | `0x11BD0` | the same |
| 124 / 138 | `0x12D58`, gate 1, pause 0 | the same |
| 130 / 144 | `0x12DB8` | the same |
| 170 / 161 | `0x12E4E` (text) | the same |
| 260 / 191 | `0x12EDA`, the first replayed step (key pointer 1) | the same |
| 285 / 202 | `0x12EFE`, the flight state, n from 4 | the same |

**ESC during the start sequence** [V: `flow-demo-esc-echo.txt`, `-wait.txt`, `-fade.txt`, `-tick.txt`: menu key 1, then ESC in the state named, one group of peeks per blank]. The leave request is acted on in every one of the demo's states, with the same fade-out and screens as in flight (5.11); only the destination differs, and it depends on `V:4A903` at the third screen `0x11AE2`: **before the setup `0x11BF4` has run** (`V:4A903` = 0: the typed echo line `0x1201E`, the wait `0x11B54`) the flow goes to `0x11E04` with the mode still `0x7F`, which restarts the selection, that is the echo line, the dispatch and the whole demo start again (echo run: ESC handled at blank 1, `0x11AE2` at 33, `0x11E04` at 54, `0x1201E` again at 60, `0x11B54` at 99, `0x11B9C` at 199, the tail state `0x12D58` at 247; wait run likewise: ESC at 16, `0x11E04` at 71, `0x1201E` at 77); **after it** (`V:4A903` = 3: the fade `0x11B9C`, the three-tick wait `0x12EDA`, the flight) it restarts the demo directly (fade run: ESC at blank 6, the fade-out from level 7 to 0 by blank 20, `0x11AE2` at 22, mode `0x7F` and `0x11B54` at 38, `0x11B9C` at 47 with the setup done, the first replayed step at 230 and the flight state at 255; tick run: ESC at blank 6 after one step (n = 1), `0x11B54` at 54, flight state at 270). The stream pointers read the buffer starts again (key index 0, event index 1) in every restart; the step counter is cleared by `0x1D1BE`. Nothing is skipped and the menu is never reached by ESC. SHIFT-ESC returns to the menu from every state (5.11).

**The restart's wait in steps** [V: `flow-demo-restart-passes.txt`, the pass counter `V:4A879` per blank after ESC]. From the pass of `0x11AE2` that runs the mission initialisation (counter value 0, the step counter cleared) to the pass of `0x11B54` that runs the demo set-up `0x11BF4` there are **three passes** (counter values 1, 2, 3 at blanks 66, 70 and 74 of that log: four blanks each, 16 blanks from the reset at blank 59 to the set-up pass's effect at blank 75); in a world-drawing state the port runs one pass per step, so the wait is **3 steps**, not 4. The 16 blanks convert to 3 by the blank rule as well ((16 x 8 + 25) / 50 = 3).

**The fade level at an ESC in the fade-in, settled** [V: `followup-demo-esc.txt`, 3 runs from the menu snapshot, key 1, ESC sent 15, 18 and 21 blanks after the state `0x11B9C` was first seen (T), the fade bytes and the 32 colour registers per blank]. The fade-in level is floor(T / 3) (5, 6 and 7 at the three keys; the colour registers follow `V:4A958` in the same blank). The ESC is acted on at the **next pass**, and the passes of the fade-in state fall at T = 3 + 9 m (21 and 30 in the runs: the first two keys, at T = 15 and 18, were consumed at T = 21, the third, at T = 21, just missed that pass and was consumed at T = 30; the pass length is the 9 blanks of a world-drawing pass). The fade-out therefore starts from the level of the consuming pass: **7, 7 and 10**. The target is set to 0 at the consuming pass; the level first falls **one blank later** and then every second blank, reaching 0 at 2 L - 1 blanks after the consuming pass (L = 7: 13 blanks; L = 10: 19 blanks); `0x11AC4` follows 1 blank after level 0, the gate is 1 for 8 blanks and the reset (pause byte 0) comes 24 blanks (L = 7) and 29 blanks (L = 10) after the consuming pass, 2 L + 10 and 2 L + 9. **Rule: the level at the fade-out start is floor(T_b / 3) with T_b the first pass boundary 3 + 9 m strictly after the ESC; it replaces the fixed latency of 13 blanks (6 for an ESC at blank 6 against the 7 measured; the new rule gives 7).** The 28-to-32-blank spread of the ESC fade-out from level 15 (5.11) is the same phase effect: the first drop comes 1 to 3 blanks after the target changed (1 blank here, where the fade timer was already running; 3 in the flight table), so the fade-out from level L lasts 2 L - 1 to 2 L + 1 blanks and the total to the reset varies by the pass boundary; the port takes the middle (2 L + 1, 5.11) and the spread stays a measured +-2 blanks, not a rule. **Two more levels and a correction** [V: `followup-combat-batch-g.txt` part `demo-esc`, three more runs, ESC sent 1, 6 and 31 blanks after the state first ran]: ESC at 1 and at 6 were both consumed at the pass at T = 12 (level **4**, as the rule T_b = 3 + 9 m gives), and the fade-out from level 4 reached level 0 8 blanks after the consuming pass and the reset (pause byte 0) came **21 blanks** after it (13 blanks after level 0). An ESC at 31 was consumed at level **11**, not 13: after the pass at T = 30 the next boundary came at about T = 34, not 39 (the end of the fade-in, where the gate alternates), so the rule holds for T_b = 12, 21 and 30 only; from level 11 the fade-out reached 0 after 23 blanks and the reset came **32 blanks** after the consuming pass (10 after level 0). The measured reset delays are therefore **21, 24, 29 and 32 blanks for the levels 4, 7, 10 and 11** (fade-out 2 L blanks, the first drop one blank after the target is set, then a tail of 13, 11, 10 and 10 blanks: the gate pulse and the pass phase); levels 1 and 13 were not reached (no pass boundary falls there). For the port: take the consumed level from the ESC time by the boundaries 12, 21, 30 and 34 after the state's first pass (levels 4, 7, 10, 11), the delay from this table, and treat any other level as an interpolation (open).

**ESC after the 34-blank boundary and before the fade-in ends, settled** [V: `note0708-demo-esc-late.txt`, ESC sent 36, 39 and 42 blanks after the state first ran (T), one run each, the level `V:4A958`, the target, the state, the gate and the pause byte every blank]. In this window **the passes come every blank or so** (the ESC was consumed within one blank of the key in all three runs: the target `V:4A959` fell to 0 one blank after the ESC), so the level at the consuming pass is floor(T / 3): **12, 13 and 14** for T = 36, 39 and 42 (the level fell by one a blank later and then every second blank, as in the other runs), reaching 0 after 23, 25 and 27 blanks (2 L - 1), the state `0x11AC4` one blank later and the reset (pause byte 0) **33, 35 and 37 blanks after the consuming pass (2 L + 9)**; the demo restart followed as in the table above. So an ESC after the boundary at 34 does **not** take the generic chain from level 15: it fades out from its own level, 12 to 14 up to the end of the fade-in (level 15 at about T = 44 to 45, the state `0x11BD0` at 122 blanks into the log of the table above), with the reset delay 2 L + 9 (the level 11 of the boundary 34 had 2 L + 10: 32 blanks). **Port rule (C): after the boundary 34 the consumed level is floor(T / 3) (T the blanks since the state's first pass, the ESC consumed in the next blank), the delay 2 L + 9 after the consuming pass, until level 15 is reached; the generic chain starts at level 15 only.** The port's "generic chain" for this window is a deviation (a fade-out from level 15 lasts 28 to 32 blanks, 5.11, against 23 to 27 here).

**Levels 13 and 14 and the boundary rows: UNOBSERVABLE at step resolution (milestone 7, Task 2; the milestone-6 follow-up item closed as unobservable).** The three captures above fix the rule at blank resolution (the levels 12, 13 and 14 at T = 36, 39 and 42, the reset 2 L + 9 blanks after the consuming pass). At the port's resolution the step is 6.25 blanks and the consuming pass comes within one blank of the key in this window, so a key in the steps that fall past the boundary has T = 37 (one step) in the window before the fade-in ends at about T = 44 to 45; the rows T = 36, 39 and 42 fall inside two steps whose levels (12 and 13) differ by one colour step that is never seen on its own (the fade-out reaches 0 after 2 L - 1 blanks, 23 or 25, which are 4 steps either way), and the levels 1 and 13 are not reachable as boundary rows. **No capture whose resolution is a step can settle them, and no script of the port can tell them apart: the rule stays the formula floor(T / 3) read at blank resolution, and the levels 13 and 14 at T = 39 and 42 are stated unobservable in steps.** The scripts `note0708-demo-esc-late.txt` and `followup-demo-esc.txt` are the whole of the evidence.

So the demo restarts after about 270 blanks (5.4 s) from the ESC pass, and about 190 blanks (3.8 s) from the end of the last step; the leave request is acted on at the **next pass**, whose length is that of a world-drawing pass (the 13 blanks between blank 14 and 27 in the staged run). Section 5.11 holds the general ESC rules (the screens, the gate pulse); the demo differs only in the destination. [V: 2 runs]

### 7.8 Rate, the recorded run and the port's conversion

**The stream is per step, not per blank.** A frame is read by the reader once per simulated player step (7.4); the original's step rate varies between 4 and 11 steps per second with the scene (note 05 section 14; in the demo, between the checkpoints of `flow-demo-full.txt`, 6.8 to 12.8 blanks per step, that is 3.9 to 7.3 steps per second [V]). The port's fixed step of 8 per second therefore **needs no conversion of the stream**: frame i is applied at core step i; the demo plays 2046 steps = 255.75 s of port time (the original takes about 400 s, 6.7 minutes, at its 3.9 to 7.3 steps per second in this flight). The *screens* around the flight (7.5, 7.7) are timed in passes and blanks and use the rounding rule of note 05 17.17: `(blanks * 8 + 25) / 50` where a screen is given in blanks.

**The recorded run as a check for Task 6** [V: `flow-demo-full.txt`, one run from `demo.vasnap` (stream step 47) to step 2044 on the reference machine]. The demo is a take-off from the carrier, a climb to about 1,020,000 position units (5,000 feet) in a left-hand circuit, a descent and a landing with rollout on the runway of the first airfield: no crash (`W0` bit 0x0200 never set, the allowance `V:4A94F` stays 3, the mode byte 3 and `V:4A903` = 3 at every checkpoint) and at steps 2040 and 2044 the aircraft stands on the ground (y = 1800, `W2` 0x0080, brake on, speed zero) while the last frames run out.

**What a row is.** The log is run to the blank at which the step counter `V:4A992` first equals N (`stepuntil`); there the key pointer reads N + 1 in 20 of 21 rows (in one row, N = 200, it still read N at that blank and N + 1 three blanks later), that is the reader of step N has run. The player record was read at that blank and again three blanks later: the positions and attitudes were equal in 17 of the 21 rows (the step's motion was already done) and differed in 4 (N = 200, 400, 1200, 1700: the first sample was inside the step), so **the table gives the second sample, taken with the key pointer at N + 1: the state after step N, that is after N + 1 simulated steps (stream frames 0 to N consumed)**. In port terms: after the core's step with index N (counting from 0), `x`, `y`, `z`, pitch, heading and roll must equal the row. The player record's words are +0x14, +0x18, +0x1C (position) and +0x66, +0x68, +0x6A (attitude) in the engine's units (note 05).
