**Inputs of an exact comparison.** (a) The landmark phase of the step has one machine-dependent input, the constant z of note 05 section 11b (note 05 line 240: 48 on the reference machine, 1 in the rig image): the demo lands on airfield 1, so the final rows (from about step 1300) depend on it; the port uses the reference machine's value 48 for this comparison. (b) The first 47 steps have no checkpoint: the snapshot starts at step 47, and the carrier start of step 0 is the one of note 05 17.14 at heading 28600. Task 6 plays the stream from step 0 through its flight model and compares from step 100 on; an exact port of note 05 reproduces the rows exactly, because the stream is per step and the step does not depend on the display. The rows are facts of the original's run, not tolerances: the port's own step rate (8 per second) does not change step-indexed values. The original spent 18,944 blanks on the 1,944 steps from step 100 to 2044 in the first run of this capture (9.75 blanks per step on average; 6.8 to 12.8 blanks per step between two checkpoints, that is 3.9 to 7.3 steps per second), so the whole demo takes it about 400 s.

### 7.9 Unplayed content of the stream, the recorder and the stores

The files hold 3676 frames, of which the first 2046 are played. Frames 2046 to 2049 are the 0xFF marker; 2050 to 2392, 2397 to 2432, 2437 to 2748 and 2753 to 3675 are further takes of the same kind (keys of the same value set, 16 frames with events) separated by three more markers; no rule of the reader can reach them (rule 3 stops at the first marker, a flight start always begins at index 0) [V: rig part 1 and the emulator]. The authors recorded several sessions into the buffer with the pause key (the pause body writes the four 0xFF bytes at the record pointer while recording, `0x1EDE0`) and saved the buffer; the shipped demo is the first session. The recorder, the pause marker, the record buffer overflow (mode 4) and the stores are unreachable in play (7.1, 7.4); the loader creates the two files empty when they are missing (7.1). All of this is listed in section 11 as unreferenced content.

### 7.10 What the port does (Task 6, rules)

1. Extract the stream (Task 3b, 7.1); play frames 0 to 2045; stop at the first run of three 0xFF key bytes if the shipped file ever differs (the rule is the reader's: 7.4 rules 3 and 4), and treat a stream without a marker as ending at its length.
2. Per core step s: before the step, execute the event words of index s (not for s = 0) in the order of 7.3, through the same code paths as the keys (the port's view, gear, brake, flare, radar and fire handlers), with the live shift state clear; then apply the key byte s as the player's control byte for the step.
3. The mode byte is 3 during the flight; the replay flag stops the live control keys (11.1 phase R).
4. At the end of frame 2045 request the ESC flow as for ESC (section 5.11) with the destination "restart the demo"; ESC and the end share it; SHIFT-ESC returns to the menu.
5. The start sequence of 7.5 is the demo's own part of the mission start; the port may shorten the briefing line wait only by the conversion of 7.5 (C).
6. The recorder, the stores and the developer modes are not ported (section 11).

## 8. Training

The two training items of the main menu are the two parts of one flight school: **part 1** (menu key 3) lets the player watch an instructor aircraft fly the seven manoeuvres in a loop, and **part 2** (menu key 4, "part 2") puts the player behind the same instructor, who flies the manoeuvres one at a time on a trigger while the player follows. There is **no detection of the player's flying**: nothing in the code reads the player's attitude, rates or time to decide whether a manoeuvre was "done", there is no score, no pass or fail and no time limit on the player. The lessons are driven by the instructor's own state machine (section 8.5), by two keys (RETURN and the fire key), and end only when the player leaves with ESC. The plan's "detection rules per manoeuvre" are therefore the instructor's **driver rules** (the conditions on the instructor's own attitude by which each state passes to the next), and the "success and failure outcomes" reduce to prompts and the allowance of the flight (8.9). Rig count for the driver rules and the sequencer: `training_check.py` and `instructor_check.py` (8.11).

Addresses are flat image addresses (note 00); `V:xxxxx` is a variable at `0x4xxxx`; "record" is a 0x200-byte entity record of the table at `0x4B23C` (note 05), record 0 the player, record 4 (`0x4BA3C`) the instructor. Angles are the engine's words: 28,800 per turn, so 80 units per degree. A "step" is one pass of the world update (note 05 section 14: the original runs about 7 per second in flight; the port 8); every counter below counts steps, so no blank conversion is needed.

### 8.1 Where the two parts start

| item | key and writer | mode byte `V:4A95E` | security prompt | briefing (text id) | flight state |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| part 1 | menu digit 3: `0x1EB18` stores index 2 | 2 | yes (V: the prompt appears, answered with the code of the frozen challenge, 8.12) | 9 (`0x12488`) | `0x12EFE` with the camera on record 4 (V: part 1 start) |
| part 2 | menu digit 4: `0x1EB6E` stores 0x7D | 0x7D | yes (V) | 10 (`0x12498`) | `0x12D58` then `0x12EFE`, camera on the player |

Both parts go through the common mission start (`0x12202` for 0x7D, `0x12190` for 2; note 08 sections 5 and 6 own the screens): the briefing text is typed out, the state waits for the end of the typing, and the flight begins without a key press (V: part 1, sample 1 to 27 of the capture `flow-training-part1`; the briefing is not left by a key in this capture). The text ids 9 and 10 are the two briefing screens: part 1's names the keys that act (the keypad for the point of view, RETURN to skip a manoeuvre, ESC to go on to part 2); part 2's gives the throttle to set (80 percent), the task of following the lead aircraft, and the keys RETURN (re-position, then skip) and S (the lead's smoke trail). A third group of ids, 0x66 to 0x6B, are the short screens after ESC, one per mode (`0x11E04`): 0x67 for mode 2 and 0x68 for mode 0x7D.

**Briefing durations** [V: `flow-training-brief1.txt`, `flow-training-brief2.txt`, 1100 consecutive blanks each from the frozen prompts `training-code-3.vasnap` and `training-code-4.vasnap` after the countercode is accepted; blanks counted from the answer]. Part 1: the briefing record (id 9) is posted at blank 24 and typed over the start scene (next paragraph) until the engine byte `V:4A898` becomes 1 at blank 659 (635 blanks), the end hold ends at blank 667 (8 more), `0x12DB8` follows at blank 668 and the flight state (the lead flying) at blank 736. Part 2: the record id 10 is posted at blank 67 and typed until blank 883 (816 blanks of typing), the hold ends at blank 891 and `0x12D58` follows (the player's cockpit view, the common tail of 6.2). The text engine is the generic one of section 3 (the record's colour byte gives the characters-per-pass delay): about 2,230 passes for part 1's record and 3,556 for part 2's (the countdown falls by one per pass), i.e. 3.5 and 4.4 passes per blank in these runs; the port types the records by the engine's rule and needs no separate figure. No key is needed in either part.

**What is drawn behind the briefing, and the stage lists** [V: frames at 300 and 600 blanks of `flow-training-brief1/2`, and the logs]. The briefing text is typed over the **start scene**, not a blank screen: in part 1 the external view of the lead aircraft over the bay (record 4 viewed, `V:4A83D` = 1), in part 2 the runway with the lead ahead of the player's position; the gate `V:4A84D` is 0 while it types (one picture, no animation), so nothing moves behind the text. Stage lists from the countercode's acceptance (the port starts at the first line; blanks from the answer, then the steps by the pass rules of 5.1: text-only states 37.5 passes per step, a world-drawing pass one step):

| stage | part 1 | part 2 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| briefing record posted, typed | blank 24 to 659 (635 blanks; about 2,230 passes: 59 steps) | blank 67 to 883 (816 blanks; about 3,556 passes: 95 steps) |
| end hold | to 667 (8 blanks, 47 passes: 1 step) | to 891 (8 blanks) |
| camera approach of mode 6 (state `0x12D58`, camera mode 6, the map-like approach over the bay; KP-ENTER skips it) | none: the part 1 branch goes from the briefing to `0x12DB8` | blank 899 to 1300 (401 blanks; the view becomes the cockpit at 1312) |
| `0x12DB8` (countdown 5: six world-drawing passes) | blank 668 to 705 (37 blanks: 6 steps) | blank 1324 to 1402 (78 blanks: 6 passes at the long cockpit pass) |
| `0x12E4E` and the notice | an instant (the engine byte is set to 0xFF for the mode 2: no notice) | notice id **0x59** (the take-off notice: the player starts on a runway, bit 3 of `B4` clear) posted at 1402, typed to 1438 (36 blanks), hold to 1444 |
| `0x12EDA` (four steps, the first pass with the simulation on) | blank 705 to 736 (31 blanks: 4 steps) | blank 1444 to 1486 (42 blanks: 4 steps) |
| flight state `0x12EFE` | blank 736 | blank 1486 |
| the **first instructor step** | the first pass of `0x12EDA` (blank 705): the lead is scripted from the start and takes its first pass there (the first pass of a placed record makes no motion, 8.3) | the lead stays unscripted and does nothing until the player's speed is non-zero (8.4 step 1): its first manoeuvre step is the pass after the throttle moves the aircraft |

(Part 1's 68 blanks after `0x12DB8` are the six passes of its countdown (37 blanks), the one pass of `0x12E4E` that acts at once, and the four passes of `0x12EDA` (31 blanks): 668 to 736.) [V: `flow-training-brief1.txt`, `flow-training-brief2.txt`, `flow-training-brief2b.txt`]

**Frames and the lead behind the briefing** [V: `flow-training-brief-frames.txt`; frames `extracted/captures/flow-training-brief1-300.ppm`, `-brief1-600.ppm`, `-brief2-300.ppm`, `-brief2-600.ppm` (git-ignored), described, not committed]. At blank 300 of part 1 the lead is **visible**: a grey aircraft over the bay, nose to the right, behind the first lines of the briefing; at blank 300 of part 2 the lead is visible ahead of the player's view on the runway. In both, record 4 reads W0 `0x11C8` with the cell words (73, 78) and (60, 63), and the cell sub-bytes already set: `+0x0A` = 0x0A in part 1 and 0x0D in part 2 (so not 0): the scene passes of the mission start (`0x123C2`, `0x127C8`, `0x12452`, all gate 1) run the world update, whose first pass computes the sub-bytes (8.3), **before** the briefing's picture is drawn. The original therefore never draws the placed lead with sub-bytes 0 in this flow; whether its drawer would show a record with `+0x0A` = 0 was not tested (the walker's rule for sub-cells is note 04a's). **Port rule (C):** compute the lead's cell sub-bytes at placement time (or run one world pass before the first draw), so that the lead is in the draw list from the first frame of the briefing and not after about 70 steps.

**One conversion rule for `0x12DB8` and the tail** (ruling, **C**; Part A 6.4 to be aligned). `0x12DB8` is a **gate-on** state: it counts **passes, one step each**, as section 5.1 states for every gate-on state: its countdown of 5 is **6 steps**, in every mode and whether or not the approach was skipped. The blank counts (30 + 56 blanks = 5 + 9 steps in 6.4, 4 + 6 in an earlier reading of the part 2 tail) are measurements of how long the original's long passes took, not the rule. The tail after the cockpit view is taken is, in passes [V: the step counter `V:4A992`]: `0x12D58` **2 passes** (part 2: n 0x2E, 0x2F, 0x30 at blanks 1300, 1312, 1324; free flight after a KP-ENTER skip: n 0x33, 0x34 at k = 2 and 14, note 6.2), then `0x12DB8` **6 passes** (n 0x30 to 0x36 in part 2, 0x34 to 0x3A in 6.2), then the notice and its hold (text passes), then `0x12EDA` 4 passes. So the skipped and the unskipped tails are the same 2 + 6 steps; the 14 of the skip is blanks.


**The ESC restart's own delays before the part 2 briefing** [V: `flow-training-esc-post.txt`, part 1 in its flight state, ESC at blank 0, per blank for 200 blanks then per 5; one run]. The restart is not the first-start sequence: after the ESC flow's fade-out and reset (5.11: `0x11A96` at blank 1, the fade 15 to 0 by blank 34, `0x11AE2` at 36 with its reset pass at 43) the state `0x11E04` stores the mode 0x7D at blank 50 and the **echo line (text id 0x68) is typed** from blank 55 (engine byte 1 at 63, 0x81 at 72; the countdown is 150, not 210, because the restart has `V:4A8E3` set), then `0x123C2` at blank 87 (gate 1: the scene is drawn), the fade state `0x123EE` at 172, the prompt scene's set-up `0x127C8` at 220 (the prompt itself is skipped), `0x12452` at 315 and the **briefing record id 10 is posted at blank 395** by `0x124B2`. So the part 2 briefing starts **395 blanks after the ESC** (about 63 steps by the blank rule); from there the stages are those of the table above (typing 816 blanks, hold, the camera approach, `0x12DB8`, `0x12E4E` with the take-off notice, `0x12EDA`). A port that reuses the first start's delays (briefing at blank 67 after the countercode) is shorter by the echo line, the scene and fade stages: 328 blanks.

**ESC and SHIFT-ESC.** In part 1, ESC ends the flight and starts **part 2**: the ESC flow's state at `0x11AE2` rewrites mode 2 as 0x7D (writer `0x11AF8`), the screen shown is the one of mode 0x7D (text 0x68), and the mission starts again as part 2 **without a second security prompt** (V: `flow-training-part1-esc`: ESC at lesson 2, mode 0x7D at the next sample, text 0x68, then 0x0A, then the 0x7D flight state; the sampled state words never show the prompt id). In part 2, ESC **restarts part 2** (V: `flow-training-part2-esc`: the flight state is left, the 0x7D start runs again through briefing 10 to the flight, lesson 0, with no menu and no prompt: the menu's the menu's ESC rule (ESC restarts the selection)). SHIFT-ESC returns to the main menu from either part (V: `flow-training-part1-shiftesc` and `-part2-shiftesc`: mode byte 0, the menu state, text ids 6 and 0x64 at the next samples; note 08 section 5). The keys that act in each part are in 8.8.

**Census: nothing reads the player's flying** [R: a scan of the disassembly of the whole image for these variables]. The writers and readers of the sequencer's variables are `V:4A851` (lesson): read at `0x270F2`, `0x271CC`, `0x27206`, `0x272A4`, `0x272B6`, `0x27338`, `0x27368`, `0x273F0`, `0x27418`, written at `0x2710E` and `0x27402`; `V:4A852` (trigger state): read at `0x1E8BA` (RETURN handler), `0x1F090` (fire handler), `0x270F8`, `0x27326`, `0x3098C`, `0x30A52`, `0x30B8E` (the instructor's end routine and waits), written at `0x27134`, `0x27330`, `0x27362`; `V:54E42` (cursor): read at `0x271E6`, `0x272E6`, `0x27306`, `0x273C4`, written at `0x12514`, `0x1D1D0`, `0x27248`, `0x273E6`, `0x2740E`; `V:4A84B`: written at `0x12E9C`, read at `0x2726C`, `0x30962`, `0x370E6`, `0x37130` (the strip drawer). The compares of `V:4A95E` with 0x7D are at `0x11EA2` (the screen after ESC), `0x12136` and `0x12980` (the mission-start switches), `0x124F2`, `0x1E8B0`, `0x1ED7E` (the S key), `0x1F086` (fire), `0x270DC` and `0x27222` (the sequencer), `0x285AC` (the end check), `0x2CE56` and `0x2D236` (placement) and `0x35F6E`; the compares with 2 are at `0x11422` (external message line), `0x11AF4` (the 2 to 0x7D rewrite), `0x11E8C` (the screen text), `0x12480` and `0x12E98` (briefing and start), `0x1DB22` and `0x1DCB0` (the key groups), `0x1E8A6` (RETURN), `0x27262`, `0x272C2`, `0x27314`, `0x27446` (the sequencer), `0x28558` (the end check does nothing for modes 1 and 2) and `0x2D22A`. Every one of these sites is in the front-end flow, the key handlers, the sequencer, the instructor's AI routine or the HUD; none of them reads the player's pitch, roll, rates, altitude or speed except `0x27238` (the player's indicated speed is tested for zero to start the instructor in part 2) and the instructor's own attitude words in 8.5. So there is no measurement of what the player does.

### 8.2 The two tables

Two tables of 8 rows of 8 bytes, row index = the lesson number `V:4A851` (0 to 7; row 0 of the first table is zero):

| table | address | row | field | offset | size | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A | `0x2749A` | 0..7 | prompt id | +0 | word | warning-table entry of the trigger prompt (entries 46 to 52); 0 in row 0 |
| A | | | name id | +2 | word | entry shown while the manoeuvre runs (entries 23 to 28 and 43); 0 in row 0 |
| A | | | unused | +4 | long | 0 in every row |
| B | `0x274E2` | 0..7 | camera | +0 | long | low byte the external direction `V:4A96A` (one value in rows 0 to 6, another in row 7), the byte above it the view selector `V:4A83D` (the same in every row); the camera words are disk data and are not listed (FNV-1a-32 of the sixteen camera bytes of table B, byte 0 then byte 1 (the byte above it) of each row's long, in row order, `f6bc0e93`) |
| B | | | script | +4 | long | bit 31 set: the low byte is the instructor's **manoeuvre state** to start (the 8 shipped rows are all of this kind) |

| lesson | manoeuvre |
| --- | --- |
| 0 | the take-off and climb (part 2 only; part 1 skips it) |
| 1 | aileron roll |
| 2 | break turn |
| 3 | inside loop |
| 4 | split-S |
| 5 | vertical half-loop |
| 6 | barrel roll |
| 7 | inverted flight |

The prompt and name entries of the rows are disk data and are not listed here (their FNV-1a-32 hashes and shape are in the constants table of 8.13): the prompt entries of lessons 1 to 7 are seven consecutive warning entries in a permuted order, and the name entries are seven entries, six of them within a group of six consecutive ones. The state a row starts is the **first state of the manoeuvre's chain** in 8.5 (the chain is code; the row's bytes are hashed in 8.13). All of it is in `tables/training.bin`, which the extractor writes (little-endian table A, rows 1..7, then table B and the rate tables; the tests that need the real rows read it under the asset guard).

(Which of the table's rows names which manoeuvre was read from the warning table's texts at `0x41FF0` and confirmed in the capture, which observed only lesson 1 showing entry 23 in part 1, V.) V: `training_check.py` checks both tables (8 + 8 rows, by their hashes in 8.2). A third row-like use: the camera lines of the advance routine index table B by the **entity index** of the instructor (4), not by the lesson (R and V in the rig, 8.11), so the camera of every lesson is the direction and view of row 4 (the rows of the other entities are never read for the camera).

**The script pointer.** A positive long in the "script" field would be a pointer to a pointer to a recorded key script (a word length at +0 of the buffer, up to 509 key bytes from +2, a negative byte ends it); the interpreter for it is at `0x272F2` to `0x273BC` (the cursor `V:54E42` indexes the buffer; at 509 bytes it posts warning entry 29 and stops the instructor), and a recorder at `0x271B8` writes `+0x65` of the instructor into such a buffer while `V:4A854` is non-negative. **None of it is reachable in the shipped game** [R]: all eight script longs are negative; `V:4A854` is zero and nothing writes it except the recorder's own end; the text for entry 30 (`0x273B2`) has no branch to it; the entries 29 and 30 are never shown. The port implements neither (section 11 lists them).

### 8.3 The instructor and the other state

**Mode 2's flag bit 0x0100 is on record 4, not on the player.** The mission-start branch of the mode 2 stores it with a word move to the absolute address `0x4BA3E` (6.1 item 7 calls the word "the player record's second word": the address is record 4's `W2`, `0x4B23C + 0x800 + 2`), which is what makes the part 1 lead scripted from its first pass; the player's own `W2` reads 0x0080 and its `W0` 0x11D8 in every sample of the part 1 capture (the runway start of airfield 1, speed 0, state 0, 150 samples unchanged), so the flag has **no effect on the dormant player** [V: `flow-training-part1.txt` samples 0 to 150; the player's step runs each pass and finds nothing to do]. (Note for Part A: 6.1 item 7 should say record 4.)

**The instructor** is record 4, a normal entity of class 0x10 (the F-16 class; id 0x3D in part 1) that is stepped like any other aircraft (note 05): the step 0x2A2A2 calls the AI state routine `0x30A7E` for it, which writes the key byte `+0x65`; the same step then reads that key byte as the player's controls are read (note 05 section 2). It flies at a fixed throttle: `+0x2B` is 0x60 (96 of 120, 80 percent) from the start of every lesson of part 1 and from the end of the take-off roll in part 2 (the sequencer writes it, 8.4; the take-off state 0x23 itself raises it from the placement's value with the throttle key until it reaches 0x60).

| | part 1 (mode 2) | part 2 (mode 0x7D) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| placement | `0x2CE36`, table row of the mode (note 05 17.17 rule 5): record 4 at (307,757,056, 3,145,728, 328,728,576), attitude (0, 0, 0), speed words `+0x6C`, `+0x6E` = 8192, throttle 0, `W0` 0x11C8, group 4, `+0x38` = 0xFF (no target), `+0x2C` = 0 [V: rig, 4 variants of the selector, no difference; emulator sample 0 reads the same y and speed] | `0x2CFDA`: a copy of **record 0** (256 bytes: the player's attitude, speed, throttle and flags), class set to 0x10, `+0x2C` = -1, group 4, placed **72 model units ahead of the player along the player's heading** (position + 72 x 1024 along (-sin h, 0, cos h), no vertical offset, the **heading-only matrix** of 6.1: rig-checked there on 300 player states, 0 differences; the pitch and roll of the player do not tilt the offset; V: the logged offset of the first four samples has length 73,728 and the lead's heading equals the player's, 27,192; the earlier wording "along the nose, third column" is withdrawn) |
| scripted flag | `W2` 0x0100 set from the start (V: sample 0) | clear until the player's indicated speed `+0x6E` is not zero, then set by `0x27242` (V: `flow-training-part2`: the take-off state starts in the sample after the throttle key F8) |
| camera | the viewed entity `V:4A994` = 4, offset `V:4A996` = 0x800, external view `V:4A83D` = 1 at direction 3 | the player's cockpit |
| allowance `V:4A94F` | 3 (the default of `0x120BC`) | 1 (`0x12202`; note 05 17.17 rule 7) |

**The instructor's step against note 05's per-entity branches** [R for the branches, V for the result: `instructor_check.py`, 8.11]. Each pass `0x26AF8` handles record 4 as follows (the player's pre-steps and cues are not run for it):

| item of note 05 | applies to record 4 (class 0x10, `W2` 0x0100 set, index 4)? |
| --- | --- |
| order of the pass (`0x26CD4` to `0x26CFC`): the sequencer `0x27222`, then `0x278F6`, then the step `0x2A2A2` | yes, in this order; `0x278F6` takes the class-1 branch: it decrements `+0x4C`, requires `W0` bit 0x1000 (active) and `V:4A846`, and because `W2` 0x0100 is set skips the AI steering (the pursuit code of `0x27998` onwards) and returns 1, so the step runs |
| the player-only pre-steps (`V:4AA6C` = 0, the effect timers, `W2` bit 0x0001 and `W0` bit 0x0002 clear, `0x26F28`, the sound cue `0x270A0`, `0x27B1E`, `0x2835A`) | **no**: they are in the player's branch of the pass; record 4 gets only the decrement of `+0x4C` from `0x278F6` |
| the view gating of `0x2A2A2` (note 05 17.2: in the external view the `W0` bits decide) | applies to every record; the instructor has `W0` bit 0x1000 and so runs the whole step in part 1's external view |
| the AI state routine | `0x2A37A`: with `W2` 0x0100 set and the state byte 0 the routine is **not called** and only the recentre bit is set; with a non-zero state byte it is called (`0x2A3A6`) and writes the key byte `+0x65`, the recentre bit, the phase flags and the state byte; the input conversion `0x1DFAA` follows in the same step (the replay stream and the recorder are skipped for index not 0) |
| the speed step `0x15ED4`, the attitude step `0x31AE0`, the motion step `0x16B0E`, the cell bookkeeping, the collision routine | yes, the same routines; the **coupling** `0x32426` follows the rule of both notes: it is called for a non-player aircraft whenever the block at `0x31D9E` runs, that is whenever neither the pause byte `V:4A866` nor `V:4A867` is set (note 08 6.1 item 2: those two gate the block, including the rate update), and for the player only when the switch `V:4A847` is also set; "always" in note 05 17.17 g means independent of `V:4A847`. In training both gates are clear in flight, so the instructor is coupled in every pass [R, `0x31D9E`-`0x31DD0`] |
| the stick response table | `0x425D8` (the player's), **not** `0x42654`: the selection at `0x155D2` to `0x155EA` takes the player's table for index 0 and for any record whose `W2` bit 0x0100 is set (note 05 section 5's exception), and the instructor has the bit set |
| the player's pointer-throttle and fuel pre-block of `0x1557C` | no (index 0 only) |
| the dying path of non-player records (`0x2A2C4` to `0x2A2E2`, `W0` bit 0x0400; every 32nd frame `0x2D53C`) | only if the instructor is destroyed; in part 2 the end check of 8.9 then fires when `W0` bit 0x0040 clears |
| the state byte `+0x05` in the step | **yes**: note 05 17.15 and 17.7 read `+0x05` = 0x0A as the wreck drift (pitch toward level at 0x50 per step); the AI's wait state 0x0A is the same byte, so after every manoeuvre the **flight step itself** levels the instructor's pitch while the AI waits (V: in the player-index replay below, leaving this byte out gave a pitch error of 16 units at the first state 0x0A, step 55) |
| **pitch rate without a pitch command** (`0x15B7A` called from `0x1557C`) | **differs**: with no stick command the pitch rate `+0x56` decays by a quarter for the player (index 0: 1000 becomes 750) and by **half** for every other record (1000 becomes 500); within 15 units of zero either moves one unit toward zero [V rig: `training_step_check.py` part 1, `0x1557C` on 8 rates, both signs and small values, for index 0 and index 4: 16 of 16 equal to the rule]. This is the whole of the divergence at step 144 of 8.3's player-index replay (the first step of the break turn's pull in which the AI gives no stick bit with the rate at -337: the rate becomes -169 for the instructor, the player rule would give -253); it **closes** Open question 1 |
| the vertical velocity words while the aircraft rolls on the ground (`0x16B0E`, the test of the entity index at `0x16D42`) | **differs** (index 0 only): the player's code adjusts or zeroes them, a record of another index keeps them [R: the test and its branch, `0x16D42`-`0x16D6E`] |
| lift-off (`0x171DC`-`0x1722C`) | **differs**: the player (index 0) books the flight-clock variables (`V:4A9C4`, `V:4A9C8`, 10.7), sets bit 0 of `+0x21`, clears bit 1 of `V:4A98A` and sets bit 8 of `V:4A982`; any other record calls the AI hook `0x1F0E2` instead and touches none of them (so **the lead's lift-off and touchdown write nothing to the flight-clock variables or the mission-status words**: they are the player's own) [R, `0x171DC`-`0x17254`, `0x16F30`]; the jolt kind of `0x171D4` is stored for any record, so a lead's lift-off shakes the display like the player's (note 06 open question 1) [R] |
| the first pass of a placed record | **no motion** [V rig: `training_step_check.py` part 2, record 4 after `0x2CE36`, W0 = `0x11C8`: the first `0x26AF8` leaves position and altitude unchanged, clears W0 bit `0x0100` (`0x10C8`), changes the speed word (8192 to 8110) and the state byte (0 to 0x0C); the second pass moves it (the z word 328,728,576 to 328,736,592)] |
| the state byte `0x0A` (and 6) in the input conversion (`0x31BA8`-`0x31CD4`) | the conversion that follows the AI (`0x1DFAA`, then the attitude step) **levels the wings by a roll command** while the state byte is 0x0A or 6: W0 bit `0x2000` is set (and stays set after the pass), and the roll bits of `+0x65` are set from the roll word: 4 for 281 to 14,400, 8 for 14,401 to 28,519, 0 for 0 to 280 (a dead band of 280 units) and 28,520 up [V rig: `training_step_check.py` part 3, 20 cases (states 0x0A and 6, ten rolls): key byte roll bits 0, 0, 0, 4, 4, 4, 8, 8, 0, 0 and W0 bit `0x2000` set in all]; **which records**: the levelling code is reached only when the record is **scripted** (`W2` 0x0100) or its byte `+0x64` has bit 4 (the AI epilogue flag, 8.5) set; an unscripted record without that bit gets only its key byte ANDed with 0xC3 (`0x31B8A`-`0x31BA8`): the unscripted part 2 lead in state 6 gets no `W0` 0x2000 and no levelling [V rig: `training_step_check.py` part 3 for the scripted rows; scripted records are levelled with `+0x64` bit 4 clear or set (W0 `0x30C8`, roll bits 4); an unscripted record is levelled only with the bit set (W0 `0x30C8`) and not without it (W0 `0x10C8`, no roll bits), states 0x0A and 6 alike: 8 combinations in a one-off run]. The same code computes a pitch-rate target by speed (below 0x1000: -640, below 0x2000: -800, else -1040) but **for a record of class 0x10 it is overwritten** by the record's own rates at `0x31CF8`-`0x31D00` before use, so it drives nothing for the instructor [V rig: state 0x0A at speed words 0x800, 0x1800 and 0x3000: pitch rate 0 and the same pitch drift of 80 units per step in all three; the drift is the wreck drift of note 05, independent of speed] |

**What was run.** (1) The original's world update `0x26AF8` for 300 steps from the part 1 start state (placement `0x2CE36`, `W2` 0x0100, mode 2, the switches as in the game, the ceiling `V:4A7B2` = 0x800000, the system-clock and effect routines patched to return) reproduces the emulator's instructor in every one of the 44 logged samples of the first 297 steps of the cycle (speed word, pitch, heading, roll and state byte; the lesson 1 samples include the speed 8110 at step 0 and 7536 at step 7 of the capture). (2) The same record placed in record 0 and stepped by the same `0x26AF8` with the key byte, the recentre bit, the state byte and the phase flags that the AI routine left in each step (read by a stub at `0x2A3AC`, just before `0x1DFAA`) gives **identical position, pitch, heading, roll and speed for steps 1 to 143**; at step 144 (lesson 2, state 7, the first step in which the AI releases the stick) the two differ by a few units (pitch 232 against 216) and diverge from there. The cause is the pitch-rate decay: with no stick command a record of another index halves its pitch rate where the player's decays by a quarter (`0x15B7A`; table row above): at step 144 the AI's pitch command gives no stick bit with the rate at -337, so the instructor's rate becomes -169 and the player rule would give -253 [V rig]. So the port runs the instructor with the player's step rules **plus the per-entity differences of the table above** (the half-decay of the pitch rate is the one that matters for the lessons; the others concern the ground, the lift-off bookkeeping and the first pass).

**The full write set of the part 1 placement** [V rig: `0x2CE36` for the mode 2 over an all-zero record 4, every non-zero byte after the call; the position and attitude fields are those of the row above and of 6.1]. W0 = `0x11C8` (`+0x00`, `+0x01`); `+0x07` = 0x49 and `+0x09` = 0x4E (cell words from the position); `+0x0C` and `+0x0E` = 0x1800 (the fine words); `+0x38` = 0xFF; `+0x5D` = 0xFF; `+0x5E` = 4 (group); `+0x5F` = 0x44, `+0x60` = 1, `+0x61` = 0xF4 (the template's words); `+0x62` = 0x10 (class); `+0x63` = 0x3D (id, the F-16 template of the instructor); `+0x64` = 0x10; `+0x6C` and `+0x6E` = 0x2000 (speed words 8192); `+0x71` = 0xFF; `+0x73` = 0x61, `+0x74` = 0xA8; `+0x7A` = 3; **`+0x7C` = 0xE0 (the gear byte: gear up)**; `+0x7D` = 3; `+0x7E` = 0; the matrices at `+0x80` to `+0xA2` (the identity, 0x4000 words); `B4` (`+0x39`) = 0 and the cell sub-bytes `+0x0A`, `+0x0B` = 0 (the first pass computes them). **The lesson cycle depends on the gear byte**: with the placement's `+0x7C` = 0xE0 (and with 0xE0 written again) the lessons start in steps 0, 93, 196, 384, 541, 690, 780 and the wrap in 962; with the gear byte 0 or 0x60 (gear down or extending) every start from lesson 4 on is later (385, 542, 702, 792, 974) [V rig: `training_step_check.py` part 6, 4 gear values]. A port that places record 4 from its own table must therefore write the gear up and keep it (the instructor never moves the gear).

**Variables of the sequencer** (all in the zeroed variable area; they are cleared by the mission start):

| variable | meaning | values |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `V:4A851` | lesson number | 0 at the start; 1 to 7 while a lesson of the table runs; the advance wraps 8 to 1 |
| `V:4A852` | trigger state | 0 normal; 0xFF the prompt for the next lesson has been posted and the instructor waits for the trigger; 0xFE as 0xFF after a RETURN re-position. Part 2 only (part 1 keeps 0) |
| `V:54E42` | script cursor (a word) | 0 before the instructor has been given its state for this lesson, 1 after |
| `V:4A84B` | part 1 flight is running | set at `0x12E9C` when the part 1 flight begins; gates the sequencer of part 1 and selects the wait after a manoeuvre |
| `V:4A866` | pause | the sequencer does nothing while it is non-zero |
| `V:4A968` | centre-screen notice selector | 0xFE written when part 1 skips lesson 0 (`0x272D2`) |

### 8.4 The lesson sequencer (per step, record 4)

The routine `0x27222` runs for record 4 in every pass, before the record's own movement (`0x278F6`, `0x2A2A2`); the player's part is `0x270A0` (8.6). In order:

1. **Part 2 only, instructor not yet running** (`W2` 0x0100 clear): the instructor's state byte `+0x05` is cleared; if the player's indicated speed `+0x6E` is 0 the routine ends; otherwise `W2` 0x0100 is set and the cursor cleared **and the routine goes on with step 2 in the same pass**: the table walk of step 5 then starts lesson 0 (state byte 0x23) in the very pass in which the player's speed first became non-zero; it does not end there [V rig: `training_check.py` part 2, the sequencer on random states with the lead not yet running, 0 differences with the model that continues; V part 2: the take-off state is on the first sample after the throttle key].
2. If the pause `V:4A866` is set, or `W2` 0x0100 is clear, the routine ends.
3. **Part 1 only**: if `V:4A84B` is 0 it ends. If the player's `W2` bit 0x0800 is set (RETURN, 8.6) it is cleared and the instructor is **re-placed once**: `W0` is cleared, the placement `0x2CE36` is called (record 4 returns to its start position, attitude and speed: V in the rig, the placement is deterministic), `W2` 0x0100 and the throttle 0x60 are set, the advance helper `0x273F0` (step 7, second half) is called, and the table walk (step 5) is entered with the new lesson. The helper does not place: the placement at lesson 7 belongs to the end-of-manoeuvre action (step 6), which a RETURN does not pass through, so a RETURN at lesson 7 re-places once, not twice [V rig: the sequencer with the lesson number 7 and the RETURN bit, the logging stub of `0x2CE36` called once; `training_check.py` part 2, 1500 states].
4. **Lesson 0 of part 1** (`V:4A851` is 0 or less): the throttle is set to 0x60, `V:4A968` to 0xFE, and since row 0's script is negative the "end of manoeuvre" action (step 6) is taken at once: part 1 never flies the take-off, the first lesson that runs is lesson 1 (V: the first sample of the lesson loop reads lesson 1 with the state 0x0C and the name of entry 23).
5. **Table walk.** Row B of the lesson: a zero script ends the routine; a negative one: if the cursor is 0 the trigger bit `W2` 0x0800 of the instructor is cleared, the state byte `+0x05` is set to the low byte of the script and the cursor to 1, and the routine ends; if the cursor is non-zero and the state byte is not 0 (the manoeuvre is running) the routine ends; if the state byte is 0 (the manoeuvre has finished) the key byte `+0x65` is ANDed with 0xC3 and the end-of-manoeuvre action follows.
6. **End of a manoeuvre** (`0x27314`). Part 1: always the advance, below. Part 2: if the instructor's `W2` 0x0800 is set (the fire trigger, 8.6) it is cleared and the advance follows; else if `V:4A852` is negative the routine ends (the instructor waits for the trigger), else `V:4A852` becomes 0xFF and the prompt of the next lesson is posted (the table A prompt id of the lesson number plus 1, or of lesson 1 after lesson 7) and the routine ends.
7. **The advance**, in two routines: the end-of-manoeuvre action `0x27362` sets `V:4A852` to 0 and, if the lesson number is 7 or more, re-places the instructor (`W0` cleared, the placement, `W2` 0x0100, throttle 0x60; in part 2 the placement copies the player's record again, so the instructor reappears 72 units ahead of the player with the player's attitude); then the helper `0x273F0` (which the RETURN of step 3 calls alone) increments the lesson number (8 becomes 1), the cursor and `V:4A85E` cleared, the instructor's key byte cleared; if the viewed entity `V:4A994` is the instructor, `V:4A96A` becomes the camera direction of the table B row of the entity index (3) and in part 1 only (mode 2), when the view is external (`V:4A83D` non-zero), `V:4A83D` becomes 1 for the instructor as the viewed entity, the high byte of the table B camera word, and 0xFF when the viewed entity is some other record; **in part 2 (mode 0x7D) the advance never touches `V:4A83D`** (the player stays in the cockpit view and sees nothing change) [V rig: `training_check.py` part 2, the viewed entity 0 or 4 and the view 0, 1, 2 or 0xFF, mode 2 and 0x7D, 1500 states, 0 differences]; `V:4A910` becomes 0xFF (redraw); the name of the lesson is posted (table A name id) except that lesson 4 with the instructor at or below 0x100000 (about 5,000 feet; the test is signed and includes the value) posts entry 53 (the split-S refusal notice) instead [V rig: the sequencer on altitudes 0xFFFFF, 0x100000 (posted) and 0x100001 (not posted), part 2 of `training_check.py`]; the table walk of step 5 then runs again for the new lesson in the same step.

V: `training_check.py` runs the original's `0x27222` and `0x270A0` in the Musashi rig, with the placement, the post routine and the sound calls replaced by logging stubs, against this description for random states of the variables and the two records (8.11): see the counts there.

Lesson timing is therefore: a manoeuvre starts in the step the previous one has been advanced; its own duration is the instructor's state machine (8.5); after the last state the **end routine** (8.5, END) holds the instructor in state 0x0A for 36 steps in part 1 (`V:4A84B` set) or 60 steps in part 2 (the counter `+0x4C` set to 0x24 or 0x3C), and the state byte is then cleared and the walk of step 5 sees the end.

### 8.5 The instructor's manoeuvre states (driver rules)

The state byte `+0x05` of record 4 selects one of the routines below; the AI state routine `0x30A7E` runs it once per step when the instructor is active (`W0` bit 0x0080), the simulation is running and neither `V:4A866` nor `V:4A867` is set. A scripted instructor in state 0 skips the routine and only sets the recentre bit each step (`0x2A37A`). Common elements (the same in every manoeuvre):

| element | rule |
| --- | --- |
| roll and pitch | the pitch word `+0x66` and the roll word `+0x6A` of the record, compared as signed words; 14,400 is 180 degrees, 7,200 is 90, 21,600 is 270 |
| phase flags | bits 0x08 (f3) and 0x10 (f4) of the byte `+0x20`; set and cleared by the states below; the byte is the record's flag byte of note 05 (bit 1, out of control, is never set on the instructor in these flows and every state assumes it clear) |
| counter | the word `+0x4C`, decremented once per step by `0x278F6` before the routine runs, so a state that sets 15 sees 14 in the next step; "counter > 0" tests the value after that decrement |
| recentre | sets bit 0x0010 of the low byte of `W2` (`+0x03`); the flight step re-centres the controls and the roll (note 05 17.7) |
| roll command(d) | clears bits 7 to 2 of the key byte `+0x65` and sets 0x08 (roll left) if d > 0, 0x04 (roll right) if d < 0; bits 1 and 0 (throttle) are kept. Only the sign of d matters |
| pitch command(d) | compares the target pitch rate d with the pitch rate `+0x56`: for d >= 0 it sets 0x10 (stick forward) when d > rate; for d < 0 it sets 0x20 (stick back) when d < rate; otherwise no stick bit. It keeps bits 1 and 0 and clears the others; the variant used by the barrel roll keeps every bit except 5 and 4 |
| epilogue | `+0x64` bit 0x10 is set after every state that commands the controls (not after the end routine's wait state) |
| rate tables | pitch-rate targets (word table `0x3132C`, index 4 = 320, 6 = 400, 7 = 480); roll-rate targets (word table `0x31342`, index 7 = 720, 8 = 800) |
| set-up phase | every manoeuvre but the take-off begins with a **set-up** pair of states: the first clears the phase flags (the vertical half-loop clears only f3), sets the counter and falls into the second, which recentres while the counter is above 0 and then starts the manoeuvre proper in the same step |
| state changes fall through | a state that stores the number of the next state **runs the next state in the same step**, with the counter it has just stored (the set-up states of every manoeuvre, the hold states, the changes 0x24 to 0x25 to 0x26, 0x27 to 0x28 to 2, 0x0F to 0x10, 0x16 to 0x0D, 0x1C to 0x1E to 0x1F to 0x21 to 0x22 to 0x16, 0x13 to 0x0F, and 6 to 8). **Three changes do not fall through**: 0x23 to 0x24 (the step ends with the epilogue), 2 to 7 (the pull starts in the next step), and every change into the wait state 0x0A (the END routine ends the step without an epilogue) [V rig: the 60,000 + 100,000 records of 8.11 against a transcription that falls through in exactly these places, 0 differences]. The state tables below list the transitions; read each as "and then the new state, unless it is one of the three" |

Rig: all states below against `0x30A7E` on scripted records with random fields (counts in 8.11). The thresholds are those the rig confirmed; the semantics in the "reads as" column are interpretation.

**Take-off and climb (lesson 0, part 2; the chain of states in the table)**

| state | each step | leaves when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0x23 | throttle: key bit 0x01 set while `+0x2B` (signed byte) < 0x60, cleared (bits 1 and 0) otherwise | `+0x6E` >= 0x840 (2,112; the indicated speed word, about 176 knots): state 0x24, counter 60 |
| 0x24 | nothing (epilogue) | counter < 0: f4 cleared, state 0x25 |
| 0x25 | pitch command(-40), with f4 clear: while pitch < 14,400; after f4 is set while pitch >= 26,800 (25 degrees nose up) | pitch < 26,800 with f4 set: pitch command(0), key byte 0, state 0x26 |
| 0x26 | nothing | altitude `+0x18` > 0x240000 (about 11,500 feet): the end routine |

**Aileron roll (lesson 1; the chain of states in the table)** [V: rig; V: emulator `flow-training-part1` shows 0x0C for 15 steps, then 0x0D with key 0x08]

| state | action |
| --- | --- |
| 0x0B | clear f3 and f4, counter 15, state 0x0C |
| 0x0C | counter > 0: recentre; else state 0x0D |
| 0x0D | roll command(+800) (roll left) in three phases: f3 and f4 clear: if roll >= 14,400 the command only, else set f3; f3 set, f4 clear: if roll < 14,400 the command only, else set f4; f4 set: roll < 800 ends the manoeuvre (end routine); roll >= 28,160 gives roll command(0); otherwise the command. It reads as a full left roll: through 180 degrees and back to within 10 degrees of the start |

**Break turn (lesson 2; the chain of states in the table)**

| state | action |
| --- | --- |
| 0x27 | state 0x28, counter 15 |
| 0x28 | counter > 0: recentre; else state 2 |
| 2 | target T = 7,200 if roll < 14,400, else 21,600; E = absolute difference of roll and T in word arithmetic (the overflow case of a roll word above 28,799 is replicated: a negative true difference is negated in 16 bits); E >= 1,200 (15 degrees): roll command(+800) if roll < T, roll command(-800) otherwise; E < 1,200: state 7, counter 32, key byte ANDed with 3 |
| 7 | counter < 0: end routine; else pitch command(-320) (pull) |

**Inside loop (lesson 3; the chain of states in the table)**

| state | action |
| --- | --- |
| 0x0E | clear f3 and f4, counter 15, state 0x0F |
| 0x0F | counter > 0: recentre; else state 0x10 |
| 0x10 | pitch command(-400): f3 and f4 clear: pull while pitch <= 14,400, else set f3; f3 set, f4 clear: pull while pitch > 14,400, else set f4; f4 set: pitch > 14,400 ends the manoeuvre, otherwise pull. It reads as one full loop: the pitch word goes up through 14,400 and returns below it, and the manoeuvre ends when it passes 14,400 for the second time |

**Split-S (lesson 4; the chain of states in the table)**

| state | action |
| --- | --- |
| 0x11 | altitude `+0x18` <= 0x100000 (about 5,000 feet): the end routine at once (the manoeuvre is skipped; the sequencer has posted entry 53, 8.7); else clear f3 and f4, counter 15, state 0x12 |
| 0x12 | counter > 0: recentre; else state 0x13 |
| 0x13 | roll command(+800) until roll >= 14,400 (the same f3 logic: if roll >= 14,400 at once the command only, else set f3); then if roll is exactly 14,400: state 0x0F, counter 2 (the inside loop's pull follows from the next state change of the same step); else if roll >= 13,760 (172 degrees): recentre and roll command(0); else roll command(+800). Whether the roll word ever equals 14,400 exactly depends on the recentre rule of note 05 17.7; the captured split-S completes (8.12) |

**Vertical half-loop (lesson 5; the chain of states in the table)**

| state | action |
| --- | --- |
| 0x14 | clear f3 only, counter 15, state 0x15 |
| 0x15 | counter > 0: recentre; else state 0x16 |
| 0x16 | pitch command(-400): f3 clear: pull while pitch <= 14,400, else set f3; f3 set: pull while pitch > 14,400, else state 0x0D with f3 still set, so the roll of 0x0D (f3 set, f4 clear) rolls from the inverted attitude to upright and ends when the roll word has passed 14,400 and wrapped below 800 |

**Barrel roll (lesson 6; the chain of states in the table)**

| state | action |
| --- | --- |
| 0x17 | clear f3 and f4, counter 15, state 0x18 |
| 0x18 | counter > 0: recentre; else state 0x19 |
| 0x19 | roll command(+720) and then pitch command(-480) keeping the roll bits, every step, in the three phases of the aileron roll's test on roll against 14,400 (f3 set on the first step below 14,400, f4 set on the first step at or above it after that); with f4 set and roll < 14,400 the manoeuvre ends (end routine); the commands are issued in every other case |

**Inverted flight (lesson 7; the chain of states in the table)**

| state | action |
| --- | --- |
| 0x1A | clear f3 and f4, counter 15, state 0x1B |
| 0x1B | counter > 0: recentre; else state 0x1C |
| 0x1C | roll command(+800) with the f3 logic of the split-S; at roll exactly 14,400: clear f4, state 0x1E, counter 10; roll >= 13,760: recentre and roll command(0) |
| 0x1E | counter > 0: recentre; else state 0x1F |
| 0x1F | pitch command(-320) in two bands of the roll: roll in 7,200..21,600 (band A) or outside (band B). Band A: f4 clear: pull while pitch > 14,400, else set f4; then pull while pitch < 2,400, otherwise done. Band B: f4 clear: pull while pitch < 14,400, else set f4; then done when pitch < 26,400, otherwise pull. Done: pitch command(0), clear f4, state 0x21, counter 25 |
| 0x21 | counter > 0: recentre; else state 0x22 |
| 0x22 | pitch command(+320) with the mirrored tests: band A: f4 clear: push while pitch < 14,400, else set f4; then push while pitch > 26,400, otherwise done. Band B: f4 clear: push while pitch > 14,400, else set f4; then done when pitch > 2,400, otherwise push. Done: pitch command(0), set f3, state 0x16 (then 0x0D as for the half-loop) |

**The end routine and the waits** (`0x30984`, `0x3095C`, `0x30978`, `0x30A68`)

| step | action |
| --- | --- |
| end routine, `V:4A852` >= 0 (every shipped flow) | state 0x0A, counter 36 if `V:4A84B` is set (part 1) else 60; no epilogue |
| state 0x0A | counter < 0: state 0, key byte ANDed with 3 (the sequencer sees the finished manoeuvre in the next pass); else nothing in the AI routine. **While it waits (and in state 6) the input conversion after the AI levels the wings by a roll command with a dead band of 280 and sets W0 bit 0x2000, and the flight step drifts the pitch to level** (8.3, table of the non-player step) |
| end routine, `V:4A852` < 0 | counter 10, state 6; states 6 and 8 then wait while `V:4A852` is negative and otherwise run a window test on pitch and roll before returning to state 6; **not reachable in the shipped flows** [R, and rig-checked as part of the 6,000-record runs]: `V:4A852` is negative only while the instructor sits in state 0 |

After END the instructor flies on with its last key byte cleared to throttle only (the AND with 3), at the throttle of 0x60: state 0 recentres every step.

### 8.6 The player's keys that drive the lessons: RETURN, the trigger and the smoke key

| key | handler | part 1 | part 2 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RETURN (raw 0x44, key state `V:4A966` = 0) | `0x1E8A6` | sound `0x37C4A`, then the player's `W2` bit 0x0800 is set; the sequencer (8.4 step 3) acts on it in the same pass: the instructor is re-placed, the lesson advances and the next manoeuvre starts at once (**skip**) | if `V:4A852` is negative (0xFE or 0xFF): sound and the player's `W2` bit 0x0800 is set, and `0x270A0` acts on it (below); otherwise (a manoeuvre is running, `V:4A852` = 0) the notice 31 (not ready to re-position) is posted with the code 0x401F and nothing else happens |
| fire (SPACE, raw 0x40; `0x1F07A`) | `0x1DF48` | none of the training use | if `V:4A852` is negative (and the player is not ejecting): the instructor's `W2` bit 0x0800 is set; the sequencer (8.4 step 6) takes it as the trigger. With `V:4A852` = 0 it has no training effect (it is the weapon trigger of free flight otherwise) |
| S (raw 0x21; `0x1ED7E`) | | no effect | toggles bit 0x1000 of the instructor's `W2` (the skid/smoke trail bit) and plays the sound; **the original draws a trail for it**: the camera-trail routine `0x2AC5A` keeps a ring of the last six positions of the followed record (`V:54E72`, set to record 4's offset 0x800 at every mission initialisation, `0x1D266`) and, while that record's speed is non-zero and `W2` bit 0x1000 is set, stores a trail length `+0x3D` of up to 4; the entity drawer (`0x25820`-`0x25AE4`, hunk 15) reads `+0x3D` and the ring and draws the trail segments behind the aircraft [R: the two routines; not captured as a frame]. The drawer and its writer are now described in note 07 sections 13.1 and 13.2 (rig-checked against the original; the crash and skid use of the same bit is listed there too) and in note 04a section 8d. It is visible only when the lead is in view (part 2 with an external view, or the chase view of the lead); in part 1 S is ignored |

**The re-position (`0x270A0`, part 2, the player's pass).** When the player's `W2` bit 0x0800 is set (and the mode is 0x7D) the bit is cleared and:

1. the lesson number: if `V:4A852` is not 0xFF it is incremented (8 becomes 1: **each further RETURN skips one lesson**); if it is 0xFF it stays (the first RETURN after a prompt only re-positions);
2. the prompt of the lesson after that number (table A prompt id of lesson + 1, wrapping to lesson 1) is posted, and `V:4A852` becomes 0xFE;
3. record 4 is copied over the player's record (the first 0xA4 bytes: flags, positions, velocities, attitude, matrices), then the player's `+0x63` (id) is restored, `+0x5E` (group) cleared, the class `+0x62` set to 0x11 (the F/A-18; whatever aircraft was chosen, the player is again an F/A-18 class record after a re-position [R]), `W2` bit 0x0100 cleared, the state byte and the key byte cleared; the instructor's throttle byte (0x60 after the take-off) is therefore the player's;
4. the position becomes the instructor's plus the instructor's attitude matrix applied to the offset (+9, +1, -97) model units (`0x1D478`, D3 = 9, D4 = 1, D5 = 0xFF9F): 9 units to the right, 1 up and **97 behind** the instructor, each unit 1,024 position units (the matrix words at `+0x92` are Q14, the products are shifted right by 4); the cells `+0x06`, `+0x08`, the fine position `+0x0C`, `+0x0E` and `+0x10` are derived from it as the start routine derives them (note 05 17.14), and `V:4A910` is set to 0xFF.

V: rig, 30,000 random states of the player and instructor records and the variables, 9,965 of them with a re-position, 0 differences (8.11).

**The writer of `+0x7E` = 0x1400 and the first pass.** The word `+0x7E` of a record is written 0x1400 by the AI state routine `0x30A7E` at its entry (`0x30A90`, for every class except 0x30 and before the active test) and, for the player, by the flight start (`0x1D8DC`); on the first pass of a placed instructor it goes from 0 to 0x1400 (5120) and the cell sub-index `+0x0A` from 0 to 10 (the cell words `+0x06`, `+0x08` and the fine words are unchanged: they were set by the placement), while the position does not move [V rig: `training_step_check.py` part 2 and a one-off run reading `+0x06`, `+0x08`, `+0x0A`, `+0x0B`, `+0x0C`, `+0x0E`, `+0x7E` over two passes: the second pass moves the fine z word].

**`0x270A0` has two jobs, and both note 05 and this section are right.** The routine is called once per pass for record 0 (`0x26BF4`), also in paused passes, and begins with the high-turn-rate sound cue of note 05 section 1 (a sound 8 once when the pitch-rate expression reaches 78: its stores are `V:4A940` and the sound); it then goes on to the part 2 re-position when the mode is 0x7D and the player's `W2` bit 0x0800 is set. The cue runs in every mode and the re-position only in part 2. The routine has no pause test of its own before the re-position and the key routine's RETURN handler (`0x1E8A6`) runs in a pause (note 06 item 8: every handler but E and P acts in a pause), so **RETURN acts during a pause in part 2** [R]: the player's record is copied in the paused pass (the position, attitude and `V:4A910` change; nothing is stepped). In part 1 the same bit is consumed by the sequencer, which stops at the pause test (8.4 step 2), so there RETURN is queued until the pause ends.

**The unscripted lead (part 2, before the player's speed is non-zero)** [V rig: `training_step_check.py` part 4]. With `W2` 0x0100 clear the lead is not scripted and the AI routine `0x30A7E` is the general one; started from the real part 2 placement (`0x1D4E2` runway start, then `0x2CE36` with the mode 0x7D) and stepped by the whole update `0x26AF8` with the player stationary, the lead **does nothing**: position, speed (0), key byte and `W2` stay as placed, the state byte is cleared by the sequencer every pass (8.4 step 1), and only when its counter `+0x4C` has run out the AI takes **state 6 with the counter 10** (step 0: state 6, counter 10; steps 1 to 10: state 0, counter 9 down to 0; step 11: state 6, counter 10 again: a period of 11 passes). (On random records the same routine does other things, e.g. state 1 with the counter 30, depending on the target and altitude bytes; the realistic lead has `+0x2C` = -1 and `+0x38` = 0.) The training sequencer ignores all of it.

**Whose data the part 1 text strip shows.** The external-view strip (note 06, `0x3697A`, `0x36A10`) reads the record at `V:4A996` (the viewed record's offset): in part 1 that is 0x800, record 4, so the **heading, speed and altitude figures are the lead's** (heading from `+0x68`, speed from `+0x6E`, altitude from `+0x18`); the player's record is dormant. In part 2 with the cockpit view the strip is not drawn; with an external view `V:4A996` is 0 and the strip shows the player's data [R from the two routines; the part 1 strip was seen in the captures with the lead's changing heading].

**Part 1's RETURN** differs: the instructor, not the player, moves (the placement `0x2CE36` returns it to its start point), the player's record is untouched, and the lesson counter advances once (a skip) with the name of the new lesson posted.

### 8.7 Prompts and posts

All training texts are entries of the warning-line table at `0x41FF0` (note 06): 28-byte slots, first byte class and duration (the training entries have class 1, information, and duration 0: they stay until replaced), 26 characters. The post routine `0x29BC8` stores the code word in `V:4AB98`; the training posts use the plain entry index (high byte 0) except the "not ready" notice (0x401F, a timed information). The **scroller** at `0x377B2` is the text-record engine of the briefing screens (note 08 section 2) and plays no part in the training prompts; the plan's "posts 0x1D and 0x1E (entries 29 and 30)" are in the unreachable script interpreter (8.2).

| post | entry | when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| name of the manoeuvre | the lesson's name entry from table A (8.2; not listed, hash in 8.13) | at the advance (8.4 step 7), lesson 1 to 7 |
| the split-S refusal notice | 53 | at the advance into lesson 4 when the instructor's altitude is 0x100000 or lower |
| the trigger prompt of the next manoeuvre | the lesson's prompt entry from table A (8.2; not listed, hash in 8.13) | part 2: when a manoeuvre has finished (the next lesson's entry, 8.4 step 6) and again after each RETURN (8.6 step 2) |
| not ready | 31 (code 0x401F) | RETURN with `V:4A852` = 0 in part 2 |
| never posted | 29 (pattern exhausted), 30 (end of the manoeuvres), 44 and 45 (two further trigger prompts) | no branch of the shipped code reaches them (R for 29/30: 8.2; R for 44/45: no post site in the image) |

In part 1 the external view is the only view, and the message line (row 192) is drawn in external views only when the mode is 2 (`0x11426`) or, for the HUD strip drawn by `0x370E6` and `0x37130`, when `V:4A84B` is set. **`V:4A84B` is set once, by the acting pass of `0x12E4E` (`0x12E9C`), and only for the mode 2**; it is cleared by every mission initialisation (the block `V:4A848` to `V:4A87C`). So it is 1 from the start of the part 1 flight to its end and **0 in part 2 and in every other mode**: `0x370E6` returns at once while it is 0, and `0x37130` (the external-view branch) draws only when it is 1, while in a cockpit view the strip code runs without it [R, `0x370E6`-`0x37138`, `0x12E92`-`0x12E9C`]. **What the two routines draw**: they are the external-view tail of the message-line drawer `0x3706A` (note 06, message line): with the view external the drawer's second text line, a copy of the message text at the row of `V:4A8C2`'s page (the offset `0x1B14` of the plane, about row 173, x offset 12, the same 3x5 glyph routine `0x37250`), is drawn only when `V:4A84B` is set; in a cockpit view the line is the ordinary row 192 line and `V:4A84B` is not consulted. **They do not draw the heading, speed and altitude strip**: that strip is `0x3697A` (note 06, external view text strip, rows 184 to 188), drawn in every external view whenever `V:4A891` and the simulation bit hold, independent of `V:4A84B`. So with `V:4A84B` set (part 1) the external view shows the lesson names (the message line) and the strip; in an external view of any other mode, the strip alone [R: `0x3706A`-`0x37138`, `0x3697A`-`0x369A2`]. The clause 'the HUD strip drawn by...' above is corrected to this: **the message line appears in the external view only in part 1; the strip appears in every external view**. Consequences: so the names appear in part 1 and the prompts in part 2's cockpit view; in part 2 the external views hide the line (R).

### 8.8 Keys in the two parts

The flight key handler `0x1DAA0` restricts keys by mode (the groups are those of its compare chain; R, with the emulator reading of the keys that matter):

| group | part 1 (mode 2, key state 0) | part 2 (mode 0x7D, key state 0 in flight) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| flight controls (cursor keys, `-` `=` and the comma and period rudder keys, SPACE, E, R, G, A, F, V, J, H, S, T, Y, M, BACKSPACE, backslash) | **skipped** (the group is not entered when the mode is 2) | active; SPACE is the trigger and S the smoke key (8.6); the others as free flight |
| RETURN, keypad views (KP1 to KP9, KP period) and KP-ENTER (the view toggle), the joystick codes | active: the point of view and distance of the external camera follow the keypad | active as in flight |
| `[` and `]` | active (range and zoom) | active |
| top-row digits and F1 to F10 | **skipped** (the throttle presets cannot be used; the instructor's throttle is fixed) | active (F8 is 80 percent thrust, the briefing's request) |
| P (pause), ESC | active | active |

The keypad changes the camera direction and distance only; each advance of the lesson resets the camera to direction 3 and the view to 1 when the viewed entity is the instructor (8.4 step 7), so a view chosen with the keypad lasts until the next lesson starts.

### 8.9 How a lesson is chosen and left; allowance and crashes

* **Choice.** There is no lesson menu: menu item 3 starts the loop at lesson 1 (part 1, with the instructor placed at its start), item 4 starts at lesson 0 with the take-off. The lesson number only changes through the advance (8.4 step 7) and the player's re-position (8.6).
* **Part 1 loops for ever**: after lesson 7 the next advance re-places the instructor and starts lesson 1 again (8.4 step 7: the lesson number 7 or more triggers `0x2CE36`).
* **Part 2 loops** the same way after lesson 7: the next trigger re-places the instructor 72 units ahead of the player, with the player's attitude, and lesson 1 starts.
* **Leaving.** ESC: part 1 goes on into part 2 (8.1); part 2 restarts part 2 (V). SHIFT-ESC: to the main menu from both (V). The training screens change the mode byte only through `0x11AF8` (2 to 0x7D); the ESC flow's check for the demo replay (`V:4A903` = 3) does not apply.
* **Crashes in part 2.** Collisions are on (`V:4A844` = 1: 8.10) and the allowance is 1 (note 05 17.17 rule 7): the first crash of the player ends part 2 with the game-over screen (text 0x63) and the main menu (V: 8.12). The end check `0x28A5C` fires when the instructor's `W0` bit 0x0040 is clear (the instructor is lost by a collision): `V:4A850` = 0xFE and `V:4A8E2` = 3, and the failure outcome runs; **for the mode 0x7D its text is the id 0x42, a record of one blank line (section 2.4, record 61), so nothing is shown**: the screen freezes for 14 blanks (state `0x131F4`, pause 1), the code becomes 2 and the **flight resumes**; part 2 does not end, and without an active lead record the sequencer no longer runs (the pass skips record 4: `0x26CDA`). **The bit tested** is `W0` bit **0x0040** (the active bit, bit 6 of the byte `+0x01`: `0x26CDA`, `0x28A5C`), not 0x1000 (which gates the speed step and the view rules). An inactive record is neither stepped nor drawn (note 04a: the drawer culls it) and, if it is the viewed record (part 1), the view falls back to the player (note 04a, `0x14392`); the lead's loss in part 1 therefore leaves the dormant player on the runway in view. How the lead dies: a collision result sets the dying bits (W0 `0x0200` and `0x0400`, `+0x4C` = 15), the dying path of note 05 17.10 counts the record down and, when the counter ends, clears the active bit (`0x2A2F4`-`0x2A348`, with `+0x4C` = 20); the lead is not counted as a hostile (its W0 bit 3 is set), so `V:4A963` stays. That path was read, not run: the staged crash of record 4 in the rig (altitude and nose staged) did not kill it (the AI recovered), and a real loss was not captured [R] [V: `flow-training-part2-lost.txt`, staged: request 3 and code 0xFE at blank 3, `0x131F4` at blank 34, the text list `V:4A802` reads 0x0000, 0x0000, 0x8056, 0x8053 at blank 48 (the blank record consumed, the two return words never played, section 5.13), code 2 and the flight state at blank 48]. The crash counter of the flight log is incremented by the crash hand-over (`0x12F28`: it tests `V:4A844`, which is 1) in training as in any other mode, and so is the log's crash statistic (section 10). In part 1 the player's own record is dormant (it sits at the default position on the ground, speed 0, and nothing flies it), so part 1 cannot end in a crash.
* **The player's own flying in part 2** is unrestricted and unscored; there is no limit of time, altitude or position other than the world's (the allowance).

So `V:4A83F` is 0 only before the first mission start (the menu and selection screens, as section 11.1 says) and 1 in every flight including training; `V:4A83C`, `V:4A841`, `V:4A843` to `V:4A847` are 1 for ever. ### 8.12 Captures

All captures start from `extracted/captures/training-menu.vasnap` (the main menu, `flow-training-menu.txt`) and the two frozen security prompts (`flow-training-prompts.txt`: challenge 194A for item 3, 3CCE for item 4, answered with the lookup `code @disks/countercodes.txt`); the scripts are generated by `tools/vamiga-ref/scripts/gen-flow-training.py`; every sample is one `step` of 10, 25 or 50 blanks with `peek` of the instructor record (`F:4BA3C`, 0x70 bytes), the player record (`F:4B23C`), `V:4A851`, `V:4A852`, `V:54E42`, `V:4AB98`, `V:4A802` and the state pointer. Staging with `poke` is stated where it was used. One game step is 7.1 blanks in these runs (the step counter `V:4A992` advanced 955 steps in 6,812 blanks over the part 1 cycle).

**Part 1 cycle** (`flow-training-part1`, V). Lessons 1 to 7 ran in order and the cycle restarted: the lesson number reads 1, 2, ..., 7, 1, 2 at the samples below; the step counter `V:4A992` restarts from 0 at the first lesson. Durations in steps (from the lesson's first sample; the resolution is 50 blanks, about 7 steps):

| lesson | manoeuvre | starts | the end routine (state 0x0A first seen) | next lesson starts | altitude change over the lesson |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| 1 | aileron roll | 0 | 56 | 93 | 0 (15,355 feet) |
| 2 | break turn | 93 | 158 | 200 | -105 feet |
| 3 | inside loop | 200 | 350 | 390 | -1,090 feet (peak 18,870 feet) |
| 4 | split-S | 390 | 506 | 545 | -5,720 feet (from 14,160 to 8,440) |
| 5 | vertical half-loop | 545 | 658 | 691 | +3,500 feet (peak 12,040) |
| 6 | barrel roll | 691 | 746 | 784 | +55 feet (peak 12,355) |
| 7 | inverted flight | 784 | 928 | 962 (wrap: lesson 1 again) | -675 feet (low 10,605) |

**Exact start steps of the part 1 cycle** [V rig: `training_step_check.py` part 5, the original's whole update `0x26AF8` for 1100 passes from the part 1 start (mode 2, `V:4A84B` = 1, the twelve switches 1, the ceiling `V:4A7B2` = 0x800000, the placement `0x2CE36`, `W2` 0x0100 on record 4); a step is a pass; the table above has the emulator's 50-blank samples]. Lesson 1 starts in step 0, lesson 2 in step 93, lesson 3 in 196, lesson 4 in 384, lesson 5 in 541, lesson 6 in 690, lesson 7 in 780, and lesson 1 again (the wrap, with the re-placement) in step 962, lesson 2 in step 1055. The lesson lengths are therefore 93, 103, 188, 157, 149, 90 and 182 steps, and the cycle is 962 steps. Each start lies inside the emulator's 7-step sample interval that ends at the table's first-sample step (93, 200, 390, 545, 691, 784, 962 and 1057 in the emulator log), which is the check against the real machine. A port run whose starts differ (for instance 385, 542, 702, 792, 974) differs from the original by an accumulated 12 steps from lesson 5 on and is outside the emulator's samples.

The wait of the end routine reads 33 to 42 steps between the first state-0x0A sample and the next lesson (36 plus the sampling resolution). At the wrap (`V:4A851` back to 1) the instructor's altitude returns to 3,072 (units of 1,024) with attitude 0, 15,360 feet: the re-placement. The states seen in the samples (0x0C, 0x0D, 0x28, 2, 7, 0x0F, 0x10, 0x12, 0x13, 0x15, 0x16, 0x18, 0x19, 0x1B, 0x1C, 0x1E, 0x1F, 0x21, 0x0A and 0) are those of 8.5 in the order given there; states 6 and 8 were never seen in about 900 samples of all captures (the instructor's trigger state `V:4A852` is 0 during every manoeuvre).

**Part 1 keys** (`flow-training-part1-return`): RETURN during lesson 2 (state 0x28, sample 4) gave lesson 3 in the next sample with the name of the inside loop (entry 25), the instructor back at its start (heading 0, pitch 0, roll 0, altitude 3,071 units) and state 0x0F; KP5 changed the external direction `V:4A96A` from 3 to 8 and the next lesson's start (sample 63) reset it to 3. The direction also flips from 3 to 7 and back inside lessons 3, 4 and 5 (open question 2).

**ESC.** `flow-training-part1-esc` (ESC during lesson 2): mode 0x7D at the next sample, screen text 0x68, then the part 2 start with briefing 10 and the 0x7D flight state, with no security prompt. `flow-training-part2-esc` (ESC from part 2): the flight restarts from the briefing 10 (no menu, no prompt), mode 0x7D, lesson 0, `V:4A852` 0, cursor 0: the menu's ESC rule (ESC restarts the selection). SHIFT-ESC from either part goes to the main menu (the failure-case table below).

**Part 2** (`flow-training-part2`, V). F8 (80 percent) at the flight start; the lead's state byte reads 0x23 from the next samples with the throttle key set (k = 01) and `+0x2B` rising from 9 to 97 in 12 samples of 10 blanks; the player on the runway reaches an indicated speed of 2,313 and stays on the ground (it was not flown); the lead's state 0x25 (pitch command) from about step 180 to 240 and state 0x26 from step 242 (these durations are those of the real climb until the altitude was moved with `poke`: the poke is at 0x25, `F:4BA54` = 0x23F800, so the climb time beyond the poke is not a measurement). State 0x0A for 60 steps (4C = 59 at the first sample), then state 0, `V:4A852` = 0xFF and `V:4AB98` = 0x002E (entry 46: the prompt of lesson 1) two steps later. RETURN: the player's record jumps behind the lead (distance 99,761 position units; the formula's 99,760), throttle byte 97 (the lead's), class 0x11, `W2` 0, `V:4A852` = 0xFE; a second RETURN: lesson number 1 and the prompt id 0x31 (entry 49, the break turn: lesson 1 was skipped); the trigger (SPACE): lesson 2, `V:4AB98` = 0x1A (entry 26) and the break turn states 0x28, 2 (roll to 7,112 = 89 degrees) and 7 (the pull: key 0x20) in the order of 8.5.

**Part 2 keys** (`flow-training-part2-keys`, first run of the script with a different order, V): S toggled `W2` bit 0x1000 of the instructor (0x0100 to 0x1100 and back); SPACE with `V:4A852` = 0xFF started lesson 1 at once (no RETURN is needed; the re-position only places the player); RETURN while lesson 1 ran gave `V:4AB98` = 0x401F (entry 31) for about 11 samples of 10 blanks (the timed information) and changed nothing else; SPACE during the end routine's wait changed nothing.

Flying the player through the instructor (F10 from the formation point) did not collide: the +9 unit offset clears it (distance 30,606 units at the closest sample) |
| the player's crash in part 2 (staged: the player's altitude poked to 0x40000, then UP held 2 s) | `flow-training-part2-crash` | crash bit `W0` 0x0200 at sample 15 (y 0), the crash wait state `0x138D8`, then the game-over state `0x13A36` with text id 0x63 (the F/A-18 text), then mode byte 0 and the main menu (texts 6): the first crash ends part 2 (allowance 1) [V: 1 run; note: UP is the stick-forward key, a 9 s hold makes a loop, not a dive] |
