| 0x45 | ESC | leave-flight request `V:4A8E2` = 1; with a shift flag -1 (SHIFT-ESC: back to the menu) | manual, menu text | V: note 05 17.17 rule 9; section 5 |
| 0x31, 0x46 | Z (not while `V:4A898` = 2), DEL | **removes and re-installs the vertical-blank server that reads the mouse** (the two library calls at `0x195DC` and `0x195A6`); no visible effect | **U** | V: `flow-egg-keys-select.txt` Z and DEL at the location selection: nothing changed (`V:4A900`, phase, pause); sweep rows 31 and 46 inert |
| 0x66, 0x67 | left, right Amiga key | set the flag `V:4A931`, `V:4A932`; **with the left flag, F1-F10 act as location digits with the indices 10 to 19** where the digit keys are live (5.6; in flight the same selection code is inert, below) | **U** | V: `flow-egg-amiga-f1.txt`, 1 run: left Amiga key, then F1 at the location selection: `V:4A900` = 10 and the aircraft selection followed (the throttle preset stayed 0); `flow-egg-keys-select.txt`: Amiga key then 2 chose location 2 as the plain 2 does (the flag is cleared by the next event) |
| all other raw codes | | no handler: ignored (they only reach the typed mailbox) | | V: the sweep, 60 of the 101 codes moved none of the 47 watched cells, among them every code that the chain does not name; the 41 that did are exactly the handled ones above plus two noise rows (0x08, 0x09 `W0` flicker) and the lamp counters |

The digits 1-9, 0 and F-keys with the Amiga flag also reach the selection code in flight, where they do nothing: the location writer is guarded by bit 0x0800 of `V:4A984`, which the mission start sets (note 05 17.17 rule 4) [V: sweep rows 01-0A, `V:4A900` and `V:4A86D` unchanged in all ten].

### 11.3 Selection screens: extra location digits (the rule is settled in 5.6)

The caption of the location selection lists the numbers 1 to 4. **Part A, 5.6, is the authority for all ten digit keys** (captured there: `V:4A900` read 0 to 9 after the keys 1 to 9 and 0, the starts of keys 5 and 6 at airfield 1, keys 7 to 0 out-of-table starts with the positions listed there, headings 0 for the keys 7 to 9 and 256,000 for key 0 [R], unsettled). The writer `0x1EBBA` accepts every digit and the keypad digits 1 to 8 because the walk of the start table `0x47ADE` accepts every index from 3 up. What this section adds, captured with the key census: **key 5** (`flow-egg-loc5b.txt`: `V:4A900` = 4, the aircraft selection followed, the flight began at airfield 1's block-0 position with the selector back at 0) and **key 6** (`flow-egg-loc5.txt`: `V:4A900` = 5, the same start, selector back at 0): both 1 run, agreeing with 5.6; I did not capture the keys 7, 8, 9 and 0 (Part A did). The **left Amiga key followed by F1** (`flow-egg-amiga-f1.txt`, 1 run) stores 10 in `V:4A900` and goes on to the aircraft selection; the position read after that start equals airfield 1's block-0 position and the selector still read 10 (the start routine did not clear it); 5.6 reads the same rule for F1-F10 (indices 10 to 19, R) and only this one index was run. **Port rule (C): the one of 5.6** (keys 1 to 4, keys 5 and 6 as the airfield-1 start, keys 7 to 0 and the F-keys ignored, NOTE-GAP); the extra digits are listed here for the easter-egg document, not as a feature to build.

At the aircraft selection (`V:4A849` = 1) only the digit 1 (value 0x11, the F/A-18) and 2 (0x10, the F-16) act; every other digit is ignored; the keypad digits KP1 and KP2 do the same [R, V for 1 in `flow-egg-loc5.txt`].

Menu digits, F-keys (the mission list) and SHIFT-2 belong to sections 5 and 10.

### 11.4 Mouse and joystick (answers to note 05 open question 12)

| input | what the original does | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| mouse movement (control port 1) | the vertical-blank server `0x192DE` reads the mouse counter at `0xDFF00A`: x moves `V:4A82E`, y the throttle lever `V:4A830` (note 05 17.4) | R, rig (17.4) |
| left mouse button | the system's gameport events (`0x68` down, `0xE8` up) call the fire bodies `0x1F07A` and `0x1F0D0`: the same as SPACE press and release | R (`0x18FFE`) |
| the two buttons as a state word | `0x192AC` builds `V:4A836`: bit 0 = left, bit 1 = right. **The left bit is always set**: the routine reads a byte of the program's own data (`0x1A84A`, never written, zero) where it should read the hardware input byte at `0xBFE001`, so "button down" is read permanently (V: `V:4A836` = 1 and `V:4A838` = 1 in the airborne snapshot with no mouse input, still 1 after a click, `0xBFE001` itself reading 0xFC) | V (`flow-egg-mouse.txt`, one snapshot, one click), R for the rest |
| right mouse button | with the permanent left bit the right button alone makes the state 3, which calls `0x15E84`: when fuel is left, the pointer throttle is on (`V:4A890`) and the right button just went down, the throttle preset is set to the afterburner value 0x79 if the throttle is at 0x78 or more, or back to 0x77 when `W2` bit 0x0008 is set: **the right button is an afterburner toggle at full throttle** | R (not run: the driver has no right button) |
| joystick (control port 2) | `0x1906C` reads the joystick counter at `0xDFF00C` each poll and calls the arrow-key bodies: up = UP, down = DOWN, left = LEFT, right = RIGHT, held until the centre; the joystick fire button is not read | R |
| gameport.device | opened once for the mouse events (unit 1, controller type 3); the routine at `0x18D8A` that would inject a key from a gameport event is never called | R (scan: no reference) |

### 11.5 Jokes, hidden screens and the crash message

| item | trigger | capture |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The game-over joke screen** (the two game-over records of section 2.4, ids 0x62 for the F-16 and 0x63 for the F/A-18: a taunt addressed to the pilot about the price of the aircraft) | the crash allowance `V:4A94F` reaches 0: 5 crashes in free flight (mode 1), 3 in the other modes, 1 in the practice mode 0x7D (note 05 17.17 rule 7); the id is 0x62 when `V:4A901` is 0x10 | V: `crash-flow-gameover.txt`, `crash-gameover-gate.txt`, frames `crash-gameover-*.ppm` (note 01b) |
| **The picture checksum screen** (the integrity-failure record of section 2.4, id 0x61: the integrity-failure line followed by a flag digit and four hexadecimal digits) | at start-up the program sums the bitplanes of the three pictures it loads (`pix/splsh`, `pix/inst5`, `pix/frnt5`; the routine `0x29274`: per word a rotate left by 2 and a 16-bit add, over the four planes) and compares with 0xC560, 0x7E70 and 0x4DE8; any difference shows the screen (a digit that is 1, 2 or 4 for the first, second or third picture alone, the OR of the codes when several differ, then the computed sum of the last differing picture) and the program waits there for ever (the state re-arms itself) | V: `flow-egg-checksum.txt`: a copy of the autoboot disk with one bit changed in the literal run of `pix/frnt5` (xdftool, 1 bit of 988 bytes): the screen shows the digit 4 and the sum 0x4DEC, still there 170 s after boot (frames `egg-checksum-*.ppm`) |
| the **clearance-failure and security-failure texts** (records of section 2.4) | the countercode failures and the mission-lock messages (ids 0x43, 0x5F); the countercode check is not reproduced (note 09) | note 09 |
| the **date shown in the briefing and log** | hours flown (`+8` of the record divided by 3600) pick one of four month names stored reversed in the code at `0x292CC` (the day is the hours modulo 32 plus 1, at most 30): not a joke, a fictional calendar that advances with flight time | R (`0x292F0`) |

There is no text that reacts to a name, a score, a key chord or a date: the only "humour" in the program is the game-over line, the mission texts and the credits.

### 11.6 Dead code and unreachable content

**Dead key bodies** (instructions of the handler region with no reference from anywhere, found by the reachability pass, 10 islands [R]):

| address | what it does | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `0x1DE8A`-`0x1DECC` | toggles the HUD vertical offset words `V:4A990` (0 or -1) and `V:4A9D0` (0 or -40) and the window row (0x90 or 0x8F) | the interactive form of the HUD shift. **The only live writers of `V:4A990` and `V:4A9D0` are the jolt routine `0x29A5E` (`0x29AC2`-`0x29AE0`: a four-frame one-scanline shake of the 3D window at touchdowns, lift-offs and hits; note 06 open question 1) and the clears at `0x1F052`, `0x1F058` and `0x29AFC`**; this answers note 06 open question 1 |
| `0x1DEEA` | toggles the look-at flag `V:4A86C` | dead |
| `0x1EBDE`-`0x1EBF0` | sets `V:4A85B` = 0xFF and, in an outside view, takes the KP-ENTER path | dead |
| `0x1EC0E`, `0x1EDF8`-`0x1EE08` | a no-op, and "pause on" (sets `V:4A866` = 1 and clears the low three bits of `V:4A992`) | dead |
| `0x1ECA4`-`0x1ECB4` | clears bit 6 of an entry; calls `0x1F11E` | **`0x1F11E` is the rearm routine: it refills the fuel (`V:4B2AE` = 0x61A800), the stores and the lamp counters and clears the three weapon records; it is called only from `0x170A6` (the landing refuel path) and from this dead key body**, so no key rearms |
| `0x1EEDC`, `0x1D946`-`0x1D952`, `0x1EFEA`-`0x1EFEE`, `0x1E800`-`0x1E82C` | single `bra`, an operand fragment, an operand fragment, and the head-yaw step table (data) | not code |

**The demonstration recorder and its dead store routines** [R; section 7]: the recorder `0x2A1A6` runs only for `V:4A903` = 0 or 1 with `V:4A848` non-zero; `V:4A848` is set only by the demonstration setup (`0x11C24`), which sets `V:4A903` to 3 straight after and keeps `V:4A848` non-zero for the demo flight, and is cleared by every mission initialisation (`0x1D35A`); `V:4A903` is never set to 1, 2 or 0xFD (-3) anywhere. So: the recording branch, the dev modes 1 (re-record the second event word over a replay), 2 and -3 (F-keys select cockpit views) cannot be entered in play. The routines that save and load a recording to `text/textply` and `text/textctl` (`0x18732`, `0x187BE`) have no caller; the loader `0x18668`, which runs once at start-up, **creates empty files of those names when it cannot open them** (the open mode 1006 in its fallback) on a writable disk. When the recording buffer fills (7812 steps), `0x2A200` sets `V:4A903` = 4 and pauses; unreachable.

**Text ids that no path posts.** The id table is at `0x43DFE` (hunk 64): 110 words (ids 1 to 0x6E), each an offset from the table start to a record; the six-word table at `0x43EDA` gives, per mission mode 3 to 8, the offset of a list of five ids (briefing 0 and 1, "confirmed" 3, debrief 4; index 2 is the id 0x5F, also posted directly). Posters found [R]: 58 distinct ids written as immediates by the state routines (`0x11C78`-`0x13B90`, `0x29496`), 24 ids from the mission lists (indexes 0, 1, 3 and 4 of the six lists: a, a + 1, a + 3 and a + 4 of each base, section 2.1) and the five mission-list ids 0x4D-0x51 copied from the table at `0x43DF4`. The 23 ids with **no poster** (also with the flag bits 0x4000 and 0x8000 stripped) are:

| ids | what they are | note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0x05 | the same record as id 1 (the first-tour welcome) | alias |
| 0x08, 0x19 | the same record as id 7 (the location selection; 0x19 is one byte earlier) | alias |
| 0x0B, 0x0C, 0x0D | three 7-byte records at the end of hunk 64 holding a position and colour and an empty string | placeholders |
| 0x10, 0x11 | empty records (three 0xFF bytes at `0x45FB3` and `0x45FB4`, Part A 2.4) | empty |
| 0x12, 0x13 | offsets `0x45FB5` and `0x45FB6`: inside the same run of 0xFF bytes, a misaligned read that would run into the start of the 0x14 record | filler |
| 0x14 | the pause-key hint line (record at `0x45FB7`; Part A 2.4, no writer found): **the one record with words that nothing posts** | unposted text |
| 0x15 | offset 0: points at the id table itself | null |
| 0x16 | an empty record (Part A 2.4) | empty |
| 0x17 | the same record as id 0x1A: the first mission briefing (visual confirmation operation) | alias |
| seven ids (the a+2 fillers of the rule of 2.1) | the security-failure record (the same as 0x5F, the security-failure notice of section 2.4) | seven aliases of 0x5F |
| 0x2D | alias of 0x42 (stolen aircraft beyond recovery) | alias |
| 0x52 | the second form of the menu footer line about SHIFT-ESC (0x53 is used) | duplicate |

So **every distinct text record with words except the pause-key hint (0x14) is posted by some path** (R: the posters above cannot be proven complete for ids computed at run time, but the only computed ids are the mission lists); the other 22 unreferenced ids are aliases, fillers, a null entry or empty records. This agrees with Part A 2.4 ("ids no state writes"). The records of hunk 64 are 239 strings in 110 ids; the 14 files `text/text201`, `texti0a`, `texti0b`, `texti1`-`texti5`, `textegn`, `textegn2`, `texttre`, `textger`, `textcpt`, `textwnd` are **not text** (no words in any of them; the names are the only text-like thing about them) and `textply`, `textctl` are the demonstration stream (section 7); all 14 data names are referenced directly by the boot loader (`0x19664`-`0x19BEA`).

**Other disk content.** The disk carries the Workbench 1.2 boot files: `c` (6 commands), `l` (3 handlers), `libs` (3 libraries), `devs` (the example mount list, the 12 keymaps, an empty clipboard directory), empty `fonts` and `System`, two icons and the start-up sequence that binds drivers, loads the Workbench and ends the shell: the game starts from its Workbench icon. None of it is read by the game program (R: the program names only `df0:pix/*`, `df0:text/*` and `df0:config`).

### 11.7 Cheats

None found. Writers of the crash allowance `V:4A94F` are the three mission-start constants (3, 5, 1) and the crash decrement; no key changes fuel (`V:4B2AE`: the burn at `0x1561E` and `0x17128`, the landing refuel at `0x1716E`, the dead rearm above), damage, weapons or the allowance. The only mode-dependent keys are those in 11.2 (S, RETURN, SPACE in mode 0x7D and 2) and the digit/F-key menu (section 5). The one input that "cheats" by accident is the always-set left button bit of 11.4, which turns the right mouse button into an afterburner toggle.

### 11.8 Obligations

For **Task 9** (`docs/easter-eggs.md`): the items to document are the extra location digits (11.3), Z and DEL (11.2), the Amiga-key chord (11.2), the right mouse button (11.4, marked unverified), the checksum screen (11.5, with the one-bit change as the trigger; the port does not check pictures), the game-over joke (11.5) and the S key; the dead code of 11.6 belongs in the notes only. For **Tasks 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8**: (1) no key repeat in the original: the port must act on press and release events once (a frontend repeat filter is correct, section 5 of this note); (2) the shift and Amiga flags last for one following key event (11.1), which the port can reproduce as "shift held while the key goes down"; the SHIFT-ESC and SHIFT-E chords are the only flight chords; (3) the keys the port must keep for the lessons: S (practice only), RETURN (section 8), the view keys and ESC; (4) the port drops the countercode, the checksum and the recorder; (5) the keypad digits as menu digits (11.1 phase M) are original behaviour to keep in the menu screens.

## 12. The mission scripts (Part C)

Scope: the six missions F1 to F6 (game modes 3 to 8) and what ends them: the per-update dispatcher, the six scripts, the placement rows and region triggers that feed them, the end codes and what sets each, the delay and the request that hand the result to the debrief (5.13), the interplay with the pause byte, a crash restart, a pause and ESC. The briefing chain is section 13, scoring and the counters section 14. Tags as in the header: **V** with the count stated (here: the Musashi rig, `tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/mission_check.py`), **R** read from the code, **C** port rule. Game text is identified by role only.

**What the six missions are** (the roles are read from the briefing and outcome records of 2.4, in the author's words; the figures in brackets are the mode):

| F key | mode | objective as the original poses it | how the script judges it |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| F1 | 3 | identify an unknown aircraft approaching the carrier without opening fire unless fired upon; then return to the airfield | success when 31 qualifying radar contacts have been counted with a target designated (12.5) |
| F2 | 4 | two MiG-29s attack Air Force One (a class 0x14 aircraft) which is damaged and trying to land at San Francisco; destroy the attackers | success when Air Force One is down on the ground, slow, and both attackers are gone; failure when it is destroyed (whether the passenger was aboard is chosen by the step counter's parity at the debrief) |
| F3 | 5 | two stolen F-16 test aircraft with two MiG-29 escorts head for the eastern edge; force them back without a fight | success when the kills reach the cap (2: the escorts, of which one is placed at progress 0 and the other comes from region 2 or after a completion) after the player has shadowed the thieves for 200 passes; failure when a thief's cell x reaches 0x70 |
| F4 | 6 | a downed pilot near the Farallon Islands; fight through the bandits, find the pilot and deploy the rescue pod at the site | the parachute released with SHIFT and F must land within 0x10000 units (both horizontal axes) of the site marker (record 11), else failure |
| F5 | 7 | a cruise missile with a nuclear warhead is inbound to Moffett Field; intercept it | success when the missile is wrecked before it arrives; failure when it arrives (the debrief then tells of an explosion, and by the step counter's parity either of nuclear destruction (flight over, menu) or of no detonation (flight resumes)) |
| F6 | 8 | a submarine carrier is the origin of the enemy aircraft; fly in low and destroy it while it is surfaced, against fighter cover | success when the number of destroyed counted aircraft reaches the cap 4 (the submarine itself is not counted: 12.9) |

### 12.1 Where the scripts run

All of it is in hunk 18 (`0x284F8`-`0x28B53`); the triggers and the placement are in hunk 26 (`0x2CE36`-`0x2D644`) and the data in hunk 27 (the table `0x2DEDA`, the regions `0x2DE28`, the position blocks `0x2DCE8`).

| what | address | note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the end-of-update routine | `0x2852E` | the last call of the world update `0x26AF8` (at `0x26F1E`), so **once per pass of the world update, after the player step and the entity loop of the same pass, also in the passes where the pause byte `V:4A866` is non-zero** (5.14: the world update runs, the player step does not) [R; V: the rig calls it with the pause byte 0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 0xFE and 0xFF] |
| its steps | `0x2852E`-`0x28557` | clears `V:4A872`, `V:4A873`, `V:4A874` (the weapon-fire request bytes, note 07), runs the stale-lock drop `0x26F28`, then dispatches |
| the stale-lock drop | `0x26F28` | the target lock `V:4AA78` (a record offset, -1 for none) is dropped (set to -1, `V:4A920` = 0, `V:4A9F2` = -1) when the locked record is not valid (`W0` bit 0x0040 clear) or is out of control (`B20` bit 0x02 set) |
| gates | `0x28546`, `0x28550` | the dispatcher acts only when bit 6 of the simulation word (`V:4A985` bit 6, the 0x0040 of `V:4A984` set by the mission start, 6.2) is set and the demo flag `V:4A848` is 0 (so a demo replay and a recording run no script, 7.6); a mode byte of 2 or less (signed) does nothing |
| the mode switch | `0x28558`-`0x285BC` | 3: `0x285C0`; 4: `0x285EC`; 5: `0x2872A`; 6: `0x28884`; 7: `0x28908`; 9: `0x28A18` (qualification, 9.3); 0x7D: `0x28A5C` (training part 2, 8.9); **every other value, mode 8 included: `0x28A8C`, the kill-count script** |
| the shared end | `0x28ACE` | stores the delay `V:4A978` = D0 (a word), the request `V:4A8E2` = 3, its countdown `V:4A8E4` = 4 and the key lock `V:4A965` = 1 |
| the tail | `0x28AEE`-`0x28B11` | runs instead of a script whenever the end code `V:4A850` is not 0: code 0xFF or 1 -> the landing test; every other code (0xFE, 0xFD, 0xFC, 4, 0xF0) -> nothing. **The tail has no request guard**: while the code is still 0xFF (the 6 passes before the debrief and the delay) a landing that passes the test stores 0xFC at once, with delay 0 and a fresh request 3, and the debrief then shows the completion text and never the success text; this needs a landed, stopped aircraft after a take-off in that window [R; rig: the tail cases of mission_check.py] |
| the landing test | `0x28B12` | below |

The scripts read these fields of the 16 records (record n at `0x4B23C` + 0x200 n; note 05 section 1 and note 07 give the full layout): `W0` (+0x00) bit 0x8000 (hit this step), 0x0080 (the cruise missile's "still flying" flag, F5), 0x0040 valid, 0x0008 "not counted" (the placement's bit-15 flag: set = not a hostile); `W2` (+0x02) bit 0x0080 on the ground; +0x06/+0x0C cell x and fine x (zero together = never placed); `B4` (+0x04); +0x14/+0x18/+0x1C position; +0x20 (`B20`) bit 0x80 and 0x02; +0x21 bit 0 (set at the first lift-off); +0x3A the destination index (the AI's current route head); +0x4C the counter (the dying countdown); +0x68 heading; +0x6C target speed; +0x6E indicated speed. Slots used: 0 the player, 4 and 6 the mission's principal aircraft, 8 and 10 (12) the fighters, 11 the pod, 14 the carrier, 15 the submarine.

### 12.2 The shared rules

**The end codes.** `V:4A850` is 0 while a mission runs; the scripts and the crash path store:

| code | stored by | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0xFF | the success branch of scripts 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 (and 9) | success; the debrief (5.13) types the outcome, the flight resumes and the code becomes 1 |
| 0xFE | the failure branches of 4, 5, 6, 7 (and 0x7D) | failure; text, then code 2 and the flight resumes (or the menu, when the debrief turns it into 0xF0) |
| 0xFD | the failure branch of 4 when `B20` bit 0x80 of slot 4 is set | the player destroyed Air Force One himself |
| 0xFC | the tail, when the code is 0xFF or 1 and the landing test passes | mission complete: debrief text, log update (14.2), code 4 |
| 4 | the crash paths `0x16FC2` (hard touchdown) and `0x2A8D2` (collision) | after a crash the tail ignores code 4: **no script can end the mission until the ESC restart clears the code** (9.4 shows the same for the qualification) |
| 0xF0, 0xEF | the debrief | final screens (5.13) |

**The same guard everywhere.** A script stores its code only when the request byte `V:4A8E2` is not 3, so the end code arrives once. A pending request of another kind (1 or 0xFF from ESC and SHIFT-ESC, 2 from the ejection seat) does **not** stop a script from overwriting the request with 3 inside the 6-pass window (`V:4A8E4` reads 4 in the store pass and 3, 2, 1, 0, -1 in the next five: the dispatcher `0x11748` acts when it is negative, that is 6 passes after the store, 5.13); conversely the seat's own store of request 2 (`0x27C58`) is skipped while any request is pending, so a script that has stored its 3 first keeps it (Review Focus 3: the order is the one that first finds the request byte free for it; a request 3 already pending is never replaced) [R].

**The delay** `V:4A978` (D0 at `0x28ACE`) is copied by the request-3 handling (`0x1190E`) into the countdown `V:4AB8E`; the state `0x131C8` waits for it to run out before it freezes the world, so the flight goes on for that many passes: the scripts store 4 (success of F2: four more passes, the tracking camera follows the landing), 7 (failure of F5: seven passes of the cruise missile's arrival in the tracking camera), the quirk value below (success of F6) and 0 in every other case. **Quirk of the F6 tail** (`0x28AC6` path): the routine loads only the low byte of D0 (`V:4A962`, the cap) and stores the whole word, so the delay is `(high byte of the lock offset) * 256 + cap`, where the lock offset is `V:4AA78` as it stood when the pass began (the stale-lock drop of the same pass has already set it to -1 in memory, but D0 holds the old value). With no lock held (-1) the word is 0xFF04, negative: no delay. With a lock on a record the delay is the record offset's high byte (0x04 for record 2, 0x08 for record 4, 0x10 for record 8, 0x1E for record 15) times 256 plus 4, i.e. between 1,028 (a lock on record 2) and 7,684 passes (record 15) [V: rig, mission_check.py mode 8 and 0x7F cases, about 100 over three seeds, with the lock offsets 0x0400 to 0x1E00 and -1; the emulator captures of both cases are in 12.12]. The port keeps the rule (C): `delay = (lock >> 8) << 8 | cap` as a signed 16-bit word, negative = 0.

**The pause byte as the scripts' countdown.** Scripts 4, 5, 7 and 8 do not count in a variable of their own: they use `V:4A866`, the same byte as the pause and freeze flag (5.14). The pattern: when the trigger holds, `V:4A866` is 0 -> loaded with the start value (5 in F2, 2 in F5, F3 and F6) -> decremented by one per pass. While it is positive the world is frozen (the player step, the entity timers and the step counter are skipped, 5.14; the flight state tests only the *negative* value for the pause screen, so there is no pause picture) and the script runs once per frozen pass; when it reaches 0 the script acts and the world runs again. F2 and F5 use the pass in which the byte reads 1 for the **external-view hand-over**: once (the flag is bit 7 of `V:4A876`, which receives the old camera direction `V:4A96A` with bit 7 set), the view selector `V:4A83D` is saved in `V:4A8EC`, and, when the principal aircraft is still valid, `V:4A96A` = 7, `V:4A86D` = `V:4A86C` = 1, `V:4A865` = 0; if the cockpit was the view, the heading of the aircraft is saved in `V:4A9E2`, the view-toggle request (`V:4A97E` bit 1) is set and `V:4A8EB` = 0xFF (F2) or 3 (F5): the debrief's resume (`0x1305E`/`0x130BA`) restores the view and the direction from these bytes. In the pass where the byte reaches 0 the tracking-camera routine `0x1EC14` is called for slot 4 (F2 when the aircraft is valid, F5 on failure): it sets `V:4A994` = 4, `V:4A996` = 0x800 (not compared in the rig), `V:4A9F2` = -1, `V:4A968` = 0xFE and, in the cockpit view, copies the heading to `V:4A9E2`, in an external view sets `V:4A83D` from `V:4A8EB`, and `V:4A8EE` = 3 (note 07 section 4 owns the camera).
 The consequences (the counts, the hand-over and the codes are rig-checked; the P-key behaviour is read from 5.14 and observed in the emulator, 12.12): **P during the count** (the pause key, 5.14, sees the byte non-zero and *clears* it without a pause screen; the next pass in which the trigger still holds restarts the count from the start value) and **P at a moment the byte is 0** (it becomes 0xFF; the flight state starts the pause screen at its next pass and the script, which runs only in world updates, does not run until the resume; a script that finds the byte 0xFF decrements it to 0xFE and treats a value of 1 or less, signed, as the hand-over pass; it never counts down to 0 in under 254 passes, which the pause screen's own handling pre-empts since no world update runs meanwhile). The ESC request (1) and SHIFT-ESC (0xFF) are taken by the dispatcher whatever the byte is (5.11) [R; V of the stores and the counts: the rig, mission_check.py with the byte at 0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 0xFE, 0xFF; the emulator behaviour of P in the count: 12.11].

**The landing test and the completion** (`0x28B12`, called for code 0xFF or 1): the player record's `+0x21` bit 0 set (airborne at least once), `W2` bit 0x0080 (on the ground), then **at the carrier start** (`V:4A900` = 3) `B4` & 0xC0 non-zero (any deck point under the aircraft) or **at an airfield start** `B4` bit 0x04 (over the pad), and the indicated speed `+0x6E` equals 0. When it passes: code 0xFC, delay 0, request 3, countdown 4, keys locked: the debrief types the completion text and updates and saves the log (5.13, 14.2). No other condition: no time limit, no fuel test, no check of damage [V: rig, the tail cases of mission_check.py, 18,900 states in all].

### 12.3 Placement: the row, the variant, the sub-list, the cap

Section 6.1 gives the rules; this section adds what they yield per mission and two consequences the scripts depend on.

- **Variant** (the draw of `V:4ABB0`, kept in `V:4ABD4` across an ESC restart): draw 0 or 1 gives variant 0, draw 2 variant 1, draw 3 variant 2. Rows 1, 2 and 4 to 10 have the same list for every variant; **only mode 3 has three list headers**, of which variants 0 and 2 resolve to the same four sub-lists and variant 1 differs in sub-list 0 only (below).
- **Sub-list** = the completion count of the mission (0 to 3, `V:1D024`+0x12+mode, 10.2): the mission gets harder each time it is completed (a third fighter, other positions).
- **The cap** `V:4A962` and the count `V:4A961` apply to **every entry** (the test `cap - count > 0` precedes the class test, `0x2D2D6`-`0x2D2E2`), not only to the aircraft: an entry is skipped once the count has reached the cap. The count rises by one per placed entry of the aircraft class nibble (0x10 to 0x1F, **the cruise missile 0x15 included**) whose "not counted" flag (entry word 4 bit 15) is clear. Consequence for F5: the cap is 2, the missile counts as one, the first fighter as the second, **the second fighter of the sub-list is never placed**.
- **Kills** `V:4A963` rise by one in the dying path (`0x2A32A`, after the wreck's counter has run out) for a record of class nibble 0x10 other than 0x15, with the "not counted" flag clear and `W2` bit 0x0080 (on the ground) clear [R]. So a thief or airliner that is not counted never adds a kill; an aircraft that dies on the ground does not count.
- **Turning a neutral aircraft hostile** (`0x28126`-`0x28142`): an AI routine that moves a record out of the neutral state 8 (flag bit 14 of the entry: the stolen F-16s, Air Force One, the 707 of the variant 1 of F1) clears its "not counted" flag and adds one to **both** the count and the cap. In F3 this happens when the player has fired a weapon (`V:4A86F`, set by the fire routine `0x275C4` for the player's weapon slots 1 to 3, cleared by the AI at every decision): the thieves become fighters and every one of them has to be shot down for the cap to be met (cap 2 -> 3 -> 4). [R; the AI rule is note 07]
- **Progress 0 is the state of every program start** (14.3): the entries with a threshold of 1 or 2 are not placed until the progress counter has been raised by completions. A first run therefore has **one** counted fighter in F1 (MiG-29 slot 8; the MiG-25 of slot 10 waits for progress 1), F2 (slot 8; slot 10 needs progress 2), F3 (slot 8; slot 10 needs 1), F4 (slot 8; slot 10 needs 2), F5 (slot 8; the missile is the second counted entry, so the count is 2) and F6 (slot 8; slot 10 needs 2), against the cap of 2 (4 in F6) [V: emulator, hostile count / cap / kills read 1 / 2 / 0 at the first flight pass of F1, F2, F3 and 1 / 4 / 0 for F6; the others read from the table]. The kill rule of the F3 and F6 scripts (`kills >= cap`) is reachable at progress 0 only through the reinforcement of region 2 (12.4) or, in F3, through thieves turned hostile.
- **The leader/destination word** (entry word 2): the fighters of F2 and F6 carry bit 15 clear and the record index 4 (F2) or 0x0E (the carrier, F1, F4, F6): they follow that record. Everything flagged with bit 15 set takes a route (12.4).

Per mission (the carrier, record id 8, class 0x20, slot 14, "not counted", placed first with a random offset, is in every sub-list and not repeated; "thr" is the progress threshold of the entry (placed when `V:4A95F` is at least that); positions are cell pairs):

| mode | cap | sub-list (completion count) | entries (record id, class, slot, notes) |
| ---: | ---: | --- | --- |
| 3 (F1) variant 0 or 2 | 2 | 0 | MiG-29 (6, 0x12) slot 8 at (76,76), follows the carrier; MiG-25 (7, 0x13) slot 10 at (76,76), thr 1 |
| | | 1 | MiG-29 slot 10 at (76,76), follows the carrier; MiG-25 slot 12 at (76,76), thr 1 |
| | | 2 | MiG-29 slots 8 and 10 at (78,68) following the carrier; MiG-29 slot 12 at (70,76), thr 2 (not placed: the cap) |
| | | 3 | MiG-29 slot 8 at (76,76) following the carrier; MiG-29 slot 10 at (78,68), thr 1; MiG-29 slot 12 at (64,55), thr 2 (not placed) |
| 3 (F1) variant 1 | 2 | 0 | 707 airliner (14, 0x16) slot 4, not counted, neutral (state 8), route 0x14 (west edge); MiG-25 slot 8, thr 1 (**at progress 0 this sub-list places no counted aircraft at all**: the neutral airliner is the only aircraft, the briefing camera's target is the airliner, class 0x16, not counted [V rig: `approach_check.py`], and the count/cap read 0 / 2) |
| | | 1 to 3 | as variant 0 |
| 4 (F2) | 2 | 0 to 3 | Air Force One (5, 0x14) slot 4, not counted, neutral, route 1, 2, 3 or 4 by sub-list (to the airfield block (63,71) or (68,61), then to (65,66)); MiG-29 slots 8 and 10 follow slot 4 (the second thr 2); positions (76,73), (75,73) |
| 5 (F3) | 2 | 0 to 3 | F-16 (3, 0x10) slots 4 and 6, not counted, neutral, random offsets, route 0x12 and 0x13 at (65,74) heading east to (126,74); MiG-29 slots 8 and 10 follow slot 4 at (69,73), (70,73) (the second thr 1) |
| 6 (F4) | 2 | 0 to 3 | rescue pod (13, 0x32) slot 11, not counted, at (73,68) / (65,74) / (76,76) / (76,76) for sub-lists 0 to 3; MiG-29 slots 8 and 10 follow the carrier (the second thr 2) |
| 7 (F5) | 2 | 0 to 3 | cruise missile (10, 0x15) slot 4, counted, route 5 to 8 by sub-list, altitude block 176; MiG-29 slot 8 following slot 4 (record 4); **slot 10 not placed (the cap)** |
| 8 (F6) | 4 | 0 to 3 | submarine (12, 0x21) slot 15 at (80,76), not counted by class; MiG-29 slot 8 at (77,75) following the carrier; MiG-29 slot 10 at (75,77), thr 2 |

[R: `tabs.py` dump of the 11 lists; the sub-list content, positions and flags per row; V of the table walk's result in the existing placement captures of 6.1.] Mode 8's table row has the cap 4 and room for two more counted aircraft; where they come from is 12.4.

### 12.4 Region triggers (hunk 26, `0x2D0AA`)

The walk runs **every 16th pass** (when the low nibble of the step counter `V:4A992` is 3, at `0x26B4C`-`0x26B56`; the pause stops the counter, 5.14), only for the modes the scripts serve (above 2, not 0x7D, the demo flag clear). It reads the four regions of `0x2DE28` (6.1) and for each tests the **player's cell** (`V:4B242`, `V:4B244`) against the region's box: on **entering** the box (the region's bit in `V:4A855` was clear) it sets the bit and runs the entry placer `0x2D248` over the region's first *count* entries; on **leaving** it clears the bit and runs `0x2D116`, which sends the region's entries (found by their slot) toward their destination. The regions:

The four boxes (cell ranges) are disk data and are not listed here (the constants table of Part C, row "regions", has their hash); the rest of the rows:

| region | count | entries | modes |
| ---: | ---: | --- | --- |
| 0 | 0 | two F-16 (slots 4, 6), not counted | none (inert: the count is 0) |
| 1 | 0 | two MiG-29 (slots 8, 10) | none (inert) |
| 2 | 1 | MiG-29 slot 12, follows the carrier, at (76,76), thr 1 | every mode the walk serves: 3 to 9, the qualification included (no mode filter) |
| 3 | 1 | cruise missile (10, 0x15) slot 4, route 9 (to (62,71), (60,63)), counted, mode filter 8, at (76,76) | **mode 8 only (the filter in entry word 4 bits 8 to 11)** |

The loop variable that numbers the regions is also the placer's working register, which it leaves at 0 or -1: the bit numbers of the regions after a trigger are shifted (note 07 section 2.5 has the quirk and its rig check, `spawn_check.py`); the F6 consequence is that after the box-2 or box-3 trigger the next region of the same tick uses bit 0 or 1 for its inside flag. The entry placer's rules (the same code places the sub-list entries, 6.1): the mode filter; the slot must be free (`W0` bit 0x0040 clear); the cap test; for the cruise missile class 0x15 a record whose class byte is already 0x15 and whose cell x is non-zero is not placed again; the record is cleared and filled from the entry; the progress threshold is **not** tested for region entries (only in the sub-list walk). Consequences: (1) **in F6 the first entry into box 2 puts a third MiG-29 in the air (slot 12)**; after it has been shot down, leaving and re-entering the box puts another one in, until the count reaches the cap 4; (2) **in F6 entering box 3 launches a cruise missile of the counted class: it takes one of the four counts**, so if it is launched before the fourth fighter is placed only three fighters can ever exist and the kill count (the missile adds no kill) cannot reach the cap [R: the code; V in the emulator: 12.9]; (3) the mode filter keeps the missile out of F5 (whose own list has it in slot 4, mode 7) -- this settles the mode 7/8 question: **the missile of the region is F6's, the missile of F5 belongs to F5's own list**; the F5 text and the F5 script are mode 7 [R, V: the placement of both in 12.8 and 12.9]. The "leave" handler `0x2D116` also leaves alone any entry whose record has a leader assigned (`+0x38` bit 7 with a non-zero index), and sends an entry without destination back to its own start block (record state `+0x7A` 3 or 4 -> 5, `+0x38` = 0x80, `W0` bit 0 cleared). The per-record check `0x2D53C` re-sends a region-born aircraft that is outside its region's box (note 07 section 3.4). The region index stored in `+0x5D` and the per-record check `0x2D53C` that reads it (every 32nd pass, at the record's own phase) are described in note 07 sections 2.5 and 3.4.

### 12.5 Mode 3 (F1): identification

The crash restart (`0x1D538`) calls the same player-start routine `0x1D8B8` and so clears the counter again (note 07 12.3) [R]. The script (`0x285C0`): when the code is 0, **success when the byte `V:4A927` is negative** and the request is not 3: code 0xFF, delay 0. The byte is the "mission-1 counter" of the radar scope walk (note 07 section 12.3, `0x35FC8`-`0x35FDC`; cleared at the player start `0x1D900`, never at the mission start). For every contact of a walk that is valid, not on the ground, inside the scope window (nx, nz as computed there at the fixed shift 13: |nx| <= 27 and -16 <= nz <= 21, **independent of the scope range key**), detected (|x| and |z| of the list entry at most 0x10000 list units, that is 16,777,216 position units, four cells, in each horizontal axis) and of the **aircraft class nibble (0x10, any aircraft, not only the designated one)**, while a target is designated (`V:4AA78` >= 0) and the viewed record is the player (`V:4A996` = 0): the byte goes 0 -> 30 and then down by one; at 0 it is decremented once more to 0xFF. **It becomes negative at the 31st qualifying contact**; it is never reset. The walk runs only in the cockpit view, on two passes in four (the passes with the low two bits of the step counter 0 and 1), or on every pass for the first passes after a page redraw (note 07 12.2); each aircraft in the window adds one per walk. So F1 is won by designating a target and keeping any aircraft within about four cells and inside the scope's window for 31 contacts (49 passes with one aircraft in the capture of C10 (`followup-radar-scan.txt`): the walk rate of two in four plus the second walk of each pass with the scope redraw counter `V:4A8EF` of 2 or 3, 6 seconds at 8 steps a second; 62 passes without any redraw), not necessarily consecutive, in the cockpit view. The class test admits the MiG-29, the MiG-25, the 707 and everything else of nibble 1; the designated target need not be the aircraft counted. Failure: none; the mission has no failure code (there is no failure text for F1; if the player is shot down the crash path applies). After the 0xFF text the flight resumes and **mission complete (0xFC) needs the landing test at the airfield start** (`V:4A900` = 0, F1 starts on the airfield, 6.1). [R for the call and the test, V for the counter's rule: note 07 section 12.3 (`radar_check.py` part 2); the script's reading of the byte: `mission_check.py` mode 3, 700 cases per seed.]

### 12.6 Mode 4 (F2): Air Force One

Slot 4 is Air Force One, slots 8 and 10 the attackers. The script (`0x285EC`), each pass, code 0:

1. `V:4AA6C` = 4. If slot 4's cell x and fine x are both zero, nothing.
2. If slot 4 is valid: nothing unless it is **on the ground (`W2` bit 0x0080), slow (`+0x6C` <= 0x320 = 800, signed) and both slots 8 and 10 are invalid** (both fighters dead or removed).
3. Otherwise (slot 4 gone, or the landing condition just met): the count of 12.2 with start value 5. The passes with the byte at 4, 3, 2 are frozen passes that do nothing; the pass at 1 does the external-view hand-over (view byte 0xFF); at 0:
   - slot 4 valid -> the tracking camera on it, **success**: code 0xFF, delay 4;
   - slot 4 invalid -> **failure**: code 0xFD when `B20` bit 0x80 of slot 4 is set (the player's hit destroyed it), else 0xFE; delay 0.
   Both only when the request is not 3.

The debrief (5.13): success shows the outcome id of the mission list (Air Force One landed safely); 0xFE shows one of two records chosen by **bit 0 of the step counter `V:4A992` at that moment** (passenger aboard or not); 0xFD shows the "you destroyed it" record and ends the flight (0xF0). Note the trigger: an Air Force One that lands while a fighter is alive does nothing; the script waits for both fighters to die; an Air Force One that is destroyed ends the mission at once, even if it was the player who did it.

### 12.7 Mode 5 (F3): the stolen aircraft

Slot 4 and 6 are the thieves (neutral, not counted), 8 and 10 the escorts. Each pass, code 0:

1. The **subject** A is slot 4 if valid, else slot 6 if valid; only a thief that is still *neutral* (`W0` bit 0x0008 set) is a subject. If neither is valid: go to step 5 directly. If the first valid thief is no longer neutral (it was turned hostile, 12.3): go to step 4.
2. **Failure**: the subject's cell x (`+0x06`, signed) is at least 0x70 (112): code 0xFE, delay 0 (request not 3).
3. **Shadowing**: with D = 0x30000, 0x24000 or 0x18000 for the progress counter `V:4A95F` below 2, 2, 3 or above, the player is *near* when the absolute differences of the position longs (x, y, z) are all at most D. Near: if the timer `V:4A83A` is not negative it is decremented by one and the pass ends (go to step 4); if it is negative both thieves are **re-routed** (the destination `+0x2C`-`+0x34` of slots 4 and 6 is set to the first waypoint of the route named by `+0x3A`: cell x, cell z, fine x, fine z, and the height long, the words sign-extended) and the pass goes on to step 4. Far: if the timer is not negative it is set to 200; otherwise it stays negative.
4. If the timer is not negative, end of the pass. (It starts at 0 from the placement; **the first far pass loads 200 and each near pass counts it down, a far pass reloads it**: the player must stay near the subject for 200 consecutive passes, 25 s at 8 passes a second; at -1 the shadowing is in force and every later near pass keeps re-installing the first waypoint of the route, which is the point just east of the start, so the thieves never reach the second waypoint at cell 126.)
5. **Success** test: kills (`V:4A963`) at least the cap (`V:4A962`, 2, or more after a thief was turned hostile): the count of 12.2 with start value 2 (one frozen pass), then code 0xFF, delay 0.

So F3 is won by reaching two kills (the escorts; at progress 0 one escort is placed and the second fighter comes from the reinforcement of region 2, whose box contains the thieves' start) **and** having shadowed a thief for 200 consecutive passes (the second condition can be skipped by killing both thieves: then no subject is valid and step 5 runs at once -- but a thief that has been turned hostile needs its own kill, step 4). The failure text (id 0x2C of 2.4) also ends the flight (0xF0) [5.13].

### 12.8 Mode 7 (F5): the cruise missile

Slot 4 is the missile (class 0x15, counted at placement, route to the base). Each pass, code 0:
1. `V:4AA6C` = 4; if the cell x and fine x are zero, nothing.
2. If slot 4 is valid and `W0` bit 0x0080 is set (the missile is still flying), nothing.
3. Otherwise the count of 12.2 with start value 2; the pass at 1 does the hand-over (view byte 3); at 0: **if `B20` bit 0x02 (out of control) is clear -> failure**: the tracking camera on slot 4, code 0xFE, delay 7 (the arrival is shown); **if set -> success**: code 0xFF, delay 0.
So the missile ends the mission when it stops "flying" (its flag clears at the detonation or when the dying path takes the record); shot down it has `B20` bit 0x02. The debrief (5.13): 0xFE types the "exploded" record and then, by bit 0 of `V:4A992`, the nuclear record (the flight ends, 0xF0) or the no-detonation record (the flight resumes with code 2). The mode of the F5 script is 7; the missile's region entry is filtered to mode 8 and belongs to F6 (12.4).

### 12.9 Mode 8 (F6) and the default script

`0x28A8C` is the script of every mode not named in the switch (8, and 0x7F when the demo flag is clear): code 0; **success when `V:4A963 >= V:4A962`** (signed bytes); then the count with start value 2 (one frozen pass); then code 0xFF with the delay of the quirk in 12.2. Nothing else is tested: the submarine (class 0x21) is not an aircraft class and has no kill counter, so destroying it does not by itself end F6 [R: no other store to `V:4A963`; the kill rule `0x2A32A` lists the class tests]; F6 is won by destroying **four counted aircraft**. Four are available only as 12.4 says (one in the list at progress 0 and two from progress 2, because the second fighter has the threshold 2; one from box 2; a further one when the first of the box-2 fighters has died and the player re-enters the box) and only if box 3's missile is not launched first. The F6 briefing says to destroy the submarine; the original's script does not check it [R, open question C4].

### 12.10 Mode 6 (F4): the rescue pod

The "rescue pod" the briefing speaks of is a **parachute record the player releases**: the key combination SHIFT and F, accepted only in mode 6, creates a class 0x31 record (a copy of the player's first 0xA4 bytes with the parachute template, note 07 section 6) when `V:4A97A` is 0 and one of the records 1 to 3 is free, and stores that record's offset in `V:4A97A` (`0x27596`). (The ordinary ejection seat, class 0x30, does not store it.) Slot 11 is the **site marker** of the downed pilot, a class 0x32 object placed by the list. The script (`0x28884`), each pass, code 0:

1. `V:4A97A` is 0 (nothing released): nothing.
2. The parachute record must have `W0` bit 0x8000 set (it has landed: the parachute's step sets the bit when its height reaches 0 or less, note 07 section 6) and its counter `+0x4C` at most 0. The counter starts at -20 and counts up by one per flying pass; after the landing it is set to 4 if it is 5 or more, else counted down to 0. So a parachute that falls for fewer than 20 passes (a release from the deck) is judged in the pass of its landing, and one that has fallen longer is judged four passes after the landing, when the counter has run 4, 3, 2, 1, 0.
3. Compare its x (`+0x14`) and z (`+0x1C`) with the site marker's: **both absolute differences at most 0x10000 (signed compare)**: success, code 0xFF; otherwise failure, code 0xFE; delay 0, request not 3. There is no frozen pass.

So releasing the parachute over the site wins; releasing it elsewhere, or releasing it and letting it land far away, loses (the allowance is not touched, the flight resumes after the text with code 2). A parachute lost in a crash or never released leaves the mission undecided: the site marker has no other rule. The seat's own request 2 (the bail-out ending, 5.13) is made only for class 0x30, so a parachute never produces it. If the player ejects with the ordinary seat the seat's request 2 ends the flight with the bail-out text and no script outcome. [R from `0x28884`, `0x27596`, `0x1EE4E`; V of the script's decision: the rig, mission_check.py mode 6, 700 cases with near and far pairs; the emulator run of the release is 12.11.]

### 12.11 The script state across a crash restart, a pause, an ESC

| event | what the script state does | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| crash restart (note 05 17.17) | the end code is set to 4 by the crash and **kept** by the restart; the tail ignores 4, so the scripts are dead until an ESC restart (or a new selection) clears the flag block; `V:4A83A`, the counts `V:4A961`-`V:4A963`, `V:4A927`, `V:4A876` and the placed records are **not** reset (the placement runs only in the dispatch state `0x1201E`); the allowance falls | R from `0x16FC2`, `0x2A8D2`, `0x28AEE`; rig: code 4 -> no script acts, about a ninth of the 18,900 states carry code 4; V emulator: code 4 poked in F2 stays 4 for 40 samples (12.12) |
| pause (P) | no world update runs while the pause screen is up, so no script runs and the counts stand; a P pressed while a script's countdown byte is positive clears it (12.2). The edge [R, V: exactly one pass, 5.14 "P on the other screens"]: the key handler sets the byte to 0xFF before the flight state notices it, in the same pass, so one world update runs with the byte at 0xFF; a script whose trigger holds then finds the byte non-zero, decrements it to 0xFE, treats a signed value of 1 or less as the hand-over pass (the camera block runs once, if not yet done) and does not count down further; the resume clears the byte, and the count restarts at the start value | R, rig for the byte cases; V: emulator, F2 (12.12) for the clearing |
| ESC / SHIFT-ESC | the request is taken at once (5.11) and the restart sets the pause byte to 1 (the fade-out state), so a script whose trigger holds finds the byte non-zero, decrements it to 0 and **fires in the first pass of `0x11AE2`** (the gate-1 pulse pass): its code is stored, request 3 and its countdown `V:4A8E4` = 4. The reset block of that state clears the **code** (`V:4A850` is in its flag block) **but not the request or its countdown** (`V:4A8E2` and `V:4A8E4` are in none of the 6.1 reset ranges): request 3 lingers with the countdown at 3 for the next samples, is consumed by the menu state `0x11E04`, and the restart then **detours through the menu set-up `0x11D30` for about 9 samples** before `0x11E04` and the dispatch `0x1201E` (the restart without the stray request goes `0x11AE2` -> `0x11E04` -> `0x1201E` directly: the code-4 control run). The hand-over of 12.2 does not run in that pass (the byte jumps from 1 to 0, skipping the pass in which it reads 1), but the tracking-camera call `0x1EC14` of F2 (slot 4 valid) and F5 (failure) does, and what a script wrote to `V:4A96A`, `V:4A8EB`, `V:4A8EC` and `V:4A9E2` **survives the reset** (they lie outside its ranges; `V:4A876` is inside the flag block and is cleared; `V:4A97E` bit 1 and `V:4A994`/`V:4A996` are inside the cleared word range 0x4A978 to 0x4A9DF). Then the dispatch state runs again: the placement, `V:4A850` 0, `V:4A83A` 0 | R from `0x11748`, `0x1D1BE`; V: emulator, F2 (12.12) |
| the outcome screens | the flight resumes (code 1 or 2): the scripts are alive again only in code 1 (the landing test); code 2 is dead | R |
