| **F1, success and completion** [continuing from the snapshot, `V:4A927` at 1; the 0xFC landing staged: the player on the pad with the lift-off bit `+0x21` bit 0 poked; `mission-m3-ok2.txt`, 1 run] | `V:4A927` 1 -> 0xFF; **no frozen pass** (`V:4A866` stayed 0); code 0xFF, request 3, `V:4A8E4` 4, delay 0; `0x131F4` 6 passes later (step counter 0x75 -> 0x7B; delay 0), text 0x1E and 0x8055 (return to base), the code becomes 1 and the flight resumes; after the landing poke the tail stored code 0xFC in the next pass (no frozen pass either), `0x131F4` again, text 0x54 and 0x8053, log: `+0x02` 2 -> 0, `+0x06` 5 -> 3, `+0x07` 1 (the old completion count of mode 3), `+0x38` 3 -> 4, completion byte of mode 3 1 -> 2, `+0x36` 5 |
| **F3, failure** [staged: the first thief made inactive and its x poked to cell 0x70; `mission-m5-fail.txt`] | code 0xFE two passes after the poke, request 3, `V:4A8E4` 4, delay 0, no frozen pass; `0x131F4` 7 passes later; text 0x2C and code 0xF0; `0x13A4C`, `0x13A84`, `0x11A70`, `0x11D30`: the flight ends and the menu follows (5.13) |
| **F3, the shadowing timer** [staged: the first thief inactive, the player poked onto it; `mission-m5-shadow.txt`, one step per sample] | `V:4A83A` read 200 and then fell by **one per world pass** (0xC8, 0xC7 ... 0xC4 while the step counter went 0x49 -> 0x4D); the near test held for the 40 samples |
| **F3, the destination re-installed** [staged: the timer poked to -1, the second thief's destination x poked to cell 126 (its second waypoint), the player put back on the first thief; `mission-m5-shadow.txt`] | within the second world pass the second thief's destination read cell 0x42 (66) again with fine x/z 0x2000, 0x20A0: the first waypoint of the route named by its `+0x3A` (0x13), as 12.7 says |

### 12.13 What the port does (C), for Task 7

1. **One script per step**, called after the player step and the entity loop of the same step and in frozen steps too (the countdown byte needs it); `missions_step` follows 12.1 to 12.10: the gates (simulation switch, demo flag, mode above 2), the mode switch with the default script for every unlisted mode, the tail for code 0xFF and 1. One pass of the original is one step (the world update runs once per pass; the delay and the countdown are counted in passes, so in steps: **a delay of `n` passes is `n` steps**).
2. **The end code once**: every store is guarded by "request not 3"; the store of 3 and the code are made together (12.2). The crash code 4 and the codes 0xFE, 0xFD, 0xF0, 0xEF disable the scripts; 0xFF and 1 enable only the landing test.
3. **The region walk every 16th step** (the low nibble of the step counter is 3), the inside bits, the exit handler and the placer exactly as 12.4 and note 07 2.5; the placement rows from `scenario.bin` (the extractor already carries the rows, lists, regions and blocks, note 02 section 9; nothing new is needed for the scripts, whose constants are in the Constants of Part C).
4. **Progress starts at 0 in every run** (14.3) and rises by one per completion; the sub-list is the completion count; the variant comes from the core's seeded generator (note 07 2.4).
5. **The mission texts**: the lists of 13.3 (ids are numbers, no text in the core); the F2 and F5 parity choices read the step counter at the debrief.
6. **For the manual** (Task 10, in the author's words, no game text): F1, identify an unknown aircraft by designating it and keeping it in view at close range, without firing unless fired upon (the win comes after about eight seconds of close contact with a target designated), then land at the airfield; F2, protect Air Force One by destroying its attackers until it has landed safely (losing it ends the mission; the outcome text depends on whether the president was aboard); F3, shadow the stolen F-16s closely for about 25 seconds so that they turn back, and destroy their escorts (firing a weapon turns the thieves hostile and they must then be shot down too; if one crosses the eastern edge the mission is lost); F4, find the downed pilot's marker and release the parachute pod (SHIFT and F) so that it lands within a short distance of the marker; F5, destroy the inbound cruise missile before it reaches its target (if it arrives the mission is lost and, depending on chance, the game ends in nuclear destruction); F6, destroy four enemy aircraft over the sea near the submarine's position (the submarine itself is not required; avoid flying into the zone beside the submarine, a strip a few cells wide next to its position, before the fighters are down: it launches a cruise missile that uses one of the four counts). After a win the player flies back and lands at the carrier or the airfield to complete the mission and unlock the next. ESC restarts the mission; a crash disables the scoring until the restart.

### 12.14 Answers to the implementation review (N1 to N7)

**N2, a second request 3 while the outcome of the first is running** [R from `0x11820`-`0x11828`, `0x118EC`-`0x1191E`, `0x28AEE`-`0x28B10`]. The per-pass dispatcher handles a negative end code (0xFF, 0xFE, 0xFD, 0xFC, 0xF0, 0xEF) at `0x118EC`: only the request 3 is looked at, in **every state**, and when its countdown is negative it clears the request, copies `V:4A978` into `V:4AB8E` and stores the state `0x131C8`. The scripts and the tail run in every world update, the frozen ones of the delay and of the outcome screen included (the pause byte 1 does not stop the update, 5.14). So while the code is 0xFF (the 6 passes before the debrief, the delay, the typed text) a landing that passes the test (a take-off made, on the ground, on the pad or deck, speed 0) stores 0xFC with delay 0 and a new request 3: after its 6 passes the dispatcher stores `0x131C8` again, the freeze state and `0x131F4` run again and **rebuild the list for the code 0xFC** (the engine is reset first: a success text still being typed is cut and replaced by [0x54, 0x8053]); the log update and the save run once, at that second debrief. If the new request arrives before the first `0x131F4`, the success text is never shown. In flight the aircraft of the frozen screens does not move, so this happens only for a player who is already stopped on the deck or the pad when the success is decided; a port that stops the scripts during the outcome screen differs from the original in exactly that corner. The code 0xFC is not re-armed by itself (the tail ignores 0xFC) and the debrief of 0xFC ends with code 4.

**N3, why the qualification's 0xFF does not become 0xFC.** The tail (`0x28AEE`) is entered only by the scripts of modes 3 to 8 and the kill-count script; the script of mode 9 (`0x28A18`) and that of mode 0x7D (`0x28A5C`) return at their first instruction when the code is not 0, **without the tail**. So mode 9 has no completion: after its 0xFF the debrief gives 0x4A and the restart.

**N4, the pause screen and the world update.** The pause screen is the state `0x13B76`/`0x13BA0` with the gate `V:4A84D` = 0; the frame routine `0x11124` skips the whole world pass (`0x11140`-`0x11146` to `0x114C0`) while the gate is 0. So **during the pause screen the world update does not run: no script, no region tick, no stale-lock drop**. The frozen passes of note 05 17.2 and 5.14 are the passes with the pause byte at 1 to 5 (flow states and the scripts' own countdown) and the gate 1: those run the world update (the entity loop's timers skipped) and the scripts. The edge of 12.11 is the one or two updates between the key and the state change.

**N5, one rule for the notice stage `0x12E4E`.** The stage is a gate-0 text stage, so 3.8 items 1 and 3 apply: it is a stage with a measured duration, typed by the pass machine and scaled to it. The list [0x58, 0x5A] (the same in the demo and in the missions that have a vector notice) is 301 typing passes and a 47-pass hold: **348 passes, 84 + 8 = 92 blanks in the demo's pass-counted run, 90 blanks (typing 7430 -> 7520 including the hold) in F1**; the blanks differ by run because the pass rate does (3.2 to 3.9 passes per blank), the passes do not. The port rule (C) is **14 steps** for the two-record list (`(84 x 8 + 25) / 50` = 13 for the typing, 1 for the hold), and for the single record 0x59 (missions without a vector notice, 6.2) 6 + 1; the 7 steps of the demo reading (section 7) came from dividing 348 passes by 50 and undercount the typed text, whose passes cost about 1/6 blank plus the clicks and glyphs (3.8: about 72 blanks, 12 steps by the fitted model, 14 by the measured stage); Part B's conversion should use the stage rule.

**N6, the ESC-with-trigger detour in stages** [V: `mission-m4-esc.txt` against the control `mission-m4-dead.txt`, samples of 4 blanks, +-4; steps by `(blanks x 8 + 25) / 50`]:

| stage | state | blanks | steps | note |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| the fade-out | `0x11A96` | 32 | 5 | the pause byte 1 from the key on |
| the fade-out end | `0x11AC4` | 4 | 1 | |
| the pulse with the reset | `0x11AE2` | 12 | 2 | **the script fires in the first pass** (code stored, request 3, countdown 4, the pause byte 0); the reset block clears the code in the second pass, not the request |
| the menu state | `0x11E04` | 4 | 1 | request 3 still pending (countdown 3), consumed here |
| **the menu set-up (the detour)** | `0x11D30` | 36 | 6 | the control run goes `0x11E04` -> `0x1201E` directly |
| the menu state again | `0x11E04` | 8 | 2 | |
| the dispatch | `0x1201E` | 4 per 24 passes of the countdown 198 to 0 | as 6.1 | then `0x12534` (the 150-pass restart countdown) |

So the stray request adds 44 blanks (7 steps) to the restart. The port rule (C): when an ESC restart begins in a step in which a script's trigger holds, apply the stray store at the first pass of the `0x11AE2` stage, keep the request and its countdown through the reset, and insert the menu set-up stage (6 steps) and the second menu stage (2 steps) between the first `0x11E04` stage and the dispatch. The code and the hand-over bytes follow 12.11.

**N7, the resume (`0x13056`-`0x13114`, entered from the flight state when the engine byte is negative and the code is 0xFF, 0xFE or 0xFC)** [R]. The code becomes 1, 2 or 4; the gate 1; the engine reset; `V:4A965` = 0; `V:4A968` = 0xFE; the display-list request 0xFF (**unconditionally**, at `0x13094`, before the test of `V:4A994` at `0x130A2`); the pause byte 0. **If the tracking camera is on (`V:4A994` non-zero) the viewed record returns to the player**: `V:4A994` and `V:4A996` are cleared; the view selector `V:4A83D` is restored from the saved `V:4A8EC`; the head offset words `V:4AA3E`, `V:4AA40` and `V:4AA3C` are set for the external view (0x32, 0x320, 0xA7) or the cockpit (0, 0, 0x90); `V:4A85F` = 0; the view refresh `0x1EFF4`; and the ring direction `V:4A96A` is restored from `V:4A876` & 0x7F. **`V:4A876` itself is not cleared and `V:4A8EB`, `V:4A8EC`, `V:4A9E2` are left as they are**, so a second hand-over in the same mission does not happen (the bit 7 of `V:4A876` stays set) and the cockpit toggle request of the hand-over has been consumed by the view code before. Without a tracking camera (F1, F3, F4, F6 and F2 with a lost aircraft) none of this runs: the view stays what it was.

**N1, the length of the camera approach `0x12D58` in steps** [V: the step counter `V:4A992` at the first and the last pass of the state; F2 to F6 `mission-m4-approach.txt` to `m8-approach.txt` (the counter at the first `0x12DB8` pass, one run each, +-1), F1 `mission-brief-m3-b.txt`, the free-flight start `flow-s2-mission-start-long.txt`]. The state counts world passes: it starts with the counter at 35 (0x23) at the carrier start (38 at the airfield start of F1) and ends when the mover sets camera mode 0:

| start | passes | blanks | blanks per pass |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| free flight, location key 1 (airfield 1; mislabelled "carrier start" in the first reading: the log's snapshot was taken after location key 1), older log | 112 | 1,176 | 10.5 |
| F1, airfield start | 121 | 1,295 | 10.7 |
| F2 | 112 (counter 35 -> 147) | | |
| F3 | 110 (35 -> 145) | | |
| F4 | 99 (35 -> 134) | | |
| F5 | 99 (35 -> 134) | | |
| F6 | 99 (35 -> 134) | | |

**The port rule (C) is one step per pass: 112 steps for free flight and F2, 110 for F3, 99 for F4 to F6, 121 for F1 (superseded for the missions by C14 below: the mover's own count from the briefing camera)**, then the 2 + 6 + notice + 4 of 6.4: the 188 steps of 6.4's first reading came from the blank rule applied to a state that runs the world (3.8 exception 4 and 5.1 say one pass per step); the 6.4 text is corrected. **C13, settled** [V + rig: `followup-airfield-start.txt`, `-2.txt` (location keys 1 to 4, the aircraft key 1 for keys 1 to 3, 1 run each, samples every 4 blanks) and `followup-approach-state.txt` (keys 1 and 4: the whole mover state at the entry and every 4 blanks)]: **the mover runs once per pass; the rig's 1,190 calls were the rig's own start state.** The rig had left the camera target `V:4AD0E` at zero, so the camera first flew toward the world origin (about 1,170 calls); with the original's target (below) in place, the rig's mover reproduces the emulator's camera path from the entry state: airfield 1 start 111 of 116 passes equal to the unit (the other 5 samples fall in the middle of a pass, before the mover ran), the carrier start 77 of 102 (the sampled value of the other 25 was taken before that pass's mover call); the rig ends the approach (camera mode 0) after **116 and 102 calls**, the emulator's state `0x12D58` lasts **118 and 104 passes**: calls = passes - 2 (the arrival and cockpit passes of 6.4). **The entry state**: camera mode 6, stage 0, the trail (`V:4AD02`) holding the first damped step, the camera one mover step away from the selection camera (x `0x10800000`, height `0x03000000`, z `0x10C00000`, a constant while the location and aircraft prompts are up; the carrier start, which skips the prompt, shows the fixed camera near the deck for one pass and then the same selection camera one step on), and **the stage 0 target = the player's start position plus 0x5A0000 (90 x 65,536) units along the player's own z axis in the player's frame** (the player's attitude matrix, so it follows the runway heading: the carrier, heading 0, offset (0, 0, -5,898,240); airfield 1 (14,466 / 7,692 Q14 matrix): (+2,769,120, 0, -5,207,760)); it is in place at the first pass of `0x12D58` (its writer was not traced). The mover then follows the stage table above, exactly. **Passes of `0x12D58` per start** (entry to the state `0x12DB8`; the step counter at entry in brackets):

| start | passes | blanks | stage 0 target offset |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| location key 1, airfield 1 (`V:4A900` = 0) | 118 (34 to 152) | 1,200 | (+2,769,120, 0, -5,207,760) |
| location key 2, airfield 2 (`V:4A900` = 1) | 93 (33 to 126) | 864 | from the start block's heading |
| location key 3, airfield 3 (`V:4A900` = 2) | 93 (34 to 127) | 800 | from the start block's heading |
| location key 4, the carrier (`V:4A900` = 3) | 104 (30 to 134) | 964 | (0, 0, -5,898,240) |

The older log of airfield 1 (112 passes, counter 33 to 145) was taken from a snapshot of an earlier boot and not repeated; the new run's 118 is the one that replays in the rig, and the counts depend on the entry camera, so **port rule (C): the approach of a free-flight start lasts the pass count of the table above (118, 93, 93, 104; F1 121 and the carrier missions as the table at the top), then the 2 + 6 + notice + 4 of 6.4; running the mover once per step from the entry state above reproduces the same counts and is the alternative if the port wants the camera path**.

**The HUD step, the window row and the view in every approach pass, by start kind (milestone 7, Task 2; N5 and C13 closed)** [V: `flow7-approach-air1.txt` and `flow7-approach-carrier.txt` (location key 1 with the aircraft key, and key 4: the location snapshot, one group of peeks at the first video frame of each value of the step counter `V:4A992`: the counter, the state, the camera longs, camera mode, mover stage, window row `V:4AA3C`, HUD step `V:4AA3E`, view `V:4A83D`, map flag and HUD switch), `flow7-training-7D.txt` and `flow7-training-2.txt` (training parts 2 and 1 from the countercode answer, every 8 blanks), one run each; a sample shows the state at the first video frame of a counter value, so a change made late in a pass is seen one sample later, +-1 pass]. In every row of the table the map flag `V:4A865` and the HUD switch `V:4A859` are 0. Pass numbers are counted from the entry of the state `0x12D58` (the first sample with the state pointer, pass 0).

| start | entry counter | stage 1 | stage 2 | stage 3 | row 179 -> 167 | stage 4 | stage 5 | stage 6 | camera mode 0 | cockpit (step 0, row 144, view 0) | `0x12DB8` | step, row, view before the row change |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| free flight, airfield 1 (key 1, aircraft key 1) | 34 | 16 | 23 | 45 | 60 | 77 | 88 | 112 | 116 | 117 | 118 | 50, 179, external (view 1) |
| free flight, the carrier (key 4) | 31 | 19 | 27 | 50 | 57 | 76 | 80 | 99 | 103 (with the cockpit) | 103 | 104 | 50, 179, external |
| training part 2 (mode 0x7D, the player start at dispatch) | 17 | (stage 3 from the entry) | | 0 | not applicable (167 from the dispatch) | 1 | 15 | 25 | 29 | 30 | 31 | 50, 167, external |

**The rules** [V from the table; the first is R from the view state routine, read at its tail, and agrees with all four row changes]: (1) **the approach shows the external view with HUD step 50 and window row 179 from its first pass** (the dispatch value, kept by the selection screens), and the cockpit view with step 0 and row 144 only from the pass after the mover has set camera mode 0 (or in the same pass: the carrier run shows both in one sample); (2) **the window row becomes 167 in the first pass in which the camera height long `V:4ACFA` is below 0x24000 while the camera mode is 6 and the view is an external one, and the view refresh runs in that pass**: the camera heights at the passes around the change were 0x25EF2 and 0x23D2E (airfield 1: the row changed in the pass with 0x23D2E) and 0x244FC and 0x22B15 (the carrier: the row changed in the pass with 0x22B15); the training part 2 approach starts with 167 (the dispatch value of its mode) and never changes the row; (3) the step stays 50 in the external view throughout; (4) after the mover's stage 6 has counted its five passes (camera mode 0) the cockpit request is served in the same pass or the next one, and the countdown state `0x12DB8` follows one to two passes later, so the last passes before it are cockpit passes (step 0, row 144, view 0), as 6.4 and note 06 say. **Training part 2** (`flow7-training-7D.txt`): the camera approach has **31 passes** (the counter 17 to 48; 416 blanks, 13.4 blanks a pass), **not the 401 blanks converted to 64 steps** of 8.1: a state that runs the world counts one pass per step (3.8 item 4), so the approach is 31 steps (29 of the mover and the 2 passes of 6.4's arrival and cockpit), and the briefing before it keeps the view external with step 50 and row 167 (the training scene's dispatch values), not the cockpit with step 0 and row 144. **Training part 1** (`flow7-training-2.txt`): no approach state is visited; the countdown state `0x12DB8` follows the briefing (672 blanks after the answer in the run), then `0x12EDA` (40 blanks) and the flight state (24 blanks later), and **step 50, row 167, the external view and camera mode 0 stay constant through the whole of it, the first flight pass included** (the port shows step 0 there). **Port status (milestone 7 code round).** `src/core/mover.c` is the mover of 07 6.2 and runs once per pass of the approach state in the free-flight starts (from the selection camera) and in training part 2 (from stage 3 at its fixed camera point); it reproduces every camera long of the airfield 1 and carrier logs (`flow7-approach-air1`, `-carrier`) and of `followup-approach-state` to the unit, the stage passes of the table above and the 227, 223, 200, 192 passes of the keys 7 to 0 (+-1 pass of sampling). **Open (measured here, not explained): at airfields 2 and 3 (V:4A900 = 1, 2) the original's target and offset targets are built from an attitude matrix one table step (0.1 degree) off the player's start matrix** (airfield 2: the matrix of heading 24801 to 24808 instead of 24800, (12532, 10553) for the sine and cosine words instead of (12551, 10531); airfield 3: the entry of heading 27185 to 27192 instead of 27200, about (5630, 15386) instead of (5604, 15396); `followup-airfield-start` and `-2`, 87 of 87 and 86 of 86 camera longs equal with those words, the start routine of the rig gives the port's matrix); the cause is not found (a heading change between the player start and the approach?), the port uses the player's own matrix there and its camera path differs from the log by a few thousand units after the first passes, and the approach's pass count is the table's (93) with the cockpit forced by the screen. The camera rows of the table are the evidence for the port's test of the mover (`flow7-approach-air1.txt` and `-carrier.txt` carry the camera longs of every pass): the stage changes above and the camera positions are what a mover of 6.4 started from the entry state of this section must reproduce.

**C14, the missions' approach is external too (milestone 7, final round; the "never sampled" of the earlier rounds was wrong)** [V: `sound7-approach.txt` (`tools/vamiga-ref/scripts/sound7-approach.txt`, Task 1 of note 10: an F1 start from the countercode prompt, `mb/m3-prompt.vasnap`, one main-loop pass per sample, 210 samples; it peeks the view byte `V:4A83D`, the mover stage `V:4A86E`, the camera mode `V:4A966`, the step counter `V:4A992`, the pending notice `V:4A968` and the distance word `V:4ABFA` every pass; it does not peek the camera longs, the HUD step or the window row)]. The state `0x12D58` of a mission (modes 3 to 8) shows **the external view in passes 0 to 125** (`V:4A83D` = 1, camera mode 6 until pass 124, 0 at 125) **and the cockpit from pass 126** (view 0; the notice byte clear from 127); the mover reads **stage 8 in passes 0 to 2** (the briefing's stage: the camera is at its target, nothing moves), **stage 0 from pass 3, stage 1 from 27, stage 2 from 35, stage 3 from 56, stage 4 from 86, stage 5 from 97 and stage 6 from 121** (the sample is one pass, +-1). So the mission approach is the free-flight approach with the entry stage 8 for the first passes, and it starts from **the briefing camera's overview position** (13.4 C1: x `0x10C00000`, height `0x05000000`, z `0x11400000`, which the camera holds from the target state to the first pass of `0x12AC0` and past it, to the unit). **The count is the mover's own** (the table of N1 above was measured at one start each; the start the placement draws changes the camera's way): the port's mover, entered in stage 8 at that position with the empty trail, stage 0 from its fourth pass, reproduces the log's stage passes **1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 to the pass** (27, 35, 56, 86, 97) and its stage 6 one pass earlier (120 against 121), so its cockpit pass is 125 against the log's 126 and its count 126 against 127; over the seeds 1 to 12 the count of F1 stays 126 and the others give F2 113 to 126, F3 102 to 125 (109 at four of the twelve), F4 114 to 125, F5 110 to 126 and F6 102 to 125 (113 at ten of them). **Port rule (C): the approach of a mission is the mover's, from the briefing camera's position; its length is the mover's cockpit pass plus one (the free-flight starts' rule: 118 = 117 calls + 1, 104 = 103 + 1); step 50 and the window row 179, then 167 by the height rule, as in the free-flight approach; the cockpit pass (step 0, row 144, view 0) is the last but one of the count.** **R, not peeked:** the HUD step and the rows of a mission approach are the dispatch values of modes 3 to 8 (06 start table: 50 and 179) and the view routine's tail, carried over from the free-flight approach (the two agree there at all four captured starts). **NOTE-GAP (this section): (a) the camera longs of a mission approach were not peeked, so the path from the overview position and the entry trail (the port: empty) rest on the five stage passes above (the single pass of difference at stage 6 is not explained: the log's start position is not known, and the placement draws it); (b) the earlier single-run counts of N1 (F2 112, F3 110, F4 to F6 99) are not reproduced by the port at the seeds tried (F3's 110 is within one pass of the port's 109, F2's 112 within one of 113, F4's and F6's 99 and F5's 99 are not reached at the seeds tried), the starts of those runs are not recorded; one capture per mission with the camera longs and the player's start position every pass would settle both.**

**N11 and C6: a real crash in a mission restarts with the code 4 kept, the allowance one less and the F1 counter cleared (milestone 7, Task 2, settled)** [V: `flow7-crash-f1.txt`, F1 from its first flight pass (`mb/m3-flight.vasnap`), `V:4A927` poked to 10 and the player's height poked to 0x80000 at a standstill, so that the aircraft falls and strikes the runway (the crash, its hold and the restart are the game's; the poke only stages the fall), every 4 blanks for 1,040 blanks, 1 run]. The aircraft struck the ground at blank 8,796 (the crashed bit in the player's first word, the end code `V:4A850` = 4 in the same sample, the allowance 3, `V:4A927` = 10); the crash-hold state `0x138D8` follows at blank 8,804; between blank 8,836 and 8,896 the restart's state `0x13980` ran the player-start routine: **the allowance read 2 and `V:4A927` read 0 in the first sample after it** (10 to 0), the state `0x139C2` followed at blank 8,908 and the flight state at blank 8,972, 176 blanks after the crash; **the end code stayed 4** to the end of the log (the scripts are dead until an ESC restart, 12.11), the designation `V:4AA78` stayed 0xFFFF and the view the cockpit. So **the restart clears the F1 counter** (the player start `0x1D8B8` clears it, as 12.5 and note 07 12.3 read), keeps the code 4 and counts the allowance down, in a mission as in free flight (note 05 17.17). The port does all three (`restart_flight` clears the radar's counter; `tests/test_crash.c`, the test of the restart, pins the counter, the ECM switch and the allowance); N11 and C6 need no code change.

## 13. The briefing and outcome chain of modes 3 to 8 (Part C)

Scope: the states between the dispatch state `0x1201E` (6.1, which stores `0x12534` for the modes 3 to 8) and the first flight pass: the intro block and the briefing, the camera approach to the first hostile, the orders screen, the vector notice and the take-off or launch notice; and the outcome screens that follow a script's request (5.13 owns the engine timing; this section adds which text each mission shows and what patches it). Tags as in the header; the per-blank counts come from the emulator captures of 13.5 (blanks converted to port steps by the note's rule `(blanks x 8 + 25) / 50`, except where the states run the world, 3.8 exception 4).

### 13.1 The state chain

| state | entered from | what it does | leaves when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `0x12534` | the dispatch (4 passes of countdown) | `V:4A965` = 1 (keys restricted); on the countdown below 0: gate `V:4A84D` = 0, fade target `V:4A959` = 15, fade level `V:4A958` = 0 (fade in from black) | the same pass |
| `0x12568` | `0x12534` | waits for the fade to reach its target; then gate = 1, display-list request `V:4A910` = 0xFF, `V:4A865` = 1, direct-draw `V:4A90F` = 1, countdown 9 | level = target |
| `0x125A8` | `0x12568` | accumulates the key latch into `V:4A84E`; **a key** (latch `V:4A85B` non-zero) when `V:4A8E3` is 0 (a first start, not an ESC restart) skips the intro: text reset, countdown -1, to `0x12744`; otherwise on the countdown below 0: `V:4A90F` = 0, countdown 1 | the countdown or a key [V: `mission-prompts.txt`: SPACE sent at `0x125A8` skipped the intro and the briefing; the countercode prompt state `0x128AA` was reached 212 frames later, F4 to F6] |
| `0x12612` | `0x125A8` | pause byte `V:4A866` = 0; the same key skip; on the countdown below 0: gate 0; for mode 3 to 8 the id list becomes **[0x5B (the intro block), the briefing]**, where the briefing is index 0 of the mission's list (13.3) when the completion byte `V:1D024`+0x12+mode is zero and index 1 otherwise (`0x29468(mode, 1, 0)` or `(mode, 1, 1)`); the date line of 0x5B was patched by `0x292F0` at the placement (10.9) | text starts |
| `0x126F6` | `0x12612` | waits for the text (engine byte `V:4A898` negative) **or a key**; then key latch accumulated, gate 1, text reset, countdown -1, `V:4A8EE` = 3 | text finished or any key |
| `0x12744` | `0x126F6` or the key skip | on the countdown below 0: `V:4A865` = 1, `V:4A90F` = 1, countdown 1, **`0x2DA7C`** (the approach target, 13.4) | the same pass |
| `0x12776` | `0x12744` | on the countdown below 0: camera mode `V:4A966` = 2, `V:4A86D` = 1 (the mover runs, 6.4) | the same pass |
| `0x1279C` | `0x12776` | waits for the camera mode to become 3 (the mover sets it on arrival, 13.4); then `V:4A86D` = 0, `V:4A90F` = 0, countdown 2 | camera mode 3 |
| `0x127C8` | `0x1279C` | on the countdown below 0: gate 0; if `V:4A8E3` is set (a restart): `V:4A964` = 1, `V:4A965` = 2, countdown 3, to `0x128DA`; otherwise the countercode screen (the port skips it, 5.5) | |
| `0x128DA` | `0x127C8` / the countercode | on the countdown below 0, with `V:4A964` > 0: next state `0x12A50`; for the modes 3 to 8 the id list becomes **[0x5D (the clearance line), the orders: index 3 of the mission's list]** (`0x29468(mode, 1, 3)`); **the missions-started counter `+0x36` of the log record is incremented and the dirty flag set** (`0x1294C`, 14.2) | the same pass |
| `0x12A50` | `0x128DA` | waits for the text (engine byte negative); KP-ENTER (latch 2) runs the shortcut `0x12CFE` (a flight at once with the defaults: practically unreachable, 6.4); on the text finished: gate 1, text reset, direct-draw 1, countdown 3, to `0x12A92` | text finished |
| `0x12A92` | `0x12A50` | on the countdown below 0: `V:4A86D` = 1, to `0x12AC0` | |
| `0x12AC0` | `0x12A92` | waits for camera mode 4 (the mover reaching the map target, 13.4); then `V:4A86D` = 0, direct draw 0, countdown 2, to `0x12AFC` | camera mode 4 |
| `0x12AFC` | `0x12AC0` | on the countdown below 0: gate 0, `V:4A86D` = 1 (modes above 1), `V:4A965` = 0, text flag reset; only mode 1 types the location prompt (id 7, camera mode 5); to `0x12B74` | the same pass |
| `0x12B74` | `0x12AFC` | on `V:4A86D`: clears it, text reset, `V:4A865` = 0, gate 1, countdown 2; **at the carrier start (`V:4A900` = 3: F2 to F6 and the qualification) straight to the player start `0x12C6E`; at an airfield start (F1, `V:4A900` = 0) `V:4A849` = 1, camera mode 6, to `0x12C02`** | |
| `0x12C02` / `0x12C36` | `0x12B74` (F1 only) | the aircraft prompt (id 0x4B, 5.7) waits for the key 1 or 2 (`V:4A849`); then the player start | the aircraft key |
| `0x12C6E` | | the player start (6.1), camera mode 6, music, `0x12D58` (the approach, 6.4), then `0x12DB8` (13.2) and the wait of 6.2 | |

[R from the state routines; the order and the per-state blank counts: 13.5, V.] The id lists are written by `0x29468(mode, slot, index)` (2.1) in `V:4A802`; the text engine plays [0x5B, briefing] and later [0x5D, orders]; 0x5B's date is patched at the placement; no countercode prompt appears in the port.

### 13.2 What the take-off notice adds (6.2)

State `0x12DB8`, for the modes 3 to 8, calls the vector lookup `0x29534`: it walks the placement sub-list (`V:4A7F2`) and takes the **first entry whose record is valid, of the aircraft class nibble and not the cruise missile (class 0x15)** [V rig: `debrief_check.py`, 300 sub-lists, found / not found]. For that record it asks the offset routine `0x1D478` for the point 500 units (0x1F4) ahead of it in its own frame, subtracts the player's x and z (shifted right by 8) and calls the aim routine `0x1454A` of note 03 with the current pitch 0, the current heading 0, dx, dy = 0, dz and the limit -1: a negative limit **snaps** to the target, so the result `V:4AB7A` is the plain arctangent heading of the vector (the 0.1 degree table, not turn-limited), in the 28800 unit. The heading word is divided by 80 (`V:4ABD6`, 0 to 359, always below 360 since the word is below 28,800), converted to decimal digits by the subtraction loop `0x29ECC` and packed as BCD by `0x295BE`, which writes the three digits into the vector-notice record 0x58 (text indices 13 to 15, 2.4; the wrap of a value of 360 or more to 000 in `0x295BE` is never reached). The lookup returns -1 when no entry qualifies, and then the notice 0x58 is not posted. The digits are R only (the heading arithmetic is the aim routine's, verified in note 03; the digit packing was not run). The notice order is: 0x58 (when found), then 0x5A (carrier launch, bit 3 of the player's `B4`) or 0x59 (take-off). **Which missions have a vector notice:** every mode whose sub-list has a qualifying record at that moment: F1 (the first MiG, or the 707 of variant 1 at progress 0), F2 (Air Force One, class 0x14: the carrier is class 0x20 and skipped, slot 4 is the first aircraft entry), F3 (the first F-16), F4 (the first MiG; the pod is class 0x32), F5 (the missile of slot 4 is skipped; the MiG of slot 8), F6 (the MiG of slot 8; the submarine is class 0x21). F1 at the airfield start posted both 0x58 and 0x59 in the capture (13.5).

### 13.3 The outcome texts per mission

| mode | success (code 0xFF, then the flight resumes) | failure | patch |
| ---: | --- | --- | --- |
| 3 | 0x1E (confirmed signature), then 0x8055 (return to base) | none | -- |
| 4 | 0x23 (landed safely), then 0x8056 (return to the carrier) | 0xFE: 0x24 (bit 0 of `V:4A992` set: the passenger was aboard) or 0x25, then the quirk-cut 0x8056 and 0x8053 (5.13: never played) and the flight resumes; 0xFD: 0x4C and the menu | -- |
| 5 | 0x2B (banner), 0x8056 | 0xFE: 0x2C and the menu | -- |
| 6 | 0x32 (pod close enough), 0x8056 | 0xFE: 0x33 (cut list as above) | -- |
| 7 | 0x38 (banner), 0x8056 | 0xFE: 0x39 then 0x803A (parity bit set, menu) or 0x805C (clear, flight resumes, the cut list again) | -- |
| 8 | 0x3F (banner), 0x8056 | none | -- |
| all, on 0xFC | 0x54 (log updated, congratulations), 0x8053 (the hint line) | -- | -- |

Whether a 0xFF flight "resumes" until the landing: yes, code 1, `V:4A965` = 0, `V:4A866` = 0; the player flies back and lands, and the landing test (12.2) ends the mission with 0xFC. The patches of 0x5B (date) and 0x58 (bearing) are in 2.4; no other outcome record is patched.

### 13.4 The camera approach of modes 2 and 3 (mover stages 7 and 8)

`0x2DA7C` (called by `0x12744`) sets the target-hostile flag `V:4A967` = 1, the chosen-hostile word `V:4AA72` = 0 and the mover stage `V:4A86E` = 7, then walks the placement sub-list in order: for each entry whose record is of the aircraft class nibble and valid, `V:4AA72` becomes its record offset, and **the walk stops at the first one that is counted** ("not counted" flag clear); a not-counted aircraft only serves as a fallback (the last one found stands). With a record found: `V:4A967` = 0xFF and the camera target is that record's x and z with a height of three quarters of the current camera height; with none found: the sound/message request 0x3E and the fixed map target (the camera position constants `0x10C00000`, `0x05000000`, `0x11400000`) and the target is reached at once. The mover's stage 7 (note 07 section 6 has the mover and `camera_check.py`): with `V:4A967` positive, the stage ends at once in camera mode 3; otherwise the camera flies toward the hostile with the damping shift 14 while the distance exceeds 0x800000 and 12 while it exceeds 0x100000 (the second test is waived when the camera trail's coordinate `V:4AD06` is at least -0x100000), and on arrival sets camera mode 3 and stage 8 with the map target; **stage 8** flies to the map position with the same two shifts and sets camera mode 4 on arrival. So the briefing shows the first counted hostile and then pulls back to the map overview, whose arrival releases `0x12AC0` (13.1). Camera modes: 2 on `0x12780`, 3 at the end of stage 7, 4 at the end of stage 8.

**The visibility test that ends stage 7.** The draw code of hunk 30 (`0x30060`-`0x300AC`) projects the chosen hostile (`V:4AA72`) every frame; when `V:4A967` is negative (the approach is still on) and the projected screen point lies inside x 20 to 300, y 14 to 186 it sets `V:4A967` = 1, and the mover's stage 7 treats a positive `V:4A967` as arrival (camera mode 3, stage 8). In every captured mission start the hostile is inside that window from the first frame: `V:4A967` is 0xFF in the pass after the target choice and 1 in the next, so **stage 7 lasts two passes** (step counter 18 to 20; F1 and F2 [V: emulator, `mission-brief-m3-a.txt`, `m4-a.txt`, 1 run each; rig `approach_check.py`: 2 passes for all 32 combinations once the test is applied after the first pass]). The camera hardly moves in them. Stage 8 (back to the map overview) runs only while `V:4A86D` is 1 (set again at the end of `0x12A92`, 13.1); the rig, which has no draw, gives 7 further passes (10 for F3) after stage 7, while the F1 capture shows camera mode 4 already at the first pass of `0x12AC0` (the step counter 28 at that pass; four passes of `0x12A92` before it). **The difference is settled (C1, below): in the original the camera never leaves the map overview during a mission briefing.** The rig script **forces** the first of these (it pokes `V:4A967` = 1 after the first mover pass, as the draw would); the window test itself (x 20 to 300, y 14 to 186) is read from the code and its effect observed in the six emulator runs (F1 to F6, sub-lists 0 and 1, variant 0) **and, for C12, in four more runs of `followup-visibility.txt` with the completion byte poked to 2 and 3: F2 sub-lists 2 and 3, F4 sub-lists 2 and 3** [V, 1 run each; the sub-list pointer `V:4A7F2` differs by 42 bytes between sub-lists 2 and 3 and between F2 and F4]: in all four the flag `V:4A967` reads 0xFF in the pass after the target choice and 1 in the next, camera mode 3 follows in the third pass, the camera sits at the overview and the chosen record is the MiG (record 8; with F2 the airliner of record 4 is chosen first and replaced in the same walk). **The visibility rule holds on sub-lists 2 and 3; variants 1 and 2 of F1 are table placements and were not run.**

**The camera rule (C1, settled)** [V: `followup-brief-camera-s7.txt` (F2 from the menu key, every blank from the approach-target state `0x12744`, 1 run) and `followup-brief-camera.txt` (from the countercode prompt: the step counter, camera, camera mode, `V:4A86D`, `V:4A86E`, `V:4A967` and the target every 2 blanks through the orders to `0x12AC0` and beyond, 1 run)]: the camera `V:4ACF6` to `V:4ACFE` holds the map overview position (x `0x10C00000`, height `0x05000000`, z `0x11400000`, the constants of the no-hostile case above) **from the first pass of the target state to the first pass of `0x12AC0` and past it, unchanged to the unit**. The target `V:4AD0E` is the hostile's position (record 8: x `0x13280000`, height `0x03C00000`, z `0x12780000`) in the two passes of the target choice and the visibility flag. The mover first runs in the pass that stores camera mode 2 and `V:4A86D` = 1: it sees `V:4A967` positive, ends stage 7 at once, sets camera mode 3 and stage 8 and loads the map target, **which equals the camera**, so the same pass's damped step has a zero difference and nothing moves. Stage 7 never flies. `V:4A86D` is 0 from the next pass (set by `0x1279C`) until `0x12A92` sets it again; in the first pass of `0x12AC0` the mover runs once, finds the camera at the target and sets camera mode 4. The rig's extra passes come from its missing draw: without the flag its stage 7 really flies to the hostile and stage 8 flies back. **Port rule (C): a mission briefing has no camera path; camera mode 3 follows the target choice after 2 steps and mode 4 is seen at the first pass of `0x12AC0`, as the rule below.** The port rule (C) is the observed one: the camera approach is **2 steps** of stage 7, then the orders, then the return to the map is complete when `0x12AC0` first looks (5 steps after the orders text ends: 4 for `0x12A92` and 1 for `0x12AC0`).

Steps by `(blanks x 8 + 25) / 50`, except the states that run the world (the step column of those rows is the pass count). ## 14. Scoring and the log counters (Part C)

### 14.1 There is no score

The original keeps no score, no points, no rank and no per-mission grade [R: a scan of the stores in the scripts, the placement, the dying path and the debrief finds no accumulator that is displayed or saved other than the counters below; 9.5 reaches the same result for the qualification]. What the game counts is the following.

| counter | where | meaning and rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hostiles placed `V:4A961` | RAM, cleared by the placement | +1 per placed entry of the aircraft class nibble whose "not counted" flag is clear (the cruise missile included); +1 with the cap when a neutral aircraft is turned hostile (12.3) |
| the cap `V:4A962` | RAM, set by the placement | the row's count word: 2 for modes 1 to 7 and 9, 4 for mode 8, 6 for the demo row |
| kills `V:4A963` | RAM, cleared by the placement | +1 when a counted aircraft (class nibble 0x10, not 0x15) finishes dying in the air (note 07 section 5.5); read by the scripts of F3 and F6 only |
| the shadowing timer `V:4A83A` | RAM | F3 only (12.7) |
| the identification counter `V:4A927` | RAM, cleared at the player start | F1 only (12.5) |
| the progress counter `V:4A95F` | RAM and the log's `+0x02` | 0 to 3; see 14.3 |
| mission end code / request / delay | RAM | 12.2 |

### 14.2 What the log records for a mission

All the fields are in 10.2; the mission slice's contribution:

| field | rule | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `+0x36` missions started | +1 in the state `0x128DA` (the verdict, 13.1) at **every** pass of the mission start that reaches it with the security flag set: a first start from the menu (after the countercode, which the port skips) and every ESC restart (the restart sets the flag and the state runs again); a crash restart does not run it; the qualification, free flight and training have their own paths and do not count | R for the restart case; V: F1, the counter 4 -> 5 at a first start (13.5) |
| `+0x38` missions completed | +1 at the debrief of code 0xFC (`0x134F2`); not at the 0xFF success, not for the qualification | V: rig `debrief_check.py` (150 cases); the 0xFC captures of F1, F5 and F6 |
| `+0x02` level | the debrief stores the live level **before** its own increment (`0x1D168`), so the saved value is one completion behind the live counter `V:4A95F` | V: rig; the 0xFC captures of F1, F5 and F6 (12.12) |
| `+0x06`, `+0x07` | the mode of the completed mission and the completion byte before its increment | V: rig; the 0xFC captures of F1, F5 and F6 (12.12) |
| `+0x12 + mode` | the completion count of the mission, +1 per 0xFC, saturating at 3 | V: rig; the 0xFC captures of F1, F5 and F6 (12.12) |
| the dirty flag | the 0xFC debrief calls the writer `0x1858A` (the save, 10.8) right after the bookkeeping; the verdict's increment only marks the buffer | R |
| weapon and crash counters | by the combat code (note 07) and the crash paths; the scripts add none | R |

**Nothing happens at 0xFF or at a failure to the log** except what the combat code counts, and **the flight time** (10.7) is booked at the end of the flight as for any flight.

### 14.3 Unlocking, the next mission, the progress counter

- **The list** (menu item 6, 5.4) shows F1 always and F(m-2) when the completion byte of the mode m - 1 is non-zero: completing mission F(n) (code 0xFC) sets its byte to at least 1 and so shows F(n+1). Completing a mission again raises its byte to 2 and 3 (they change the sub-list, 12.3), never lowers it.
- **Menu item 7** starts mode `+0x06 + 1` (forced to 3 outside 3 to 8): the mission after the last one completed (5.4).
- **The progress counter** `V:4A95F` is incremented by **every** 0xFC (not only the first completion of a mission), capped at 3, and written to `V:4A960` (the reload value of the reset block, so an ESC restart keeps it); the log's `+0x02` lags one completion behind; `0x1D150` loads the counter from `+0x02` at start-up, **before the log has been read from the disk**, so the program starts every run with progress 0 whatever the saved level (V: the counter reads 0 in every captured session although the shipped record holds 2); only the 0xFC bookkeeping raises it, and the log's level is in effect write-only. It does three things: the placement threshold of the entries (12.3: from 1 the second fighter of most lists, from 2 the third), the shadowing distance of F3 (12.7: 0x30000, 0x24000, 0x18000 for 0 or 1, 2, 3 or more) and the AI's skill table (note 07). The first word of the log (qualified) gates the F-keys and item 7 (5.10); the qualification does not touch the counters.
- **The date** in the intro block (10.9) is the hours of flight time, not the mission number.

### 14.4 Port rules (C)

1. `missions_start(g, mode)`: modes 3 to 8 clear `V:4A83A`, `V:4A927`, the counts and run the placement; the mission's variables live in the core state and survive a crash restart exactly as 12.11 lists.
2. `missions_step(g)`: once per world step, after the player step and the entity loop of the same step, in frozen steps too; the region walk every 16th step; the script table of section 12; the end code once.
3. The debrief lists of 5.13 and 13.3 with the quirk-cut failure list; the log fields of 14.2 incremented exactly at the points above; one save at the 0xFC debrief.
4. No score is shown or kept.

## Constants

### Part A (sections 1 to 6)

Constants of sections 1 to 6. Durations measured in blanks are on the reference emulator (one run unless the section says more); the steps are `(blanks * 8 + 25) / 50`.

**Text engine, records, font (sections 1 to 3)**

| name | value | unit | source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| text ids, valid ids | 110, 106 | ids | id table `0x43DFE` |

## Open questions

### Part A (sections 1 to 6)

1. **Passes per blank on screens not measured.** Section 3.8 and 5.1 measure the credits, log-disk, menu, prompt and game-over screens; the briefing and debrief screens' rates and the ESC echo are measured only through their durations. The cost model of 3.8 is a fit (error up to 14 % low on the checks); evidence for a better one: `V:4A879` against `V:4A82C` per screen together with the engine's per-pass click and glyph counts.
2. **Ids reached only through computed paths.** Section 2.4's "written by" column comes from a scan for immediate stores and the two tables; ids 0x05, 0x08, 0x0B-0x0D, 0x10, 0x11, 0x14, 0x16, 0x52 and the fillers have no reference (section 11.6 lists the unposted ones). A computed id was looked for only in the debrief state `0x131F4`. Evidence: a rig run that logs every store into `V:4A802` over a full session.
3. **Bit 14 of an id** (delay 2 for the first line) has no user in the data; read only.
4. **The key click's sound** (3.6) is requested through `0x1A042`; whether it is audible and its content are note 10's.
5. **A line longer than 48 characters, an odd column or a row above 199** is never produced by the data; the engine does not clip (section 3.4 rule 6) and the port clips.
6. **The text pointer after a backspace** (3.7 quirk): not reproduced by the port; whether a real countercode entry reaches it was not tried.
7. **Two events of every key burst are missing in the queue test** (5.8); the cause is unknown and the real keyboard device is untested.
8. **The numbers 1 to 4 on the location map** are drawn by the mark pass `0x2FAB6`, not by text records (settled, note 04 "The start marks of the overview map": the pixels equal the port's fitted table).
9. **Location keys 5 to 0**: settled in milestone 7 for the approach (227, 223, 200 and 192 passes for keys 7, 8, 9 and 0, 5.6); the positions are in the table of 5.6; the headings of keys 7 to 0 are read from the table, not measured (R).
10. **Player-record availability bytes** (menu item 7 and F1 to F6 need a non-zero first word and per-mission bytes): section 10 owns the record; the menu keys 7 and F1 to F6 were observed only with an empty log.
11. **SHIFT then 2** sets `V:4A84A` = 2 (an erase request for the log screen), which the menu reset wipes; no real sequence is known to use it (5.4).
12. **Log-disk results 1 and 2** were produced by patching the check call, not naturally (5.3).
13. **Boot protection-failure state `0x13534`**: what the three checked words are (5.2; section 11 has the screen).
14. **The 33-blank menu build**: how much is the delay loop and how much the music start (5.4).
15. **The KP-ENTER skip window** (`V:4A965` = 2) is about one blank in free flight and matters in the mission briefings (5.5, 6.4).
16. **`V:4AC13` bit 7** selects the 210-pass or 150-pass echo and is set on the menu path (5.11); what sets it is not traced.
17. **Mission-script triggers** of the `V:4A850` codes and of request 2 (`0x27C58`): closed by section 12 (Part C); what remains open is in Part C's list.
18. **Why mode 0x7E is never written** (5.10), the meaning of `V:4A8EE` (a panel redraw counter, 6.1), why the qualification result keeps `V:4A965` = 1 (5.13), the odd-counter variant of the F5 failure (0x803A) not captured (5.13), the exact blank at which the game-over planes clear (k 6 to 30, 5.12), bits 8 to 14 of the placement entries' second word (read only, 6.1).

### Part B (sections 7 to 11)

Open questions of sections 7 to 11 (numbered within each section).

**Demo replay (section 7)**

1. **Why three more takes follow the first marker** (frames 2050 to 3675, 16 with events): the reader cannot reach them; they look like further recording sessions appended with the pause key. No evidence for any other use (the only readers of the buffer are the reader and the unreferenced stores).
2. **The checkpoints for Task 6** exist only for one run from `demo.vasnap` (stream step 47); a replay from step 0 in the emulator (the menu start) was not logged to the end; the two runs share the flight (deterministic stream) but the first 47 steps are not compared (they are the port's own run-up).
3. **Bit 7 of `V:4AC13`** (the briefing line wait 210 or 150 passes): read as the audio device flag (set at `0x19996`); the 150 case was not run (note 10).
4. **The pass rate of text-only states** varies between runs (3.2 and 9.6 passes per blank for the same two notice lines, 7.5); the cause (sound DMA load or the emulator's cycle accounting) is not decoded; the port uses pass counts (section 3.8).
5. **A divergent port flight** in the demo: what the original would do is the crash flow with the allowance 3 (7.6); the port's test is whether the replay reaches step 2045 without a crash (7.8).
6. **Right and joystick buttons** are not replayed (the stream has no bit for them): in the original the demo ignores the mouse and the joystick keys that act through the arrow bodies (they set only the key byte, overwritten each step).

**Training (section 8)**

1. **Instructor flight in the port.** Closed (8.3): which of note 05's per-entity branches apply to record 4, the call order, the state byte's role in the step, the five differences of the non-player step (pitch-rate half-decay [V rig], the first pass without motion [V rig], the wing-levelling conversion of states 0x0A and 6 [V rig], the ground vertical velocity and the lift-off bookkeeping [R]), and a rig reproduction of the original's instructor (44 of 44 emulator samples, 143 identical steps of the player-index replay and the divergence at step 144 explained). Note 05's flight-model section does not yet list these differences (a note 05 follow-up, not a Task 7 blocker).
2. **The camera flip of part 1.** The external camera direction flips 3 to 7 and back in lessons 3, 4, 5 and at the wrap (V: `flow-training-part1`, samples 36, 47, 69, 78, 85, 98, 138, 153). The routine (`0x2D7F2`: the heading word jumping by 90 to 270 degrees between frames toggles the direction by 4 when it is neither 0, 4 nor 8 or more) belongs to the camera slice (note 06 / 04); not reproduced by this section.
3. **Training and the flight log.** Whether a training flight changes the log record (crash and mission counters): the crash statistic `0x12F28` runs in training (`V:4A844` = 1); the other counters were not traced for modes 2 and 0x7D (section 10).
4. **The end of part 2 by the instructor's loss.** Settled (8.9): no end; a blank-record outcome, a 14-blank freeze, the flight resumes. Not captured with a lead that is really destroyed (the staged form restores the bit).
5. **Closed by this section.** Note 05 open question 22 (the effect of `W2` bit 0x0800), note 06 open question 1 (the HUD offsets are not set by training), open question 2's training part (the writers of mode 2, the posting site of entry 53 (`0x27492`) and the entries 29, 30, 44 and 45 that no code reaches), note 05 open question 5's training switches (none: all of `V:4A83C` to `V:4A847` stay 1).

**Qualification and flight log (sections 9 and 10)**

Qualification and flight log:
1. The record's bytes `+0x0C..0x0F`, `+0x1B..0x1D` and `+0x4A..0x4D` have no accessor in the code; a write trace of a full session would show a user of them through a pointer copy (none expected).
2. The increments of `+0x3C`, `+0x40`, `+0x44`, `+0x46`, `+0x48` and `+0x36`, `+0x38` (the mission start and completion counters) are read from the code; their captures need a flown mission (milestone 5, note 07).
3. What the typed letters at index 13 and beyond corrupt in the neighbouring game variables (up to `V:4A8FD`) and in the memory after the 78-byte buffer is not traced; the port does not copy it (10.5, deviation 1). Evidence needed: a watch on the variables during a 30-character name.
4. After a save the key handler drops the next key event (`V:4A88D`): read only. A capture would press a key within the save's duration (the save takes the disk about 1 s).
5. The port counts flight time in steps / 8 where the original counts wall-clock seconds (a step is 5 to 11 per second in the original, note 05 section 14): the total of a long session differs from the original's by the step-rate ratio, a deliberate consequence of the ruling.
6. The mode 9 start before the briefing: states `0x1234C`, `0x12378`, `0x127C8` and the countercode screen belong to 5.5; the transition into `0x11CC0` for mode 9 is read at `0x129CE`.

**Gap for the cockpit (milestone 3), found while tracing section 8 and 11**

1. **The touchdown, lift-off and hit jolt is not in the port.** The routine `0x29A5E` moves the 3D window and the HUD rows by one scanline up and down over four frames at a touchdown, a lift-off and a hit (offsets in `V:4A990`, `V:4A9D0`, `V:4AA3C`; note 06 open question 1 has the tables and the setting sites). It is read from the code (R), not captured with a frame; the cockpit renderer of milestone 3 ignores the offset words. Evidence needed: a staged touchdown with `V:4A990` logged per blank, then the renderer change (a milestone-3 follow-up, not part of the front-end slices).

**Hidden keys and unused content (section 11)**

1. **The right mouse button** (11.4): the afterburner toggle is read, not run (the reference driver has no right button; `ref.cpp` `click` presses the left button only). Evidence: add `rclick` (POTGOR bit 10) to the driver and watch `V:4A928` after a press at full throttle with the pointer throttle on.
2. **The joystick** (11.4): read only; the reference driver has no joystick command. Evidence: a driver command that sets the port-2 stick directions and a frame of the stick key bits.
3. **The original keys of the dead bodies** (11.6): the HUD-offset toggle, the look-at toggle, the pause-on body and the rearm path have no key in the shipped chain; a computed branch cannot be excluded by the reachability pass (R). Evidence: none needed for play (the sweep of 101 keys moved none of them).
4. **Computed text ids** (11.6): ids built at run time other than the mission lists were not looked for beyond the immediates and tables; a path that adds a base to an id would invalidate the unposted list. Evidence: a rig run of every state routine with the text list `V:4A802` logged.
5. **Menu-phase keys** (11.1): the S-phase and M-phase lists were read; the menu's own digit stage and F-keys are note 08 section 5's captures; P at the main menu (not the selection screens) was not captured.
<!-- partC-questions -->
### Part C (sections 12 to 14)

Numbering restarts here (C1 to C12); the closed item 17 of Part A is section 12.

- **C1. The return leg of the briefing camera (stage 8). Settled** (13.4, `followup-brief-camera.txt`, `-s7.txt`): the camera stays at the map overview position through the whole briefing, because the map target equals it; mode 4 comes at the first pass of `0x12AC0`. The rig's 7 to 10 passes were its missing draw flag.
- **C2. The F1 contact with the player flying. Settled (milestone 7, V)** (`flow7-c2-flying.txt`, 1 run: the staging of `followup-combat-kills.txt` as it stands, the airborne snapshot with a MiG-29 of the mode-3 placement in formation about 500 du ahead of the flying player, the lock `V:4AA78` = 0x1000, the counter and both records every 6 blanks for 780 blanks): the player climbed and flew on (W2 0x2000, its position changed by 1.4 million units in the log), the MiG held its place, and **`V:4A927` went 0, 0x1E at the first walk, then down by one per walk (0x1D, 0x1C, ..., 0x01) and from 0x01 to 0xFF, the 31st contact**, in 47 passes (the step counter 233 to 280, 0.66 contacts a pass with the doubled walks of the redraw passes, `V:4A8EF` 3 and 2); it stayed 0xFF to the end of the log. The rule of 12.5 holds with the player flying; a free chase (a pilot closing on the MiG) was not run, because a script flies open loop, but nothing in the rule reads the player's motion except through the positions.
- **C3. F2 success by the AI's own landing. UNOBSERVABLE at a cost this sandbox allows (milestone 7)** (`flow7-c3-landing.txt`; three staged attempts, logs `-v1` to `-v3`): Air Force One (record 4, class 0x14, placed 7 cells from its destination block (68, 61)) was poked to 3 cells from the block at 0.5 million and at 4.1 million units of height, to 1.2 cells at 0.6 million and to 0.1 cell at 0.26 million, the fighter's valid bit cleared. **It never lands at that block**: low it climbs towards its cruise height all the way (0.5 to 1.6 million units in 1,700 blanks from 3 cells; 0.6 to 2.7 million in 1,500 from 1.2 cells), at 0.1 cell it slows to 0x567 and climbs again, from the placement height (the fourth run, 3,840 blanks) it descends by 328 units a blank (a slope of 1 in 10) and at the block (blank 10,786, destination cell (68, 61)) **its destination words change to the next waypoint, (65, 66)**, at a height of 2.8 million units and the speed 0x165E. The block (68, 61) is a waypoint of its route, not the end of it; the landing is the end of a route of several waypoints, which the 12.6 script needs on the ground and slow (W2 0x0080, speed at most 0x320). The unstaged flight is about 8,500 blanks to the first block (3.4 thousand units a blank over 29 million) and an unknown number more to the last, roughly the 12,555 frames of F5's missile: **hours of exclusive emulator time at the rate of this sandbox** (about 0.1 s of wall clock a blank, one shared lock). The rule of 12.6 stays [R] with the rig of note 07 for the AI's own landing (the descent and the touchdown speed are the AI routines verified in `test-ai-traj`).
- **C4. The F6 victory condition. Settled for the question asked (milestone 7, V and R)** (`flow7-c4-sub.txt`, 1 run; `-v1` is the first, failed staging): the submarine (record 15, class 0x21, W0 0x0054, at cell (80, 76)) was poked 1,500 du ahead of the player (lifted 1,024 du above it, so that the missile clears the carrier's hull, which swallowed the first attempt at deck height), designated (`V:4AA78` = 0x1E00), and the player's Sidewinder fired by SPACE (the stock fell from 4 to 3). The missile (launched at blank 4,396) flew 220 blanks, 26 passes, in a dive onto the target and struck the water 43 du beyond the submarine's position (W0 0x84CA: down, dying); **`V:4A961` to `V:4A963` stayed 1, 4 and 0 and the submarine's record kept its words** (W0 0x4044 with the radar bookkeeping bits, W2 0x0001, B20 0x40) to the end of the log, 80 samples later. So **a missile that reaches the submarine counts nothing and does not mark it**: the submarine is not a counted kill, as the code (no store counts a class 0x21 record, 12.9) says; the briefing's "destroy the submarine" has no counterpart in the script, and F6 is won by four counted aircraft. (Whether the missile hit the hull or the sea behind it is not told by the record; either way no mark of 10.3 reached the submarine.)
- **C5. The order of the ejection seat's request 2 and a script's request 3 in one pass. Settled (milestone 7, V)** (`flow7-c5-probe.txt`, `flow7-c5-same-pass.txt` and `flow7-c5-early.txt`, 1 run each): in F5 the player record of the airborne snapshot was poked onto record 0 (a flying player at 36,864 units; an ejection from the deck crashed the aircraft before the seat landed) and SHIFT-E pressed. **The probe**: the seat (record 1, class 0x30, W0 0x10CA) appeared 58 blanks after the key, landed (W0 0x90CA, height 0) by blank 5,298, and **made the request 2 (`V:4A8E2` = 2, `V:4A8E4` = 4) in the pass of the step counter 168, blank 5,311**; six passes later (counter 174) the end code read 0xF0, the pause byte 1 and the state `0x1319C`, the bail-out ending of 5.13. **The collision**: the cruise missile (record 4) was made wrecked as `mission-m7-out-b.txt` does (the low byte of its W0 cleared, B20 = 2) at the first frame of counter 167 (`-same-pass`) and 166 (`-early`): the script took its frozen pass (pause byte 1) and **stored the code 0xFF, the request 3 and the countdown 4 in the pass in which the seat's request 2 was due (blank 5,310 against the probe's 5,311)**; the seat's request did not appear while the request 3 was pending: the byte read 3 with the countdown 4, 3, 2, 1, 0, -1 (counters 167 to 172), the debrief state `0x131F4` followed (blank 5,356, the success text) and the flight resumed with the code 1; **the seat's request 2 came after the resume** (blank 5,442, the byte 2 with the countdown 4 again), and the bail-out ending after it as in the probe. So in one pass **the script's request 3 wins and the seat's request 2 is made again as soon as the byte is free** (the landed seat asks every pass until it is served); both orders of the two stores in a pass give this result, because the script's store is guarded by "request not 3" only and the seat's by "no request pending". The port's rule (`entities.c`, the seat's request stored only while no request is pending) is this one; no code change.
- **C6. A crash restart in a mission. Settled (milestone 7, V)**: see N11 at the end of 12.14 (`flow7-crash-f1.txt`: F1, a real fall onto the runway; the code stays 4 through the restart, the allowance falls from 3 to 2, `V:4A927` is cleared, the flight resumes 176 blanks after the crash).
- **C7. F3 failure and shadowing. Settled (milestone 7, V, three runs with the AI flying)**: (1) **the failure by the thief's own flight** (`flow7-c7-fail.txt`, the first thief poked to x = 0x1BFD0000, 196,608 units short of the edge, and its destination cell x to 126, the AI flying it east at 2.4 thousand units a blank): in the sample in which its cell x first read 112 (x = 0x1C000614, blank 7,691) **the end code read 0xFE with the request 3**, the debrief state `0x131F4` followed at blank 7,731 and the code 0xF0 at blank 7,751 (the text 0x2C and the way back to the menu), and the menu state at blank 7,935: the rule of 12.7 step 2 (the cell x of at least 0x70) on a moving thief, one pass after it crosses. (2) **the shadowing timer with the AI running** (`flow7-c7-shadow.txt`: collisions off, `V:4A83A` poked to 3 and the second thief's destination cell x to 126, the player copied onto the first thief every 2 blanks, samples every 4): **the timer fell by one per world pass (3, 2, 1, 0, -1 in five passes, the step counter 74 to 77) and in the next pass (78) both thieves' destinations read the first waypoint of their routes again** (cell (66, 74), fine 0x2000, height 0x900; the second thief's from cell 126 back to 66), as 12.7 step 3 says; while the timer ran, the first thief's destination words were a point at its own position (cell 65, a fine x that moved every pass, height 0), the AI's reaction to the player at its side, which the rule does not mention. (3) **A far pass reloads 200** (`flow7-c7-shadow-v1`, the first attempt with a fixed height offset: the thief descended 0x30020 below the player's height, the near test failed once at sample 26 and the timer went from 146 to 200, then fell again): the reload of 12.7 step 3. In the first, long attempt the first thief descended to its route height 0x900 (9 du) and flew into the sea 560 blanks in (y = 0x2EE, W0 0x84D9), which ended that run; the rule needs no more.
- **C8. F5 success by a real shoot-down. UNOBSERVABLE open loop (milestone 7)** (`flow7-c8-shoot.txt` and three earlier logs): the cruise missile (record 4, class 0x15, 628 kt) was staged 1,500 du ahead of the player and the player's Sidewinder fired at it (the weapon selected by poking `E+0x63`, the stock, the lock `V:4AA78` = 0x800): (1) at deck height the missile ended after 16 passes at the same z in two runs (the carrier's hull test, R); (2) from 2,048 du above the target the missile dived but the target, 7,400 units a pass, passed beneath it and was never caught in 320 blanks; (3) the player lifted without speed fell and crashed in 36 blanks (code 4). A shot at a target that flies at 628 kt needs a pursuit geometry that a script of pokes does not give without piloting. The success branch itself is staged (12.12: the slot made wrecked gives 0xFF and the debrief) and the hit marks of a Sidewinder on a class 0x15 record are rig-verified (07 10.3, `weapon_check.py hits`, 3,000 pairs, the class 0x15 other included) and observed on aircraft (07, the staged Sidewinder kill); what no capture has is the chain from the shot to the script in one run.
- **C9. The date line** of the intro block uses the flight hours of the log (10.9); the table of month names at `0x292CC` is game text (36 bytes) for the extractor. Done in extractor version 16: `tables/months.bin`, each name un-reversed and in calendar order.
- **C10. The scan rate of 12.5. Settled** (`followup-radar-scan.txt`, 1 run, F1 with one staged MiG): the contacts follow the walk schedule of 12.5 and note 07 12.1, a walk on the passes with the step counter's bit 1 clear, plus a second walk in each pass with the scope redraw counter `V:4A8EF` of 2 or 3 (n = 81, 82, 113, 114 in the run, where the counter read 3, 2, 3, 2); 31 contacts took 49 passes (n = 68 to 116), 27 single walks and the 4 doubled passes, against the pure two-in-four reading's 62 passes; the "25 in 46" of the older run is the same schedule between two redraws.
- **C11. The key skip with KP-ENTER during the briefing. Settled** (`followup-brief-skip.txt`, 1 run, F2, 100 blanks into the orders text, `V:4A965` = 2): the key is accepted; within 4 blanks the state is `0x12DB8` (the countdown), **the approach `0x12D58` never runs** (camera mode 0, gate 1, `V:4A900` = 3 and `V:4A901` = 0x11 from the mission's own start), then the countdown of 6 passes (counter 24 to 30), the notice stage `0x12E4E` (92 blanks, the counter reset to 0 at its end), the wait of 6.2 (counter 0 to 4) and the flight state at counter 4. So the shortcut is reachable in the orders window, skipping the approach's 112 to 121 passes.
- **C12. The visibility rule on F1's variants 1 and 2. Settled** (13.4, `followup-visibility.txt` for the sub-lists 2 and 3, 4 runs; `flow7-c12-f1var.txt` for the placement variants, 2 runs): with the selector `V:4ABB0` poked to 2 and to 3 after every video frame from the key to the end of the dispatch, the placement stored the variant `V:4ABD4` = **1 and 2** (so the mapping of 6.1, the values 2 and 3 to the variants 1 and 2, is V), and in both runs the approach target was the record 10 (`V:4AA72` = 0x1400), the flag `V:4A967` read 0xFF in the pass after the target choice and 1 in the next (the step counter 18 to 19), the camera stayed at the map overview and the stage was 7: **the rule holds on variants 1 and 2 of F1**.
- **C13. The mover's calls per pass. Settled** (12.14 N1; the approach's HUD step, window row and view pass by pass for the airfield, the carrier and the two training parts, and the passes of the keys 7 to 0, milestone 7, 12.14 and 5.6): one call per pass; the rig's 1,190 calls came from its zero target; passes of `0x12D58` per free-flight start 118, 93, 93 and 104 (location keys 1 to 4). **Location keys 5 and 6** [V: `followup-combat-batch-g.txt` part `keys56`, one run each]: both start at airfield 1 (`V:4A900` reads 0 from the approach on: the start routine clears the out-of-table index), the step counter goes **35 to 153 (key 5) and 34 to 152 (key 6): 118 passes**, the same as key 1; the byte read 3 immediately after the key command, **which is the value from before the key was delivered, not a store**: the key handler stores the index itself (5.6, "Keys 5 and 6 on the aircraft screen"), `V:4A900` reads 4 (key 5) or 5 (key 6) from the pass in which the key is taken to the player start, and the clear at `0x1D730` is what the approach sees. Keys 7 to 0 were not run (errata of the fix round 2: the first reading took the 3 for the byte's value after the key).
